# Decisions log

Minor mechanical choices made under BUILD-PROMPT §8.2 — smallest choice consistent
with existing patterns. One line each: date · choice · reason. Anything bigger goes
through the must-ask protocol (§8.3) or a Gymnasium proposal.

---

- 2026-08-26 · `.nvmrc` pins `20` (installed toolchain is Node 20.16.0; docs require 18+) · matches the machine CI and dev actually run on.
- 2026-08-26 · Toolchain sub-dependencies of the §10 pre-approved set installed without a memo: `vite` + `@vitejs/plugin-react` (required by electron-vite for React), `typescript-eslint` + `eslint-config-prettier` (required for ESLint to parse TS / not fight Prettier), `@types/node|react|react-dom` (type-only) · treated as "implied by the docs" (BUILD-PROMPT §7), flagged in the session report for Architect review.
- 2026-08-26 · Import-boundary lint implemented with core `no-restricted-imports` per-directory overrides instead of an extra plugin · zero new dependencies, same enforcement.
- 2026-08-26 · `postinstall` electron-rebuild deferred until `node-pty` lands in M0.3 · nothing native to rebuild before that.
- 2026-08-26 · Pixel fonts (Press Start 2P, Pixelify Sans, IBM Plex Mono) not yet bundled; token files declare the stacks with monospace fallback · font bundling owed by M0.4 floor/visual package.
- 2026-08-26 · `vite` pinned `^7` and `@vitejs/plugin-react` `^5` · electron-vite@5 peers on vite ≤7; npm's default pick (vite 8) is ERESOLVE-incompatible.
- 2026-08-26 · `@eslint/js` added as explicit devDependency · ESLint 10 no longer exposes it transitively; required by the flat-config recommended preset.
- 2026-08-26 · `electron` pinned to `^37` · electron ≥38 requires Node ≥22.12 at install time; this machine + `.nvmrc` + CI run Node 20. Revisit when Node is upgraded (flagged in session report).
- 2026-08-26 · Vitest config lives in `vitest.config.mts` (ESM) · vitest 4 cannot load a CJS-interpreted `.ts` config in a non-`"type": "module"` package.
- 2026-08-26 · `@xterm/addon-fit` installed alongside pre-approved `@xterm/xterm` · standard first-party companion for sizing the terminal to its panel; flagged for Architect review with the other toolchain implieds.
- 2026-08-26 · `scripts/patch-node-pty.cjs` runs in postinstall before electron-rebuild · two Windows build defects in node-pty 1.1.0: (a) winpty.gyp calls helper `.bat`s by bare name, which current Win11 builds no longer resolve from the cwd — patched to `.\`-prefixed; (b) its gyps demand Spectre-mitigated MSVC libs this machine's VS lacks (MSB8040) — patched to `SpectreMitigation: false`. Idempotent, Windows-only, no-ops elsewhere.
- 2026-08-26 · `src/shared/ipc.ts` must stay free of runtime deps (zod) because the sandboxed preload imports it and sandboxed preloads cannot require external modules · channel names/helpers live there; zod schemas live in the sibling schema modules imported only by main.
- 2026-08-26 · Vitest suites do not import `node-pty` (validators only) · node_modules build is Electron-ABI after electron-rebuild and cannot load under the Node test runner; PTY behavior is covered by the M1 conformance/E2E rigs per TEST-STRATEGY.
- 2026-08-26 · `pixi.js/unsafe-eval` imported in the floor module · Pixi's default shader codegen needs eval, which the strict CSP (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §5) forbids; this official module swaps in eval-free paths — CSP stays strict.
- 2026-08-26 · `config:get` returns `{ config, warning }` (ConfigSnapshot) · an invalid `~/.ephesus/config.json` must surface visibly (BUILD-PROMPT §3.7); the file is never silently overwritten, the app runs on defaults with the warning shown in the status line.
- 2026-08-26 · `postinstall` now `electron-rebuild -f` (all native deps) · better-sqlite3 joined node-pty; rebuilding everything is simpler than maintaining a module whitelist.
- 2026-08-26 · `@types/better-sqlite3` added (type-only) · better-sqlite3 ships no types.
- 2026-08-26 · `EPH_HOME` env override for the harness home root · lets tests/E2E boot against a temp home (TEST-STRATEGY §4) without touching the real `~/.ephesus/`.
- 2026-08-26 · (M0 audit) `pty:ensure-dev-shell` is a temporary M0 channel · replaced by `agents.spawn` in M1; retire it when the claude adapter lands.
- 2026-08-26 · (M0 audit) `pty.kill` lives in the `pty:` group for M0 · SDD §5 homes `kill(id)` under `agents:`; folds into the agents group in M1 with the adapter lifecycle.
- 2026-08-26 · (M0 audit) `pty:exit:<id>` push event added beside `pty:data:<id>` · needed to make kill/exit outcomes visible; SDD §5 calls its event list abridged.
- 2026-08-26 · (M0 audit) Pixel-font bundling debt re-scoped: not delivered in M0.4 as originally logged · owed by the first M1 package that touches renderer chrome (M1.6 command bar) or earlier; awaiting Architect acknowledgment.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect directive) MemPalace adopted as the Library's recall index and company archive · ADR-0016; docs updated across README, SDD §1.1/§2/§12, IMPLEMENTATION M4, BUILD-PROMPT §10; lands in M4, optional dependency with the FTS/grep degrade ladder intact.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect directive) Floor art quality bar raised to Munder Difflin level · UI-DESIGN §7 rewritten (licensed tileset path + attribution rules + walk-cycle citizen bar); new package M1.5b in PROGRESS/BUILD-PROMPT; supersedes the "no licensed-asset dependency in v1" stance. Font debt folded into M1.5b (closes the pending acknowledgment above).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `EngineId`/`HookSupport` live in `src/shared/engines.ts`, the `EngineAdapter` interface in `src/main/engines/types.ts` · the renderer's agent card needs the id + fidelity grade (type-only import), but the adapter surface itself is main-only and must stay behind the boundary lint (NFR-12).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `SpawnPlan.settings` is a `SettingsInjection[]` fed by `wireHooks()`'s `HookPlan.injections` · ADR-0009 puts shim install in `wireHooks` while SDD §3 says `SpawnPlan` composes settings injection; making them one concept with one owner avoids two competing representations of the same files.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `AgentSpawnConfig` carries `hookEndpoint` (socket path / Windows pipe name) · the shim has to be told where to POST; the smallest honest representation, and it keeps M1.3's platform difference out of the adapters.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `ResumeSupport` is only `resumeArgs(sessionId)` · session-id *discovery* belongs to the event plane (hook payloads carry it, M1.3) — inventing a discovery method here would be adapter surface the ADR does not name.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `KeySequence` carries `label` beside `bytes` · anything the harness writes into an agent on the Architect's behalf must be inspectable (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §4 "no hidden side effects for agents").
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) `HOOK_SUPPORT_RANK` encodes ADR-0009's `native > wrapper > pty-heuristic` numerically · M1.7's hook-grade-honesty check compares declared vs demonstrated fidelity; one shared ordering beats a switch in every caller.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.1) Registry is an `EngineRegistry` class plus a process-wide `engines` instance (mirrors `PtyManager`) · tests build isolated registries instead of needing a test-only reset API, and a duplicate id throws rather than silently replacing an adapter (a replaced adapter would make the agent card's fidelity grade a lie).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) The fake engine and the hook client are dependency-free ESM `.mjs` with `@ts-check`, not `.ts` · both run under whatever bare `node` spawns them, with no build step and no guaranteed `node_modules`; `tsconfig.node.json` gained `allowJs`/`checkJs` and `shims/**` so `npm run typecheck` still covers them (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §1 type safety kept without a build).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) The hook *envelope* schema landed here in `src/shared/hooks.ts`, ahead of M1.3's hook server · the fake emits a schema'd wire format the moment it exists, and invariant §9 forbids shipping one without a versioned validator; M1.3 adds the per-event payload schemas and the drift path on top of it.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) Envelope `event` is a free string, with the known set in a separate enum plus `classifyHookEvent()` · FR-2.3 requires drift to be *accepted with a visible warning*, so an unknown event name has to survive parsing to be classified rather than being rejected at the schema.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) Hook event names are harness-normalized (`pre-tool`), not engine-native (`PreToolUse`) · mapping engine vocabulary is the adapter's job; otherwise `hooks.ts`, the avatar machine and the floor would learn a Claude-ism and NFR-12 would be violated at the first second engine.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) `HOOK_EVENTS` adds `session-start`/`session-end` to the SDD §6 socket-borne triggers · they bind a spawn to an engine session id, which is what resume (FR-1.4) and `TranscriptReader` (FR-11.2) key off. `notification` (the engine's native permission-prompt choke point, SDD §9) is deliberately left out until M3 wires it — the drift path reports it honestly meanwhile.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) The fake's script format is validated by hand inside the fake, not by a zod schema in `src/shared/` · it is a test-double format; production shared code should not carry test types. It still carries `schemaVersion` and refuses a drifted version loudly (exit 2), which is what invariant §9 is actually protecting.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) The fake parses stdin character by character — Enter submits the pending line, Escape clears it and interrupts · a PTY can hand over a typed prompt and the interrupt key in a single read, and per-chunk parsing silently swallowed one of them (caught by the M1.2 smoke test before it could mislead M1.6).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) The fake supports `onPrompt`/`onInterrupt` reactions beyond M1.2's brief · M1.7 conformance owes a spawn/interrupt/kill lifecycle green for the *fake* adapter, so the double has to answer an interrupt; building it now avoids re-opening the fake in M1.7.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.2) `read-inbox` takes `consume: true`, moving messages to `inbox/.done/` · `.done/` is SDD §2's own directory and M2's S-WAKE cursor-idempotency scripts need the fake to consume; the default stays non-destructive.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) The Windows pipe name is `\.\pipe\ephesus-events-<sha256(resolved home)[0:16]>`, not `<uid>` as the M1 plan sketched · Windows has no uid, and pipe names live in a machine-wide namespace, so the discriminator must separate harness homes rather than users — which is also what lets every `EPH_HOME` test bind its own endpoint in parallel. POSIX is unchanged: `<home>/events.sock`, chmod 0600 (SDD §2).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) On Windows the documented 0600 requirement is met by the local-namespace pipe (libuv rejects remote clients) plus the per-payload token, since a pipe carries no file mode · recorded because ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §5 states the rule in POSIX terms and this is the equivalent, asserted by its own platform-gated test.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) Per-event payload schemas are *loose* (`z.looseObject`), and only `pre-tool`/`post-tool` require a field (`tool`) · an engine adding a payload field has broken nothing; the station map (SDD §6) genuinely cannot route an avatar without a tool name, so that absence is the one payload drift worth a warning.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) Rejection reasons never contain the presented or expected token · they are credentials and BUILD-PROMPT §3.6 keeps secret-shaped strings out of logs; the reason names the agent instead, which is the ref a reader actually needs.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) The hook server takes `onEvent`/`onRejected` callbacks rather than writing anywhere itself · `log.jsonl` and the single committer arrive in M2, and this keeps the endpoint's sink swappable without touching the transport. Main wires both to console with refs meanwhile.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) Distinct drift warnings are retained in-server (`driftWarnings()`), deduped and in first-seen order · FR-2.3 needs them surfaced in the UI, and M1.5 is the first package where hook events reach the renderer; the obligation is recorded against M1.5 in PROGRESS so it cannot be lost.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.3) The endpoint binds in `app.whenReady()` before the window is created · a spawn must never race its own hooks, and there is no ordering subtlety to remember later if the socket is up before anything can spawn.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) `eph-hook.mjs` is engine-agnostic; the engine→harness event mapping and payload key renames arrive as arguments the adapter writes into the settings file (`--event pre-tool --field tool=tool_name --session-field session_id`) · one shim for every engine, and Claude-isms stay inside `engines/claude.ts` where NFR-12 wants them. Renamed keys are added, never substituted, so nothing the engine sent is lost.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) Claude Code's hook set maps 1:1 onto `HOOK_EVENTS`, `PostCompact` included · verified against the engine's documented hook list; no harness event is synthesised and none is dropped, which is what makes the declared `native` grade honest.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) The shim writes nothing to stdout, only stderr · engines treat hook stdout as instructions to the model; a chatty shim would inject text into the agent's context.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) Agent base env is an allowlist (`src/main/engines/spawn-env.ts`), not `process.env` · ADR-0010 gives agents credentials only through declared grants, and inheriting the harness's whole environment would defeat that on the first spawn. **Raised in the session report for Architect ratification** — the policy follows ADR-0010, but the exact list is a judgment call.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) `injectIdentity()` for claude validates and renders rather than writing anything · the engine takes identity on the command line (`--append-system-prompt`, an ADR-0009-sanctioned mechanism), so `spawnArgs` performs the injection; what `injectIdentity` owes is the precondition — missing `identity.md`/`PROTOCOL.md` throws at spawn instead of an agent quietly running with no idea who it is.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) The identity appendix template lives at `prompts/engines/identity-appendix.md` and is read through `PromptStore` (home copy first, bundled copy as seed) · invariant §8 — prompt text is config, and it only counts if the copy the Architect can edit is the copy actually read.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) `settings.local.json` is *merged*, not replaced, and the prior file is backed up byte-for-byte to `settings.local.json.eph-backup` · the Architect's own local settings and hooks keep working while an agent runs, and uninstall restores exactly what was there (ADR-0009 hygiene; the byte-for-byte requirement is why `writeFileAtomic` now accepts a Buffer).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) An existing `settings.local.json` that is not valid JSON makes `wireHooks()` throw instead of overwriting it · backing up something unparseable and writing our own over it would look like it worked; refusing to spawn until the file is fixed is the honest answer.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4) Packaging debt recorded: `prompts/` and `shims/` are resolved from the app path, so a packaged build must ship them as extra resources (a shim inside an asar cannot be executed by `node`) · owed by M7 packaging, harmless in dev and CI.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) `AgentManager` takes an `AgentSpawner` seam instead of importing `PtyManager` · node-pty is Electron-ABI after `electron-rebuild` and cannot load under vitest, so injecting the spawner is what makes the lifecycle rules (ordering, rollback, token lifetime) testable per-PR rather than E2E-only.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) `argv[0]` is resolved against the *plan's* PATH before `pty.spawn` (`src/main/which.ts`) · a conpty spawn on Windows does not walk PATH/PATHEXT, so a bare `claude` (an npm `.cmd`/`.exe` shim) died with ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND. Found by running the real app, not by reading code — the adapter keeps naming its binary logically and the platform detail stays in one place.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) The agent id is claimed synchronously before `spawn()`'s first await · two spawn calls in flight for one agent both passed the liveness check, and the second's hook token replaced the first's in the endpoint registry, leaving the running agent's hooks rejected with a token mismatch. Observed live; regression test named for it.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) `starting` added to `AGENT_LIFECYCLES` · the reservation window (probe + settings install) is real elapsed time during which the id is claimed and no process exists; showing `running` through it would be a lie on the agent card.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) A `spawn` may replace an agent whose lifecycle is `exited` or `missing-binary`, and is refused otherwise · that is the FR-1.4 respawn offer; refusing every duplicate would make a crashed agent unrecoverable without a restart.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) `agents.send(id, text)` writes Architect text into the agent's PTY verbatim · FR-1.3's queue-until-idle decision belongs in main and lands with the command bar (M1.6); this is the transport it will use.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.4b) The renderer receives agent-card changes on `state:agents` (SDD §5's push list) carrying one card, not the whole roster · card updates are frequent and per-agent; the renderer keeps a projection keyed by id.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) Tool *classification* is the adapter's table, applied generically by the shim (`--classify`/`--class`/`--class-prefix`) · SDD §6's station map is keyed by tool class, and turning `Read`/`Bash`/`mcp__*` into a class is engine knowledge — a table of Claude tool names inside the floor would be exactly the NFR-12 leak the ADR forbids. An unmatched tool gets no class and the avatar works at its desk.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) `arrive` is emitted by MAIN from the shared floor geometry, not by the renderer · SDD §6 has the edge but names no owner; giving it to the renderer would make the floor authoritative about when an agent reached a station, which ADR-0002 does not allow. `src/shared/floor.ts` holds the station tiles so both planes compute the same walk duration.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) `AvatarSnapshot` carries `origin` beside `station` · a walk is only renderable as (from, to, since); without the origin the renderer would have to remember where the avatar was, i.e. hold state the model does not have.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) `arrive` at the desk leaves the avatar `thinking`, not `working` · SDD §6's arrow reads `thinking ──arrive──► working`, but §2.4 defines `working` as "at a station, tool in use", and a desk is not a station a tool runs at. Resolving the ambiguity toward §2.4's definition; flagged in the session report.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) `stopped` and `archived` are modelled as terminal phases beside the ten §6 states · both words appear in the §6 diagram as endpoints but not in its state list; they are not poses (the avatar has left the floor) and terminal phases absorb every later event.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) `stop.pending` is always false until Hermes lands (M2) · the ADR-0013 autonomy branch asks "is mail or a task waiting", and today nothing can be waiting. That is the truth about this system, not a placeholder.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) The renderer remembers each sprite's last drawn pixel position and starts an interrupted walk from there · a real tool usually finishes before its walk does, so the next snapshot describes a walk from a station the avatar never reached; drawing that literally teleports the sprite forward and then walks it back. Presentation smoothing only — phase and destination still come entirely from main. Found by watching the live run.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5) **Architect text must be written to a PTY as two writes: the text, then the submit key.** Claude Code's TUI turns on bracketed-paste mode (`ESC[?2004h`) once booted, and a single write ending in CR is taken as a paste rather than a submitted line — the agent silently held the text and never ran. Diagnosed live (early sends worked, later ones did not); binding on the M1.6 command bar.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) Citizens are a pure sprite-recipe module (`floor/citizen.ts`) returning rectangles, not canvas calls · UI-DESIGN §7's bar (8 directions, 4+ frames each, ≤5 palette colours, distinct silhouettes) is then a unit test rather than an opinion, which is also what TEST-STRATEGY §4 asks for — assert the scene's state model, not pixels.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) Eight directions are authored as five views (front, back, side, front-side, back-side) with the four westward ones mirrored · standard pixel-art construction; each view stays hand-shaped instead of interpolated, and the diagonals are distinguished by a narrower three-quarter torso and a visible ear on the back-side view.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) Silhouettes are assigned by a stable hash of the role string, with `orchestrator` reserved · roles are free-form in the registry (SDD §4.1), so a fixed role→silhouette table would be inventing a role vocabulary the docs do not have. Same role always looks the same.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) The status badge is drawn outside the sprite's five-colour budget · it is a UI marker (UI-DESIGN §8 double-encoding), not part of the citizen; counting it would force a real colour out of the recipe.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) The licensed tileset is *discovered*, never imported: `import.meta.glob` over a gitignored drop directory · a static import of a file that may not exist (or may not be redistributable) would break the build and the licence at once. Empty drop ⇒ procedural tiles plus a visible `tileset: procedural (none installed)` note.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.5b) Pixel fonts are fetched and byte-checked at runtime rather than imported · a dev server answers a missing file with index.html, and handing that to the font parser produces a confusing "invalid sfntVersion" instead of the plain fact that the face is not installed. Missing faces are shown in the status strip (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) The queue-until-idle decision is a pure function in `src/shared/commands.ts`, *made* in `src/main/commands.ts`, and only *rendered* by the command bar · FR-1.3's rule is one decision in one place; a text box that decided for itself would be holding authoritative state (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §4), and the M1.6 risk note names exactly that.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) The queue flushes off the same avatar snapshots the floor draws · "idle" then means one thing to the Architect's eye and to the queue, instead of two clocks that can disagree.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) `success` counts as ready, not busy · it is a 250 ms flash on the way to `idle` (SDD §6) and the agent is already free; holding through it would add a visible stutter for nothing.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) Held text accumulates (newline-joined) rather than replacing · an Architect who types twice while an agent works meant to say both things; dropping the first would be silent data loss.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) Every held phase carries its own reason ("agent is mid-tool", "blocked at a gate", …) rather than a generic "busy" · the bar shows the reason, and "busy" tells the Architect nothing they could act on. Asserted by a test that rejects the generic string.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) Interrupt clears the queue · stopping an agent did not mean "and then say this anyway" (FR-1.3). Wired in the `agents:interrupt` handler so it holds however the interrupt is triggered.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.6) `SUBMIT_KEY_DELAY_MS = 150` between the text write and the CR · the two-write requirement came from a live diagnosis (bracketed paste swallows a trailing CR); 150 ms is above input-coalescing windows and imperceptible, and it is injectable so tests stay deterministic.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.7) The fake engine's adapter takes engine id `custom` · ADR-0009 reserves that id for exactly this, so the roster needs no change to give the conformance suite a second implementation. A suite with one implementation only proves that implementation compiles.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.7) The fake adapter deliberately uses a *different* mechanism for every part it can — identity through the environment rather than argv, its own settings filename, its own transcript format · the suite must test effects, not mechanisms (SDD §3), and only a second, differently-built adapter proves it does.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.7) "Hook grade honesty" is operationalised as: `native` requires every `HOOK_EVENTS` name demonstrated, `wrapper` at least one, `pty-heuristic` none; an adapter is honest when demonstrated ≥ declared · ADR-0009 names the grades but not a measurement, and a check without one would be vacuous. The suite includes a *negative* case — an adapter claiming `native` while reporting two of eight events is caught — so the check is proven to bite.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.7) The per-PR grade check for `claude` is the wiring, not a live spawn; the demonstrated-events check runs against the fake · TEST-STRATEGY §1.3 puts real-engine tests in the nightly suite, not per-PR. The claude adapter's live demonstration is recorded from this session's M1.4 run instead.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1.7) `claude` declares no `TranscriptReader` yet, and the suite records that as a declared capability gap rather than a failure · ADR-0009 marks `transcripts?` optional and says capability differences become visible product tiers; the reader needs real transcript fixtures and belongs with the cost ledger in M3.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 exit) `pty:ensure-dev-shell` and `pty:kill` retired, closing the two M0-audit deferrals · the claude adapter has landed, so the terminal panel attaches to the *selected agent's* PTY (UC-03 step 2) and killing goes through `agents:kill` where SDD §5 homes it. The M0 dev shell is gone with them.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 exit) `ptyIdSchema` widened to allow dots · a PTY id is now its agent's id (`AgentCard.ptyId`), and the old dash-only pattern rejected every `resize` for `agent.mason`. Caught by the exit review, not by a test — added to the pty validator suite.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 exit) Typing in the terminal panel bypasses the command queue, by design · FR-1.3 governs *prompts*; answering the engine's own permission dialog or cycling its modes is the Architect operating the engine's interface, and holding those keystrokes until idle would deadlock (the agent is mid-tool *because* it is asking).
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 exit) A newly spawned agent takes the selection only when nothing is selected · an agent appearing must not yank the Architect's attention off the one they are watching.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 exit) SDD §1.1 and §5 updated to name the M1 modules (`agents.ts`, `avatars.ts`, `commands.ts`, `prompts.ts`) and the `avatars`/`commands`/`hooks` IPC groups · IMPLEMENTATION §0.4: "a milestone isn't done if the SDD lies about the code". `fsx.ts`/`home.ts`/`index.ts` stay unlisted — the map is a subsystem map, not a file listing.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 audit fix) FR-1.6 install offer now spawns with `baseAgentEnv()` instead of `{}` · an empty environment cannot resolve `npm` from PATH nor start it (the exact Windows conpty failure class DECISIONS-LOG M1.4b records); no EPH_* vars — the installer is not an agent yet. Env assertion added to the FR-1.6 test.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 audit fix) `gate-verdict` restores the prior STATION, not just the phase · SDD §6 says "verdict → prior state"; the avatar now walks back from the watch post to where it was (`resumeStation` on the snapshot), with a named regression test. Previously it teleported to the desk in a phase/station combination no other path can produce.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 audit fix) `sk-`-prefixed fixture value renamed to a scanner-neutral string · BUILD-PROMPT §3.6 has no obviously-fake exception, and `sk-` is what secret scanners key on.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 audit) Floor status badge is color-only for `idle`↔`waiting` and `alert`↔`thinking` pairs · UI-DESIGN §8 deviation, low urgency — `waiting`/gates/breaker cannot fire before M2/M3; owed a glyph/label second encoding when M2.6's Activity tab or the §5 hover card lands.
- 2026-08-26 · (M1 audit) Claude adapter omits optional `resume` although Claude Code supports `--resume` · capability gap recorded like the M1.7 transcripts note; wire it with FR-1.4's respawn-offer work (M2's ghost path or M4's respawn-with-memory), not as adapter gold-plating now.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect, M1 close) Pixel fonts sourced from npm: `@fontsource/press-start-2p`, `@fontsource/pixelify-sans`, `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` (3 new deps, Architect-approved) · `scripts/sync-fonts.cjs` copies the latin-400 woff2 files into the gitignored drop on every install; fonts.ts and its visible-missing state unchanged. Closes M1.5b's font must-ask.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect, M1 close) M1 demo captured as screenshots in `docs/demo/` and embedded in README + the M1 implementation record · capture used a temporary env-gated auto-spawn in main, removed in the same commit; the README shot intentionally shows the interim (pre-tileset) art state.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect verdict) All 15 toolchain sub-dependencies RATIFIED after list review: vite@7, @vitejs/plugin-react@5, typescript-eslint, @eslint/js, eslint-config-prettier, @electron/rebuild, @xterm/addon-fit, @types/{node,react,react-dom,better-sqlite3}, @fontsource/{press-start-2p,pixelify-sans,ibm-plex-mono} · closes the open item flagged since M0.1.
- 2026-08-26 · (Architect directive) Interim floor tileset: Kenney CC0 packs (Roguelike/RPG + Roguelike Indoors, licenses verified in-pack) staged in the gitignored tileset drop — app detects `tileset: 2 sheet(s)` · LimeZu Modern Interiors (whose free tier is private-use-only and repo-incompatible) will be purchased by the Architect and replace them with zero code change; rendering *from* sheets (beyond intake/detection) is owed by the art integration in M2+.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) Every `git` invocation lives in `src/main/git.ts`, and `scripts/check-invariants.cjs` fails CI on a `git` call anywhere else · ADR-0004's single-committer claim is only worth what enforces it; the tripwire was proven to bite by planting a probe file (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §5 asks for grep-able tripwires in CI). The same script flags truncating writes to `log.jsonl`/the cost ledger (invariant §5).
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) Commit identity is passed per invocation (`-c user.name=Ephesus …`), never written into the repo config, along with `commit.gpgsign=false` and a neutered `core.hooksPath` · the Agora must not inherit — or depend on — whatever the machine's global git config happens to be; CI runners frequently have no identity at all, and a user's global hooks must never run against the company's repo.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) `ensureRepo()` commits ONLY when it actually seeded something · committing unconditionally swept up whatever a crashed run had left behind and labelled it "seed the Agora" — the work survived but the history misnamed why it landed, and ADR-0004 leans on that history for forensics. Caught in the live blackout run, not by a test; regression test added.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) Fault points (`before-stage`/`after-stage`/`before-commit`/`after-commit`/`before-reconcile`) are in the production commit path, not a mock · S-BLACKOUT has to interrupt *between* staging and committing, and a seam that only exists under a mock proves nothing about the real ordering.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) Retries live in the committer queue alone, with backoff-then-clear-lock ordering · BUILD-PROMPT §7 forbids retry loops around git elsewhere; a lock can only be stale here because this module is the only git caller and it serialises.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) Settings install/uninstall moved out of each adapter into one `InstalledSettingsPlan` (`src/main/engines/settings-install.ts`) · claude.ts and the fake adapter carried byte-identical copies of the rule most likely to damage someone's repository, and a fix in one would not have reached the other. The conformance suite still passes for both adapters unchanged, which is what made the refactor safe.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) The installation is recorded in the durable registry BEFORE the file is written · a crash between write and record would leave an untracked file in the Architect's tree, which is exactly the failure this closes.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.1) `git init` is in `agora.ts`, not `home.ts` · keeps `home.ts` git-free and leaves `src/main/git.ts` as the only module the tripwire has to allowlist.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) The log envelope (`ts`,`seq`,`kind`) is validated; the refs beside it are open (`z.looseObject`) · SDD §4.3 lists twenty kinds arriving across six milestones, each with different refs. A closed schema would mean editing `log.ts` every time a subsystem lands, and rejecting an event because a later milestone added a field would be losing the book of record to protect its formatting.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) `ts` and `seq` are stamped by the appender, never accepted from the caller · a caller-supplied seq could collide and a caller-supplied timestamp could reorder history; NFR-13 depends on the log being reconstructible in order.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) A torn final line is ignored on read and **left on disk**; the next append starts on a fresh line · truncating it would be a rewrite, and `log.jsonl` is append-only forever (invariant §5). Sequence numbering resumes from the last *intact* entry.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) Registry fields are split required / required-nullable / optional following the two worked examples in SDD §4.1 (Artemis carries `status`/`hookFidelity`/`spawnedAt`, Mason carries `hire`) · rather than inventing one uniform shape neither example matches.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) `canCloseTask()` returns *every* reason a task cannot close, not the first · a caller that fixes one obligation should not have to ask again to discover the next. Only `→ done` is guarded (SDD §4.2); inventing a fuller state machine would be improvisation BUILD-PROMPT §7 forbids.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) A corrupt `registry.json`/`tasks.json` yields the empty default plus a retained warning, and the file is never rewritten · same degradation contract as M0's `config.json`: visible, non-fatal, non-destructive.
- 2026-08-26 · (M2.2) Lifecycle events reach the log through an injected `onLogEvent` on `AgentManager`, not an imported Agora · keeps the lifecycle testable without an Agora on disk and routes every spawn/exit through one place.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) `requires_reply` is DERIVED from the act at compose time and re-checked at pickup; a message whose flag disagrees with its act is rejected · ADR-0003 calls the anti-livelock rules "transport rules, not etiquette", and a sender that could clear the flag could opt out of the obligation table.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) A message in agent A's outbox claiming `from: agent.b` is rejected as a forgery · single-writer-per-file means an outbox carries only its owner's mail; without this check one agent could impersonate another by writing in its own directory.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) A message the router cannot carry is parked in `outbox/.rejected/`, not deleted and not left in place · deleting loses what the agent got wrong, leaving it re-rejects forever. SDD §2 does not name this directory — smallest choice consistent with `inbox/.done/`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) Delivery ordering is rename → log → drain outbox, with no transaction · a crash before the rename loses nothing (the outbox file is still there); a crash after it delivers the same `<id>.json` twice at worst, and a second rename to the same path is a no-op. Both directions asserted with the fault seam.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) `.done/` is the authority on what was consumed; `cursor.json` is the high-water mark · they answer different questions, and only `.done/` survives out-of-order delivery. Replay of an already-consumed id is dropped by the `.done/` check, not the cursor.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) `sweep()` serialises on a promise chain like the committer does · the watcher and the periodic timer both fire on the same mail, and two passes racing over the same files would double-deliver.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) Hermes exposes a `route()` seam that M2.4 fills with the hop-cap/bounce/broadcast rules · keeps delivery mechanics and routing policy separable, so S-LIVELOCK and S-BOUNCE can assert the rules at the module boundary.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.3) Hermes fault points (`before-deliver`/`after-deliver`/`before-drain-outbox`/`before-consume`/`after-consume`) live in the production path · same reason as the Agora's: S-BLACKOUT must interrupt real ordering, not a mock's.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 CI fix) The conformance suite's live-spawn helper sends the interrupt key exactly once, guards `stdin.writable`, and swallows a late `stdin` EPIPE · a stdout chunk arriving after the child was killed wrote to a dead pipe, and the async EPIPE failed the whole vitest run rather than an assertion. Green on Windows, red on CI's Linux — found only because CI was checked before the next package started.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) `DEFAULT_HOP_CAP = 8` · ADR-0003 names the rule but not the number. Eight leaves room for a genuine multi-turn exchange (ask → clarify → answer → confirm, twice) while catching a two-agent ping-pong within seconds rather than minutes of budget. Configurable per `RoutingContext`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) The hop cap is checked BEFORE the address · a livelocked ping-pong must be stopped whether or not its recipient still exists; the point of the cap is to stop the loop, not to adjudicate the address. Asserted by a case that diverts a capped message aimed at a dead agent.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) With no orchestrator yet, `to: "human"` and hop-cap diversions queue at `agora/human/` — outside `agents/` · FR-3.7 routes `human` to Artemis as the Architect's proxy, and until that proxy exists losing the message would be the worst outcome available. `human` is deliberately not an agent id and never appears in the roster.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) A bounce is written straight into the sender's inbox, not through the sender's outbox · the sender did not write the refusal, and an outbox carries only its owner's mail (the same rule the forgery check enforces).
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) A broadcast with no recipient other than the sender bounces rather than delivering to nobody · "never drop silently" (FR-3.4) applies to a broadcast into an empty company too.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.4) `needs_human` is carried and logged but does NOT change the address · SDD §4.4 calls it a flag the "router/Artemis may flip", and FR-3.7 makes `to:"human"` the addressing mechanism. Acting on the flag is Artemis's job in M3; re-routing on it here would be inventing a rule the docs do not have.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) The three ADR-0013 guards are decided by ONE pure function (`decideStop`) so their ordering is visible and testable · `stop_hook_active` is checked first so a hook can never chain off its own continuation, and the cap is checked *before* pending work so a pathological loop stops even while mail keeps arriving. Reversing either would make the guards decorative.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) `DEFAULT_BLOCK_CAP = 20`, `PATHOLOGY_SIGNAL_AT = 10` · ADR-0013 requires a cap to exist, not a particular value. The signal sits deliberately *below* the cap so the breaker (ADR-0011, M3) can steer at rung 1 before the backstop stops the work outright.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) `decideStop` returns FACTS (`pendingMail`, `pendingTasks`), never prose · ADR-0013 calls the block reason "a real prompt surface subject to the same review as system prompts", so it is rendered by the caller from `prompts/hermes/stop-block-reason.md` (invariant §8). Asserted by a test that fails if the decision object contains English.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) The Stop-hook reply travels back as the HTTP response body, and the shim relays it to stdout — but ONLY when it contains a decision · M1.4 made the shim silent on stdout because engines read it as instructions; the autonomy loop is precisely the case where that channel is the point. Silent-unless-instructed is preserved, with a regression test for both halves.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) The wake watchdog nudges once per *pending episode*, keyed on the inbox emptying rather than on a timer · "exactly once" (FR-3.5) means once per batch of mail, not once ever; a nudge is suppressed entirely when the agent is not idle, which is the stale-nudge case ADR-0013 names.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) The nudge is delivered through the command queue, not a raw PTY write · it is a prompt, so FR-1.3 queue-until-idle applies to it exactly as it does to the Architect own typing.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) `stop.pending` is now the same fact Hermes gives the Stop hook, injected into `AvatarDirector` as `hasPendingWork` · the floor and the autonomy loop can no longer disagree about whether an agent is done. **Closes the second M1 carried item.**
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.5) Every stop decision is logged with its machine-readable `because` tag · a loop that continues silently is exactly the pathology R2 warns about, and this is where the breaker and the next briefing will read it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.6) The roster row is written from `AgentManager` through an injected `onRosterChange`, mirroring the same lifecycle the card and the log already mirror · one owner for the write, and the lifecycle stays testable without an Agora on disk.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.6) Every hire gets `seat: 'terrace'` for now · SDD §4.1 shows `temple` for Artemis and `terrace-3` for a worker, but seat assignment belongs with the floor layout and Artemis's reserved seat in M3. A single honest placeholder beats inventing a numbering scheme the docs do not define.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.6) The Activity tab holds a cursor and pages forward; `log:append` carries no payload, only "the log grew" · the panel is a pointer to `log.jsonl`, never a second record (NFR-13), and a push carrying rows would immediately become a second copy that could disagree with the file.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.6) Appends are coalesced into one pull per 120 ms · SDD §11 asks for batched log rendering; a burst of thirty events costs one render rather than thirty.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.6) The tab strip shows only Floor and Activity · UI-DESIGN §4 lists nine Command Center tabs, but offering a tab for a subsystem that does not exist would be inventing UI (BUILD-PROMPT §7). The rest arrive with their milestones.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7) The scenario suites run REAL spawned `fake-engine` processes over real git, a real socket and real files · every one of them is about what happens to state on disk when something goes wrong, and a mock cannot be interrupted between a rename and a commit. "Restart" is modelled by abandoning the objects mid-flight and building a fresh company over the same home, as a killed process and a new one would.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7) `clearStaleLock` retries and then gives up quietly instead of throwing · a git child of the process we just replaced can hold `index.lock` open for a moment on Windows, and failing to clear a lock is a delay the commit retry absorbs, not a boot worth losing. Found by S-BLACKOUT.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7) Temp-home cleanup is separated from `Company.close()` · a blackout scenario deliberately runs two companies over one home, and deleting the directory while the survivor still has a commit in flight is a teardown race, not a finding.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7, Architect-visible) The claude adapter now grants each agent file access to its OWN `agora/agents/<id>/` directory via `settings.local.json` permissions · an agent's mailbox lives in the harness home, outside its working directory, so the engine's permission model blocked every outbox write — FR-3.2 was unimplementable without it. Found by a real agent in the exit demo answering "the write was blocked by permissions". The grant is the narrowest thing that makes the documented design work: its own directory, never the Agora, never `agents/`, never a sibling mailbox. **Raised in the session report as a permission-default decision.**
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7) `InstalledSettingsPlan` reference-counts per settings path; the LAST agent out restores the original · two agents in one repository share one `settings.local.json`, and the first to finish was deleting the file the second was still running under — the survivor lost access to its own mailbox mid-turn. Found by the two-real-agent exit demo; regression tests named for it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2.7) `prompts/agora/PROTOCOL.md` gained the concrete message format, and `identity.md` the agent's mailbox path · a real agent cannot follow a contract that never tells it where its outbox is or what a message looks like. The exit demo proved the addition works: the agent quoted the rule back ("`requires_reply: true` as protocol mandates for a `request` act").
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out) `Agora.commitSoon()` replaces every `void agora.commit(...)` · `void` attaches no rejection handler, so a commit that exhausted its retry budget became an `unhandledRejection` — a failed vitest run in CI, and a dead Electron main process in production. A git failure is the exact fault ADR-0004's queue exists to absorb; it must never be what takes the company down. The give-up is now recorded (bounded at 50) and reported through `onCommitError`. Found by CI going red on a run where all 581 tests passed.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out) `onCommitError` carries a written contract that it must not log or queue work · the failing subsystem is git itself, and a handler that commits would recurse. `index.ts` reports it to the same `console.warn` surface hook drift and settings-sweep failures already use.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out) The watcher's `sweep()` and the pty exit handler got the same treatment (`onSweepError`, `onExitError`) · both are fire-and-forget by nature and both provably reject — three existing tests assert `sweep()` rejects — so both were live harness-killers of the same class, not hypothetical ones.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out) Each of the three regression tests installs a `process.on('unhandledRejection')` probe AND asserts the failure was recorded · asserting only "no unhandled rejection" would pass vacuously if the failure path stopped firing. No test deliberately creates an unhandled rejection to prove the probe works — that would fail the run it is defending.

- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M2 close) The M2.7 mailbox permission grant is **RATIFIED as-is** · each agent's `settings.local.json` grants file access to its OWN `agora/agents/<id>/` directory only — the narrowest grant that makes FR-3.2 implementable. Recorded as an Architect-ratified permission default; no memo obligation attached.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M2 close) The M2 demo view stays textual · the exit criterion is a behaviour, not a screen, and the verified transcript is the honest artifact. Embedded in README with status "M2 complete"; a screenshot of the Activity tab is owed from the next local run and noted there.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 plan) S-GATE's voice/remote clauses are built as POLICY SEAMS in M3 · source-channel and repeat-back are first-class gate-policy inputs tested with scripted stubs (fake STT transcript, fake remote channel); the Herald (M6) and Harbor (M7) adapters plug into the same seam. S-GATE passes fully in M3 at the policy boundary, per TEST-STRATEGY's own "policy layer test with scripted STT" wording.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 plan) FR-5.4 "respawn with memory intact" means ENGINE-NATIVE RESUME in M3 · adapter `ResumeSupport` (claude `--resume` with the session id the event plane records) + re-injected identity/roster context. Library-backed `memory.md` continuity is M4's job; the M1-audit resume capability gap closes inside M3.7 rather than as adapter gold-plating.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 plan) Telemetry in M3 is span CAPTURE only · the breaker's repetition/error signals need span data (agent, tool, duration, outcome) anyway, so M3.5 records it; the FR-11.6 waterfall UI waits for a later milestone rather than being invented UI now.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 plan) Sheet-based floor rendering and the owed §8 badge double-encoding ride M3.6's floor-layout package · the temple room needs drawing anyway, and gates/breaker make `waiting`/`blocked`/`looping` reachable for the first time — the color-only badge pairs stop being low-urgency exactly there.
- 2026-08-27 · (CI) `check-invariants.cjs` allowlists `scripts/arm-hooks.cjs` and `scripts/check-attribution.cjs` for git invocation · ADR-0004's single-committer rule governs the running app's Agora; these two are development-repo tools that execute outside the app process against this repository itself and can never touch a harness home. The allowlist stays named-files-only — the tripwire caught them the moment they landed, which is the tripwire working, not a false positive of design.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) The sweep timer and the watcher share ONE guarded tick (`sweepAndWake`) · the close-out fixed `void this.sweep()` on the watcher path and missed the identical defect on the periodic timer — the audit proved it process-fatal with a live probe. One tick, one rejection handler, and the wake watchdog chained on, so there is no second path to forget.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) `wakeCheck()` runs on every production sweep tick, not on its own schedule · the watchdog had zero production callers — S-WAKE proved the decision logic while the shipped app never ran it. Chaining it onto the sweep means it runs exactly as often as delivery does, which is the only time pending mail can newly exist.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit, Architect verdict) Inbox consumption is HAND-OVER: `decideOnStop` and `wakeCheck` move mail to `inbox/.done/` in the same act that renders its content into the block reason / wake nudge · nothing else ever consumed in production, so a protocol-following agent re-blocked on the same handled mail until the cap. Consumption at hand-over makes "the session has seen it" and "it is archived" the same event; PROTOCOL.md now says handed-over mail is already archived. The alternative (agents move their own mail) was rejected for hinging the R2 pathology on an LLM remembering a chore.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) `wakeCheck` refuses to run without a nudge sink · consuming mail with nobody to hand it to would archive messages no session ever saw — silent loss dressed as tidiness.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) `{{messages}}` in the hermes prompt surfaces carries the handed-over messages as verbatim JSON (`formatHandover`) · the data is data; every word of framing stays in `prompts/hermes/` (invariant §8). The no-prompts test fallback is now a mechanical serialization for the same reason.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit, Architect verdict) Data-plane degradations surface through `agora:health` + a status-strip chip · `fileWarnings()`/`commitFailures()` had no consumers and every runtime error callback ended at `console.warn`, which invariant §7 does not accept as "visible". One bounded collector (`reportDegradation`, cap 50) feeds the chip; `console.warn` stays as the developer trace.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) A schema file that failed to parse refuses overwrite until a later read succeeds · the M2.2 decision promised the corrupt file is "never rewritten", but the first roster write after corruption replaced it with the empty default. The refusal throws; `onRosterChange` reports it as a degradation and the company keeps running.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) Bounce subject/body render from `prompts/hermes/bounce-*.md` · the refusal is read by an LLM, so it is a prompt surface like the block reason and the nudge (invariant §8). The 200-char subject truncation stays in code — mechanics, not words.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) `EPH_BLOCK_CAP` is the ADR-0013 env-configurable cap, parsed by `blockCapFromEnv` · only a positive integer wins; junk, zero and negatives are refused visibly and the default holds, so the environment can tune the cap but never disable it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit) `HookServer.onEvent` may be async, with `onEventError` reporting a handler failure while the engine gets fail-open `{ok:true}` · the Stop decision now consumes the inbox before replying, and an awaited handler that rejected would otherwise be a new member of exactly the unhandled-rejection class this audit closed.
- 2026-08-27 · (M2 close-out audit, recorded not fixed) The router-authored bounce still says `from: <original sender>` · SDD §4.4 gives the router no legal `from` identity, and inventing one is a schema change (§8 must-ask). Flagged for M3, where Artemis becomes the natural author of such refusals.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) The secret store lives at `<harness home>/secrets.enc`, mode 0600 · SDD §2 lists the harness home's contents but predates the broker; the store must sit outside the Agora (never committed, never agent-readable) and outside SQLite (`db.sqlite` is a file the ledger and window state share), which leaves the harness-home root. SDD §2 updated to list it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) `secrets:test` asks "can the broker still retrieve this?", not "does the provider accept it?" · no provider adapter exists before the Herald (M6) and the Harbor (M7), and a test that pretends to reach a provider it cannot reach is worse than none. The question it does ask is a real failure: an OS keychain that rotated leaves a file of undecryptable blobs whose first symptom would otherwise be an agent spawning without its credential.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) A machine with no OS encryption backend gets a REFUSAL, not a plaintext fallback · ADR-0010 permits "an encrypted file keyed per-machine" as the fallback, which is what `safeStorage` already is; storing plaintext instead would break NFR-8. the broker REPORTS the refusal at construction through `onDegraded` → the status-strip chip, and the store file is never created. Reporting, not a queryable `health()` field: the M2 close-out audit already ruled that a state with no consumer does not satisfy invariant §7, and the first draft of this package repeated that mistake. Proven live: on a box with no keyring, `set` throws and no file appears.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) The redaction filter holds back a trailing region ONLY when it is genuinely a prefix of a known secret · the alternative (a fixed look-back window) would delay the last characters of every prompt an engine draws, on every chunk, forever. With the prefix test, ordinary output is emitted with zero delay and a credential torn across two PTY reads is still caught — proven live with a 0.4 s gap between the halves.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) Values shorter than 4 characters are never masked (`REDACTION_MIN_LENGTH`) · a three-character "secret" has no entropy to protect and masking it would shred every terminal that prints those characters — more damage than the leak.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) `AgentManager` re-scopes whatever `resolveGrants` returns to the declared names before building the env · "undeclared vars never reach a spawn" has to hold at the boundary that composes the environment, not only inside whichever resolver is wired in. Written after a test that fed the manager an over-answering resolver caught the gap.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) `writeFileAtomic` gained an optional `mode`, applied to the TEMP file before the rename · a secret store that is world-readable for even the width of a follow-up `chmod` is a leak.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1) `check-invariants.cjs` now implements the two ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §5 tripwires that were documented but unenforced: no credential read from `process.env` outside `src/main/watch/` and `src/main/herald/`, and no secret-shaped string anywhere in `src/`, `shims/`, `scripts/` or `test/` · both proven to bite on planted probes. `EPH_*` is excluded — the harness's own spawn-scoped variables are declared capabilities, not credentials. The secret-shape patterns are prefix-anchored rather than entropy-based: a heuristic that fired on any long random string would flag every hook-token fixture in the suite, and a tripwire nobody trusts gets deleted.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) The `secrets:` IPC group is **exactly SDD §5's four channels**; the `list`/`health` channels the first draft added are gone · SDD §5 calls its listing abridged, but neither extra channel had a consumer, and widening a documented IPC signature is a BUILD-PROMPT §8 must-ask rather than an implementation detail. The API-surface test now pins the documented four, so a fifth fails CI whether or not it reads a value. Storage health reaches the Architect through the existing degradation surface instead.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) The broker memoizes its decrypted values and invalidates on `set`/`delete` · the redaction filter consults them on EVERY pty chunk for every agent; decrypting per chunk would be N synchronous OS-crypto calls on the main thread at the PTY data rate (SDD §11) and — worse for a write-only broker — a fresh plaintext copy of every credential on the heap just as often. A test counts decrypts across 50 chunks and asserts exactly one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) Grants resolve in `start()`, not in `spawnConfig()` · ADR-0010 injects credentials *at spawn*, and the config is built before the version probe runs. The old placement decrypted credentials for the missing-binary path that deliberately discards them, and — after an install offer finished — reused a stale config, so a credential stored *while watching the install* never reached the agent that followed it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) The stream wiring moved to `src/main/pty-stream.ts`, which imports no native module · with it inside `pty.ts` the only thing standing between an echoed credential and the renderer was covered by a manual live run: deleting the `filter.push` call left all 633 tests green. Eight tests now assert the edge, and four of them fail on exactly that deletion.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) `check-invariants.cjs` applies only the SECRET rules to `test/` · the first draft added `test` to the shared search list, silently extending the git and append-only tripwires there too. TEST-STRATEGY §6 explicitly wants integration tests against real git in temp dirs, so that widening would have failed CI for a rule that was never about tests. Proven both ways with planted probes.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.1 review) `delete` appends `secret-rotated` with `removed: true`, and the `spawn` entry carries grant NAMES · FR-11.4 names set/rotate/**delete** and ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §4 requires anything written into an agent's environment to be logged *and* on the card; the card half held, the log half did not. SDD §4.3's refs are open by design, so neither is a schema change.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 run topology) Each M3 package branch is cut from the PREVIOUS package's branch, not from `main` · the run's instruction says "cut from main", which presupposes each package merges to `main` as it lands — but `main` is protected (PRs only, one review, ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §2), no PR was authorised for this run, and the packages are genuinely dependent: M3.5's breaker consumes M3.2's burn-rate projection, M3.8's routing consumes M3.7's Artemis, and M3.9's suites consume all eight. Cut strictly from `main`, the chain would stop building at M3.5. Chaining keeps every rule that matters intact — one package per branch, one commit series per package, nothing committed to `main` directly, no PR opened — and leaves the Architect free to merge the chain or cherry-pick per package. **Raised in the session report.**

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) The fold cursor counts FACTS ALREADY FOLDED per transcript file, not bytes · `TranscriptReader.read()` returns a whole file's facts, so the ledger folds `facts.slice(folded)`. Re-reading a growing transcript is the common path, not an edge case, and this makes it structurally incapable of double-counting. A file that SHRANK re-folds from zero and reports it: silently skipping a rotated file's first N facts forever is the under-reporting the ledger exists to prevent.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) `costUsd` stays null unless a row reported one; it is never coerced to 0 · Claude Code reports no per-message cost, and a `0` would read as "this was free". The UI must be able to tell "nothing spent" from "not reported" (invariant §7). Deriving dollars would need a price table this milestone has no source for.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) Cache-creation and cache-read tokens count as INPUT tokens · they are tokens the provider billed on the way in; leaving them out would under-report by an order of magnitude (the real transcript folded 72.3M input tokens, of which almost all are cache).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) The burn-rate projection refuses to answer with less than 5 minutes of history · a rate computed from a handful of seconds projects absurd numbers and would trip the breaker on a healthy agent's first tool call. "We do not know yet" (null) is the honest answer, and `because` says which input decided the verdict.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) Spend is folded on a 15 s timer, not per hook event · it is not a real-time quantity, and a fold per tool call would re-read every transcript dozens of times a minute for a figure nobody reads that often (SDD §11). The tick is guarded and non-reentrant, and its rejection handler is attached — the harness-killer class the M2 close-out audit closed.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) `budget` log events fire only on STATE TRANSITIONS · a breached agent emitting an event every tick would turn the book of record into a metronome, and the briefing that reads it would drown.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) `budgetSchema` lives in `src/shared/agents.ts`, re-exported by `registry.ts` · defining it in `registry.ts` and importing it into `agents.ts` is a cycle (`registry.ts` already imports `agentIdSchema` from `agents.ts`), and a cycle in a module zod initializes at import time is a crash, not a style problem — it broke 15 suites before it was moved.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) The conformance suite's transcript case takes a per-adapter SAMPLE instead of one hardcoded format · the existing case wrote the fake engine's JSON and asserted every adapter read it, so the moment the claude adapter declared `transcripts` it "failed" for parsing its own engine's format correctly. Every engine writes its own (NFR-12); what conformance owns is the behaviour ADR-0009 specifies — missing file yields nothing, unrecognized lines are skipped, a good line yields exactly what it said.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2) `SpawnRequest` gained an optional `budget`, mirroring `registryEntrySchema.budget` · FR-11.2 enforces per-agent budgets and the hire is where one is declared. Optional exactly as the registry's is: an unbudgeted hire is legal and reports `unbudgeted`, never a zero the Watch would immediately breach.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) A gate rule can only PERMIT; there is no deny rule · denial is the default, and a deny rule would immediately raise "which rule wins" — the ordering bug deny-by-default exists to avoid. The policy shape is the smallest that serves UC-08 and FR-11.1's four named classes plus the two choke-point classes; no policy language was invented.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) A spend rule with NO cap permits nothing · an uncapped spend allowance is an allowance nobody actually wrote, and reading it as "unlimited" would be the most expensive possible misreading of a config typo. Same for a spend request that does not state an amount: it cannot be under any cap.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) A rule permits the `local` channel unless it names others · NFR-9 treats remote and voice approvals as different acts from a click at the keyboard, so neither is implied by a local allowance.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) Refusing a voice approval for want of repeat-back leaves the gate OPEN; it is not a denial · "we could not confirm you meant it" and "no" are different answers, and collapsing them would teach the Architect that the microphone denies things at random.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) The policy REQUIRES repeat-back; the surface that took the approval PERFORMS it · only that surface knows whether the human actually repeated it back. That split is the seam the Herald plugs into in M6 and is why S-GATE's voice clause is testable now with a scripted stub (Architect decision).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) A `gate-policy.json` that fails to parse composes as deny-all, with a visible warning · the safe direction for a parse error to fail in. Proven live: a corrupt file leaves `autonomy=manual rules=0` and reports why.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) `notification` joins `HOOK_EVENTS`, and the claude adapter maps `Notification` to it · the M1 carried item. Its payload schema is loose like the rest: a notification with no message still opens a gate, because "the engine is waiting on you" is the fact that matters and losing it to a missing field is the stall the event exists to end.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) `packageNotification` fills UC-08's four fields honestly rather than inventing a risk assessment · the engine says only that it is waiting and roughly what about. The blast radius of an action the engine has NOT performed is exactly "whatever it was about to do", and the rollback for a refusal is that it never happens.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) A `needs_human` message is delivered AND gated · FR-3.3 forbids escalation that swallows mail; the gate is about the decision behind the message, not about the message.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) Only the LAST gate clearing on an agent returns its avatar to its prior state · an agent held behind two gates must not walk back to its desk after the first verdict.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) The `gate:open` push carries the gate that opened (or null on a verdict) as a NUDGE; the renderer re-reads `watch:approvals` · a push carrying the queue would become a second copy that could disagree with main. Same rule the Activity feed follows.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3) Gate prose lives in `prompts/watch/` · what an agent or the Architect reads about a held action is a prompt surface like the block reason and the bounce (invariant §8). The gate itself carries a machine-readable `because`. **Corrected at review: the first draft created the prompt files and never loaded them while the packaging prose sat inline in `.ts` — the entry claimed compliance the code did not have. The packaging now renders from `prompts/watch/packaging-*.md`.**

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) The Watch folds only the transcripts belonging to session ids the event plane recorded, and the fold cursor is keyed `(agent, source)` · an engine keys its transcript directory on the working directory, not on the agent. FR-1.5 makes worktree isolation optional, so a shared `cwd` is the documented default — and the first draft folded the whole directory, attributing two agents' spend (and the Architect's own `claude` history in that repo) to whoever ticked first. Mis-attribution is the exact reason ADR-0011 rejected provider-side caps. **An empty session list folds nothing**: recording nothing until we know whose it is beats recording it as ours.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) `UsageFact` carries the engine's timestamp, and `day` is the day of SPEND · the ledger's `day` column IS the budget window (SDD §4.6 with registry §4.1). Folding on the harness's clock billed an old transcript's tokens to today — an agent in a previously-used repo breached on its first tick from history alone — and billed an agent running across midnight to tomorrow. ADR-0009 names the `transcripts` member, not the fact shape, so this is a §8.2 mechanical choice rather than a §8.3 schema deviation; recorded here because it changes what a documented column means.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) The burn-rate window carries its own spend baseline · `spent` is durable (all of today, across restarts) while `elapsedMinutes` can only measure this process's uptime. Dividing the first by the second projected a healthy agent straight into `projected-breach` after any restart or mid-day spawn — the false trip ADR-0011 warns about, and the 5-minute floor could not prevent it because the *origin* was wrong, not the sample size.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) An agent's transcripts are folded ONE last time on exit, before its session is forgotten · usage written in the seconds between the final tick and the exit would otherwise never be folded, and that spawn is never `running` under that session again. Under-reporting is the ledger's one unforgivable failure.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) The enforced budget comes from the ROSTER when the spawn request omits one · the renderer is untrusted (invariant §2), and taking the ceiling from whatever it supplied meant a hire whose registry entry declared 500k was unbudgeted the moment a spawn call left the field out, with a harness restart losing the declaration entirely.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) `AgentSpend.reporting` distinguishes "this engine cannot report usage" from "this agent spent nothing" · ADR-0009 makes `transcripts` optional, so the first is a visible product tier; a bare zero for both is the silent fallback invariant §7 refuses.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) `check-invariants.cjs` gained a tripwire for `UPDATE`/`DELETE FROM cost_ledger`, with no allowlist · the ledger became a SQL table in M3.2, so the rewrite vector is no longer a `writeFileSync` the old pattern could see. `cost_fold_cursor` is deliberately not covered — it is metadata about reading, and it is meant to be updated. Proven to bite on both statements.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) `COST_SCHEMA_VERSION` deleted rather than persisted · invariant §9 versions schema'd *files*; the ledger is a SQLite table governed by migration on open, and a constant nothing writes was the appearance of compliance with nothing behind it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review) `MemoryLedgerStore` is a TEST DOUBLE only; its doc no longer offers it as a runtime fallback · cumulative spend in memory is exactly what invariant §11 forbids, so a harness without SQLite must fail visibly rather than keep totals a restart erases.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.2 review, plan) FR-11.2's UI clause had no owner in the M3 plan and is assigned to M3.4 · `watch: budgets()` shipped with no consumer and M3.8's "Ledger tab" is the task kanban (FR-4.3), not cost. The Watch panel is the Watch's surface; the strip shows the two figures the requirement names and nothing more. **Raised in the session report.**

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) `watch: humanQueue()` is added to the `watch:` group · SDD §5 calls its listing abridged and names the generated `.d.ts` as normative once code exists, so filling in a channel the design requires is not a deviation. The test the M3.1 review taught: a channel is legitimate when something *consumes* it and a requirement demands it — here the M2 carried item (`agora/human/` accumulating unread) and FR-3.7. `secrets:list`/`secrets:health` failed that test and were removed; this passes it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) The approvals panel re-reads `watch:approvals` after every verdict instead of editing its own copy · the renderer is a projection (invariant §2). A panel that removed the row itself would be holding gate state, and would disagree with main the moment a verdict was refused.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) A refused verdict is SHOWN in the panel, not swallowed · "we could not take that verdict" — a voice approval never repeated back, a gate that settled while the row was on screen — is exactly the state the Architect must see (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) `gateApproveSchema` lives in `src/shared/gates.ts`, not inline in `ipc.ts` · ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §3 puts validators in `src/shared/`, next to the types they validate.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) `humanQueue()` skips a file it cannot parse rather than failing the whole queue · one torn message must not make the rest of the Architect's mail invisible. The sweep already parks and reports malformed mail; a reader's job is to read what is there.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) Budget state is rendered as a WORD, not a colour · UI-DESIGN §8 and NFR-15 require status double-encoding; `breached` reads as `breached` in a screenshot with no colour at all.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.4) An engine that reports no usage renders `—` and "engine reports no usage", never `0 tok` · a zero from an engine that cannot report and a zero from an agent that spent nothing are different facts (invariant §7).

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) NFR-9 is enforced on the VERDICT, not only on the request · `requiresRepeatBack` was derived from the hold reason, which under deny-by-default is almost always `no-rule` — so the flag was false on exactly the destructive ops the clause protects. And `decide()` never consulted `rule.channels`, so a remote approval of a gate the policy never opened to remote was taken. A gate held under deny-by-default matched no rule by definition; checking the channel only on the way in left NFR-9 binding on nothing at all. `checkVerdictChannel` now polices the approval side, and `repeatBackRequired` derives from the gate's own facts, with destructive ops always requiring repeat-back by voice.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) A rule at `autonomy: 'manual'` permits nothing, and an effective level of `manual` permits nothing · the file already said so in prose; the rank comparison let `manual === manual` fall through to allow, which left ADR-0012's tightening with no floor. FR-11.1 reads `manual` as "I approve this by hand".
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) `tool-permission` is never permittable, whatever the policy says · "allow" is not a meaningful verdict for the engine's own dialog: the harness has no action to permit, the engine is blocked on a human, and a policy that answered it would silently restore the invisible stall the M1 carried item was about (invariant §7). The stall is also reported as a degradation, independent of the gate.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) `wireGateChokePoints` is the single implementation of SDD §9's three choke points, called by both `index.ts` and the scenario rig · the rig had copied the wiring character-for-character, so S-GATE would have stayed green with the production choke points deleted — the same defect class the M3.1 review caught in `pty.ts`. Proven by planting exactly that defect: 6 tests across both suites now fail.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) Repeated submissions coalesce per (agent, kind), keeping the FIRST packaging · engines emit notifications repeatedly, and a gate per event would bury the queue and turn `log.jsonl` into a metronome. The first packaging is kept because that is the question the Architect is already being asked; swapping it mid-decision is worse than a duplicate.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) Gate ids carry 64 bits of suffix, not 16 · under a shared clock, twenty gates had ~0.3% odds of a collision, and a collision silently overwrote a still-open gate *after* its `onOpen` had fired — a held action vanishing from the queue.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) Two rules for one kind are refused by the schema AND resolved strictest-first by the matcher · either alone would do; both means an Architect who wrote two by accident is told rather than quietly overruled, and the matcher never depends on which was written first.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) The spend cap is `maxSpendTokens`, not `maxSpendCents` · the durable ledger reports tokens (the engine reports no per-message cost), so the cents field was being compared against a token count and the policy knob was silently meaningless. It becomes a currency cap when an engine reports one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) `gate:open` carries the gate ID, not the gate · both the channel's own doc comment and the M3.3 log entry said it was a nudge and that the renderer re-reads `watch:approvals`; the code sent the whole `OpenGate`, which is the second copy of the queue that contract exists to prevent.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) The gate-policy warning reports on CHANGE, not on every evaluation · the policy is re-read per evaluation, so a corrupt file plus a chatty agent evicted every other entry from the bounded health buffer — the opposite of what invariant §7 asks for.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.3 review) `gate-policy.json` is documented in SDD §2 · a new schema'd file at the harness-home root, and the first draft added it without recording the choice. It sits beside `config.json` rather than inside it because a policy the harness cannot read must compose as deny-all *without* taking the rest of the config down with it.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) The breaker's signals are pure functions over spans the caller supplies; nothing in `src/shared/breaker.ts` reads a clock, a file or a process · a trip has to be explicable after the fact rather than a mood the harness was in, and that means the same inputs always producing the same verdict.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) Repetition means the same tool AND the same argument fingerprint · an agent reading twenty different files is working; an agent reading one file twenty times is stuck, and only the fingerprint tells them apart. A breaker that fires on the first is one the Architect turns off, and then it protects nothing.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) Spans carry a DIGEST of the call's arguments, never the arguments · spans are local-only (NFR-10) but they are also read by the briefing compiler, and a tool call's arguments can contain anything the agent was working on. Asserted by a test that plants a path and a secret-shaped value and greps the serialized spans.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) The error signal ignores spans still open · an open span has no outcome yet, and counting it either way would be a guess.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) A **rung dwell** (60 s default) gates every climb after the first · without it the ladder climbed once per tool call, so an error storm reached `stop` three calls after the floor and the steer — queued until the agent is idle (FR-1.3) — may not even have been delivered. A ladder that climbs three rungs in three seconds is the kill switch ADR-0011 explicitly is not. Rung 1 is never delayed, and recovery is never delayed: an agent that stopped misbehaving should not serve out a sentence. **Found by the S-BREAKER scenario, not by review.**
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) Rung 2 HOLDS mail in the sender's outbox rather than dropping it · ADR-0011 says "pause its Hermes inbox deliveries"; constraining an agent is not the same as losing its mail, and the held message arrives when the pause lifts.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) The breaker evaluates on span CLOSE, not on a timer · the signals are about tool calls, so a tool call finishing is exactly when the answer can have changed. The dwell, not the cadence, is what paces the ladder.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) `Breaker.forceEvaluate` exists for scenarios only and has no production caller · a scenario asserting the ladder must be able to step it without sleeping a minute per rung; the dwell is the property, not an obstacle to it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5) Every rung's corrective sentence is a file in `prompts/watch/steer-*.md` · the breaker supplies facts (`{{tool}}`, `{{repeats}}`, `{{errors}}`) and every word is config (invariant §8). The template is chosen by the signal tag, which is data.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.5, bug fix) Bundled pixel fonts are fetched by RELATIVE path · a packaged app loads its renderer over `file://`, where `/fonts/x.woff2` resolves to the filesystem root and `fetch` fails outright — so the built app had never loaded a face and showed "3 of 3 pixel faces missing" permanently, while the dev server's http origin made it look fine. A degradation warning that is always on is worse than none: it trains the Architect to ignore the surface every other degradation shares. Found by reading an M3.5 evidence screenshot; regression test resolves the path against both a `file://` and an `http://` base.
