- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) A seat is `temple` or `terrace-<n>`, assigned by a pure function over the seats already taken · SDD §4.1 writes both by example, and the two properties that make a seat a *place* — an agent keeps it, and the temple is Artemis's — are only testable if assignment has no clock, no randomness and no dependence on the order the roster is read in.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) A seat that does not parse is REASSIGNED, not rejected · every roster written in M1 and M2 says `seat: 'terrace'`, and tightening `registryEntrySchema.seat` from `string` would have turned all of them into unreadable rosters. The floor also draws an unparseable seat rather than crashing on it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) `AgentManager` remembers the seats it hands out, on top of the roster it is given · the roster is the durable memory, but it is written *by* the result of the assignment, so two hires in the same tick would otherwise both be handed `terrace-1`. Asserted by a test that starts two spawns before either roster write lands.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) A worker holding the temple seat in the roster is turned out of it · a file written by hand, or by an older harness, is not authority to sit in the temple (ADR-0005). The reserved seat is reserved by the assignment rule, not by everyone else's good behaviour.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) `role === 'orchestrator'` is the single predicate for who owns the temple, exported as `isOrchestratorRole` · the roster's `orchestratorId` is not set until Artemis is hired (M3.7), and the floor already read the same role string for her silhouette. One predicate, so the two cannot drift.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) The floor's layout is a pure `floorPlan()` of typed cells, and art *paints* it · ADR-0014 requires the floor to render only from event-plane data and never become a second source of truth. UI-DESIGN §7's licensed tileset is exactly the thing that could quietly invert that — a station existing because a tile was drawn there. With the plan in front, installing a pack changes how the floor looks and cannot change what is on it; the same run with and without a pack produced identical seats, phases and census.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) A tileset pack ships its own `*.tiles.json` tile map, validated by `src/shared/tileset.ts` · every pack lays its sheet out differently and the sheets stay out of the repository (§7 licence rules), so hard-coded frame indices would be fiction: they would compile, look deliberate, and paint whatever sat at those offsets. `station:<name>` overrides the generic `station` key, and an unmapped cell falls back to the procedural tile so a partial pack cannot punch holes in the floor.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) A sheet with no map, an invalid map, or a map naming a missing sheet all leave the floor procedural AND say which · a tileset that quietly failed to load would look exactly like one nobody had installed yet (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) The tile map refuses a `tilePx` that does not divide the 32px world tile · UI-DESIGN §7 permits "integer scaling only, pixel-snap preserved"; a validator is where that stops being a sentence.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) Every status badge carries a GLYPH beside its colour, and the canvas carries a census in words · UI-DESIGN §8 requires status double-encoding, and three pairs of phases share a status colour (`alert`/`thinking`, `ghost`/`archived`, `blocked`/`stopped`). M3 is also what makes `waiting`, `blocked` and `looping` reachable at all. The census exists because a `<canvas>` is opaque to a screen reader: without it, §8's double encoding stops at the glyph.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6) An agent seated past the drawn desks is reported as "sharing a desk" rather than silently stacked · a hire is never refused a place, and an overlap the Architect has to notice by eye is the kind of degradation invariant §7 exists to forbid.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.6, bug fix) The tile sheet is decoded through the DOM (`Image.decode()`), not through Pixi's asset resolver · the resolver picks a parser from the URL's *extension*, and the bundler inlines a small sheet as a `data:` URL, which has none — so an installed pack fell back to procedural with a loader error. Found by installing a pack in a live run rather than by review. Nothing about a tileset should depend on the shape of the URL it arrived on; the texture is built with `scaleMode: 'nearest'`, since §7's integer upscale is only integer if nothing interpolates it.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) `artemis.ts` holds lifecycle and nothing else — hire, seat, policy text, respawn, and one question ("may she settle this?") answered from a table the Architect wrote · FR-5.1 is the constraint the module is written against. If a rule about how work is decomposed, who gets which task, or what counts as critical ever appears in this file, the mechanism/intelligence split ADR-0005 rests on has been lost. Asserted by a test that grants everything and shows the harness allows everything: it has no opinion of its own to override the Architect's with.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) Her system prompt reaches her through a general `roleBrief` seam on `AgentManager`, not a special case for the orchestrator · the manager renders the identity, appends whatever the hirer supplied, and writes the file — it never reads the text. A hire template's role brief uses the same seam in M4.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) A respawn mints a FRESH hook token · `spawnConfig` already said a token is "minted per spawn and never reused: a token that outlived its process would let a dead agent keep writing the event plane". A respawn is a new process, so it gets a new token; asserted rather than assumed.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) The respawn ladder resets only after a **stability window** (60 s default), not on every successful start · the first draft reset the counter whenever the agent reached `running`, which meant the ladder could never be spent by the one failure it exists to bound — a process that starts and immediately dies would be respawned forever. Recovery is coming back and *staying* back. **Found by a test, not by review**: the give-up case failed with five spawns where three were expected.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) Giving up is loud: a degradation, an `orchestrator` log line, and `orchestratorId` cleared · a company with no orchestrator is exactly the state the Architect must be told about (invariant §7), and a roster that still names a dead agent as orchestrator is worse than one that names none.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) `log` gains the kind `orchestrator` (SDD §4.3 updated) · FR-5.5 requires everything Artemis decides under delegated authority to be auditable afterwards, and no existing kind carried a countersignature, an escalation, or a respawn-ladder rung. The countersign *surface* — filing into the memo/gate archive — is still the Odeon's (M5).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) The delegated-authority table lives at `<home>/authority.json`, beside `gate-policy.json` (SDD §2 updated) · same posture as the gate policy: absent or unreadable ⇒ **nothing** delegated, everything escalates. A file the harness cannot read must never be the reason authority widened. Re-read per decision rather than cached, because the Architect edits it while the company runs; the parse failure is reported once per distinct reason so one bad file plus a busy company cannot flood the health buffer.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) The permission and the countersignature are the same call · `mayDecide` returns the countersignature *with* the allowance, so there is no code path that takes a decision under delegated authority without leaving the Architect something to audit (FR-5.5).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) A grant with no domains is a parse error, not "all domains" · FR-5.5's authority is per domain; a rule that forgot its domains should grant nothing rather than everything. Likewise a `spend` grant with no `maxSpendTokens` is refused by the schema, and refused again by `mayDecide` if one is constructed anyway — absent is never unlimited.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) A spend decision that names no amount is refused · "how much?" unanswered is not a small spend; it is an unknown one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) `mayDecide` has no production caller yet; M3.8's routing is the first · the plan assigns the countersign surface to the Odeon and Artemis's proxy role to M3.8, so what M3.7 owes is the table, its validator and the enforcement hook every future caller goes through. Recorded rather than hidden, on the same footing as M3.5's `forceEvaluate`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.7) `claude --resume <sessionId>` closes the M1-audit gap · `ResumeSupport` was in the adapter type from M1 with no adapter implementing it and nothing calling it, so a crashed agent came back with no idea what it had been doing. The session id comes off the event plane exactly as the interface's doc comment always said it would, and the log records whether memory actually carried over — an engine with no resume still respawns, with a fresh session, and says so rather than implying a continuity that is not there.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) The ledger endpoint is addressed as a **reserved agent id** (`agent.ledger`), not as a new `to` value · SDD §7.1 says Artemis writes the ledger via "files in its own outbox as `propose` to harness ledger endpoint" but names no address, and §4.4's `to` domain is `agentId | "broadcast" | "human"`. Adding a fourth literal would be a deviation from a normative schema (a §8 must-ask); a reserved agent id uses the first branch exactly as documented. Surfaced in the session report so the Architect can promote it to a `"ledger"` literal in §4.4 if they would rather.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) `agent.hermes` and `agent.ledger` are reserved: `spawnRequestSchema` refuses them · otherwise a hire could take `agent.hermes` and forge a router refusal in the harness's name.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8, closes a recorded gap) The router-authored bounce is now `from: agent.hermes` · the M2 close-out recorded that it claimed `from: <the original sender>` — a message the sender never wrote, attributed to them — because §4.4 gave the harness no legal identity, and flagged it for M3 "alongside Artemis's proxy role". Reserved agent ids give the harness a legal `from` with no schema change.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) The writer check lives in `routeMessage`, not in the endpoint · ADR-0003 calls the addressing rules "transport rules, not etiquette". By the time a message reaches the endpoint the router has already established that the orchestrator sent it, and "agents never touch tasks.json" is enforced on the only way in.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) A proposal applies whole or not at all, and every reason comes back at once · a half-applied decomposition is a ledger nobody can reason about, and a refusal Artemis can act on in one pass beats one that reveals the next problem only after she fixes the first.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) `→ done` is judged against the task AS IT WILL BE · a proposal that adds a result ref and closes in the same op satisfies SDD §4.2's rule; judging the opening state would refuse it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) A create with an unreachable assignee is refused · a task addressed to an agent with no mailbox is work that will never be asked for, and the ledger would claim it was assigned.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) Dependencies are checked once, after every op · a proposal may legitimately create a task and its dependant in the same breath.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) `board.md` travels through the same endpoint as the ledger · FR-4.2's "single scribe = Artemis" is only enforceable if the write goes through the harness; a file agents could write directly would make the rule a comment. The board is seeded from `prompts/agora/board.md` so the tab has something honest to show.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) `pendingTasksFor` counts `todo` and `in_progress` only · ADR-0013's branch asks "should this agent keep going?", and an agent whose only tasks are `blocked` or `stalled` should stop, not be told to continue. `review` is owed to the Odeon, not to the assignee.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) Task ids are `t-<date>-<random>`, not a counter · two proposals in flight would otherwise mint the same id, and a counter read from the ledger is a read-modify-write nobody holds a lock on.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8) The kanban cannot write · tasks are Artemis's to propose and the endpoint's to apply (SDD §7.1); a board the Architect could drag cards on would be a second writer to a file ADR-0004 gives exactly one. The panel re-reads on a `state:tasks` nudge rather than editing its own copy.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.8, bug fix) The Ledger panel carries `minWidth: 0` · a flex child defaults to `min-width: auto` ("never shrink below my content"), so six kanban columns pushed the terminal pane to a sliver and gave the whole window a horizontal scrollbar. Found by reading the evidence screenshot; the columns now scroll inside the panel.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3.9) The fake engine gains a `write-transcript` step · S-LEDGER must fold a transcript an *engine* wrote, in the engine's own format and place, not one the test hand-placed — otherwise the suite proves the reader and not the pipeline. The fake's format is the one `makeFakeAdapter`'s `TranscriptReader` already reads.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.9) A company mints a FRESH session id per hire; passing one models `--resume` · a restart without engine-native resume gets a new engine session, so "this session's spend" restarts at zero while the day's does not. S-LEDGER asserts both cases, because M3.7 made the resumed one reachable.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.9) S-LEDGER's durable plane is the `LedgerStore` seam, not SQLite · `better-sqlite3` is Electron-ABI and cannot load under the Node runner (BUILD-PROMPT §10). What the seam cannot prove is SQLite's own durability; what it does prove is the thing that actually regressed upstream — the harness keeping its own copy — and there is a dedicated test that counts store reads to pin it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.9) S-SECRETS injects a grant into the CHILD's spawn environment, never into the harness's own · the first draft set `process.env` before spawning and the invariant tripwire caught it: reading a credential from `process.env` outside the broker is exactly what ADR-0010 forbids. `runTurnIn` takes an `env` map, the way a spawn plan carries one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3.9) The exit demos run REAL processes with SCRIPTED words · the `claude` binaries in this environment are unauthenticated and sit at a login prompt, so what they would *say* cannot come from them. Every process, socket, router, endpoint, gate, committer and IPC in the demos is the real one; the agents' messages are written into their real outboxes and routed exactly as composed ones would be. Said plainly rather than implied, because a demo that overstates what it proved is worse than one that proves less.

- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close) `main` was fast-forwarded to the M3.9 tip and pushed directly, on the Architect's explicit instruction · the nine package branches form one linear chain, so the merge is a fast-forward with no merge commit and no conflicts, and all twelve `feature/*`+`fix/*` branches are contained in the tip. CI run 33068981836 is green on `main` at `0652cf6`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close, finding) ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §2's "`main` is protected: PRs only" is a policy with no mechanical backing · this run treated it as binding and never pushed to `main` until instructed; the direct push then succeeded, which means no GitHub branch-protection rule enforces the sentence. Recorded rather than quietly relied on: a rule nothing enforces is exactly the class this project refuses elsewhere. Either the protection rule gets turned on or §2 gets softened to say what is true — an Architect call, not an implementer's.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 close) The `agent.ledger` reserved-id addressing is **RATIFIED** as the standing rule for harness-owned endpoints · SDD §4.4's `to` domain stays untouched; a harness endpoint is addressed as a reserved agent id the roster refuses to hire, exactly as the bounce-`from` gap was closed. Promoting a `"ledger"` literal was offered and declined.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 close) CI runs on `feature/**` and `fix/**` pushes from M4 on · the M3 run's "CI green" was the job set run locally because the workflow only fired on main; per-package green should be a real run. Costs Actions minutes; the Architect accepted the trade.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 close) The two M4 engines are **codex + gemini** · IMPLEMENTATION M4 offers codex/gemini/opencode, pick two; the two most-used CLIs after claude give the conformance suite the most real-world coverage. Grades stay honest: whatever each demonstrates is what it declares.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M3 close-out audit) The agent↔task binding join is **carried to M5** · nothing binds a live spawn to a ledger task, so gate→`task.gates` population and rung-3 "task returns `stalled` with the breaker report" (ADR-0011) are unreachable in production; both become load-bearing with the Odeon's task-close gates, which is where the join lands. The M3.8 record's claim was corrected.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit, Architect verdict) Breaker rung 2 now lowers the constrained agent's budget: `CONSTRAINED_BUDGET_FACTOR = 0.5` · ADR-0011 names three constrain effects; mail-pause alone had shipped. The factor applies in the budget watcher's agent view and lifts with the rung — the append-only ledger is never touched. Read-only tools remain excused ("where the engine supports it").
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) Trip signal #3 rides a new `onDiverted` callback, fired once per diverted message · the old wiring sniffed `onBounced` for "hop", but a hop cap is a divert and no bounce reason says "hop" — `noteHopCap` was unreachable. Proven by a divert-not-bounce regression test at the Hermes boundary.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) Broadcast delivery is per-recipient under a rung-2 pause · one paused recipient used to hold every copy, and each sweep re-delivered the served recipients — duplicate `delivery`/`delivery-held` log entries drumming until recovery. Served copies are now single-shot (inbox/.done existence check), the hold logs once, and the outbox drains when the last recipient is served.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) `boot()` is a named function with a `.catch` that shows an error box and quits · `void app.whenReady().then(async …)` wrapped the entire boot (including the native SQLite open) with no rejection handler — the M2 fire-and-forget class, at the one place a rejection means "no app at all". A dead boot must say so, not hang silently.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) The ledger endpoint's `agree`/`refuse` replies render from `prompts/hermes/ledger-*.md` · they are read by Artemis, so their framing is a prompt surface (invariant §8); the refusal reasons stay data in the `{{reasons}}` slot.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) `to:"human"` from the orchestrator routes to `agora/human/`, never back to her own inbox · the proxy cannot proxy for herself; her escalations were self-delivering, which would swallow exactly the messages FR-3.7 exists to surface.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) The notification-hook degradation reports once per blocked episode · engines repeat `notification` while one dialog stands, and each repeat consumed a slot in the 50-entry health buffer. The gate itself already coalesced; now the report does too.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) `packaging-spend.md` states the real verdict mechanics · it promised "approving raises the ceiling for today only", which nothing implements; UC-08 packaging must be honest, so the prompt now says what actually happens (deny refuses the request; approve proceeds; ceiling unchanged).
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) `prompts/watch/gate-{held,approved,denied}.md` removed · loaded by nothing — the agent-facing hold/verdict notification they were written for has no code path yet (the orphaned-prompt class the M3.3 review itself named). They return with the path that loads them.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit) `watch:dismiss` added to SDD §5 · it had widened the documented IPC surface without the doc line + log entry `watch:humanQueue` got; by the repo's own M3.1 rule that pair is mandatory.
- 2026-08-27 · (M3 close-out audit, recorded not fixed) SDD §5's `breaker:trip` push stays unimplemented (the Watch panel polls at 3 s) · owed to a later Watch pass; the panel is correct, just not push-driven. Also recorded: pty-heuristic reduced protection surfaces in the Watch panel rather than on the agent card (placement), and the real `AppDb` remains covered by live runs only (Electron-ABI under vitest).

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) The memory layer reaches the adapter as **text on the spawn config**, not as a path · `AgentSpawnConfig.memory` is composed and budgeted in `library.ts` and handed over exactly the way `envGrants` are resolved in main and handed over (ADR-0010's pattern). A `memoryPath` would have put "how much of a long memory fits" inside whichever adapter happened to run — an engine-specific answer to a Library question (NFR-12).
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) The injection budget is 8 000 characters of whole sections, newest first · a budget has to exist (`memory.md` grows without bound until reflection lands in M4.4) and half a learning is worse than none, because the agent cannot tell it is reading half. When it bites, the agent is told so from `prompts/library/memory-elided.md` — a silently truncated memory would leave it confidently wrong about what it knows (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) The seed preamble is never injected and never counted · found by a test: with the preamble counted, a hire that had remembered nothing still reported `memoryCarried: true`. That log fact is the M3.7 carried item and has to be true, so "nothing written yet" comes back as nothing. The preamble also duplicates PROTOCOL.md's "How you remember", which the agent already has.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) SDD §10's crash **note** goes to `log.jsonl`, not into the task · `taskSchema` is `.strict()` with no notes field, and widening a normative schema to record a crash would be a §8 deviation. The book of record is where every other harness fact about an agent already lives, and NFR-13 asks for exactly this. Same reasoning as the `agent.ledger` reserved-id call the Architect ratified.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) Only `in_progress` tasks come back to `todo`, and they keep their assignee · a `blocked` task is waiting on something the crash did not change and `review` is owed to the Odeon, so neither is the dead agent's to return; and unassigning would lose the fact that this agent is the one who knows about the task, while the same row offers its respawn.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) A deliberate kill takes the same crash path as a SIGKILL · nothing distinguishes them at the pty seam, both leave assigned work unworked, and `exitCode` is recorded so a reader can tell them apart without the harness guessing.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) `ghost ──30s──► archived` is driven by the avatar clock, mirrored into the roster · SDD §6 already owns the timer; a second timer in the lifecycle would be two planes that can disagree about when an agent stopped being a ghost. `AgentManager.archive()` is the mirror, called from the avatar director's `onChange`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) S-CRASH runs real child processes through a `ProcessSpawner` test fake · `PtyManager` cannot load under vitest (Electron ABI, BUILD-PROMPT §10.3), and the one thing this scenario cannot fake is a process that can really be SIGKILLed. Same seam, `child_process` behind it; every other part — Agora, git, hook socket, `AgentManager`, `LedgerEndpoint`, `Library`, avatar clock — is the shipped one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.1) The fake engine gains an `append-memory` step and honours `--resume <id>` · a recall test is only worth something if an *agent* wrote the memory, in its own directory, as prose with no schema (ADR-0006); and honouring `--resume` makes "the resumed process reports the same session id" an observable fact rather than an argv assertion.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) Recall answers on the **hook socket**, at `POST /recall` · it needs exactly what that server already owns — the one 0600 socket file and the per-spawn token registry (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §5). A second socket would be a second thing to secure, a second stale file to clean after a crash, and no capability the first did not already have. The plan's phrase for this is "the eph-hook single-client discipline".
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) `eph-recall` does **not** fail open, unlike `eph-hook` · a hook that cannot be delivered costs the agent nothing, so the hook shim swallows it. A recall that printed nothing when the harness was down would have the agent conclude the company knows nothing and act on it — the silent fallback invariant §7 names as the one unforgivable mode. Every failure prints `recall unavailable: <reason>` and exits 1, and PROTOCOL.md tells agents the difference between that and "nothing matched".
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) The keyword index is `index/fts.sqlite`, **not** a table in `db.sqlite` · SDD §10 repairs a corrupt recall index by deleting and rebuilding it, and `db.sqlite` holds the append-only cost ledger. Derived state a repair may delete does not share a file with durable state that must never be deleted.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) The FTS rung is split at an `FtsStore` seam · everything that decides behaviour (the mtime gate, passage splitting, scope filtering, how a row becomes a hit) is ordinary TypeScript in `library-fts.ts`; only storage is SQLite, in a module `index.ts` alone imports. Same split as `CostLedger`/`LedgerStore` and the same honest limit: the seam cannot prove SQLite's own behaviour, so the real FTS5 path is proven by live run (recorded in PROGRESS) and the ladder — the Architect's named tested surface — is proven by the suite.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) `MemoryFtsStore` scores with the same `scorePassage` the grep rung uses · so "the same known-answer query, green at every available rung" holds by construction rather than by coincidence. `recallPassages` is exported and shared for the same reason: two rungs that disagreed about what a passage is would be answering different questions.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) The mtime gate compares mtime **and** size · an edit that preserves the timestamp is rare, a truncation that preserves both is rarer still, and the pair costs nothing.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) The grep rung ranks distinct terms above repetition (`distinct × 10 + min(occurrences, 20)`) · a passage mentioning every term once beats one repeating a single term twenty times, which is the difference between an answer and a keyword pile. No stemming and no stopword list: both are language-specific guesses, and this rung's promise is that you can predict what it will find.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) `EPH_RECALL` is composed in `AgentManager`, not in each adapter's deps · unlike `hookShimPath` it is identical for every engine, so adapters only forward it, and an adapter that forgets is caught by a conformance case. An empty command is *omitted* rather than exported empty — `$EPH_RECALL q` would otherwise run `q`.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.2) FTS5 terms are quoted and OR-ed · a query containing FTS5 syntax (`AND`, `*`, `"`) must be searched for, not executed; OR with bm25 ranking gives the same "more distinct terms ranks higher" shape the grep rung scores by.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) The driver is written against MemPalace **3.8.0's real CLI**, verified by running it · `--palace` is a global flag that must precede the subcommand; `mine <dir> --wing <w> --agent <a>`; `search <q> [--wing w] [--results n]`. `search` has no machine-readable mode, so the human output is parsed — tolerantly, and an unrecognized shape returns null (degrade the rung) rather than an empty list (answer "nothing known"). That distinction is the whole point: the two are different facts.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) `MEMPALACE_HOOKS_AUTO_SAVE=0` and `MEMPALACE_HOOKS_DAEMON=0` are set on **every** invocation, not once in a config file · ADR-0016's consequence is that the archive has one writer path, and a rule that lives in a file the Architect may edit is a rule with no mechanical backing — the class this project refuses elsewhere. `--daemon`/`--background` are never passed and `mempalace hook` is never installed into any engine's settings; three tests hold all of it.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) `RecallIndex.sync`/`search` became **async** · a rung may now be a local subprocess, and a semantic search takes seconds. Doing it synchronously would freeze the Electron main process and with it every agent's terminal. `Library.recall`/`reindex` follow; the `/recall` route already awaited.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) The driver keeps its **own** mtime gate on top of MemPalace's file-level dedup · MemPalace skips already-filed files itself, but a `mine` still costs a process start and an embedding pass, so a wing whose documents are all unchanged is not invoked at all. Proven by counting invocations of the fake CLI, not by trusting the report.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) A hit's `ref` is rebuilt from the wing's directory · MemPalace reports `Source: memory.md`, a basename; the driver knows which directory it mined into each wing, so the Architect gets a path they can open instead of a name they cannot resolve.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) `config.json` grows an **optional** `mempalaceCommand` · MemPalace is commonly installed into a virtualenv or with pipx, and "must be on PATH as `mempalace`" would make the top rung unreachable for a normal install. Optional, so an existing config stays valid with no migration.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3, found by the live run) `snippetOf` now opens the window where the most **distinct** terms are, not at the first match · in "why is checkout unreliable", `is` matches in the second line of every document, so every snippet opened on boilerplate while the sentence that answered sat outside the window. Deterministic, earliest-wins on ties; it improves every rung.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3, recorded limit) MemPalace files whole documents as drawers · so the top rung's granularity is a file where the keyword rungs return a passage, and a memory drawer carries the seed preamble with it. The snippet is bounded and windowed, which contains the effect; changing what is mined would mean not writing the preamble agents read, which is worse.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.3) ADR-0016 §2's "one wing per target/project" is **not** implemented · the wings here are one per agent plus one for the knowledge shelf, all Agora content. Target repos are the agents' working directories and nothing in M4 mines them. Recorded so the gap is visible rather than looking like an oversight.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) Reflection asks the agent; the harness never summarizes · ADR-0005 rejects "the harness calls a model API directly" outright, and condensing a memory is judgement, not mechanism. The job notices a memory is over threshold, delivers a harness prompt as a normal `request`, and applies whatever the agent proposes back to `agent.library`. The module has no opinion about what a good condensation says.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) `agent.library` is the reflection endpoint, on the ratified reserved-id rule · same standing rule the Architect ratified for `agent.ledger` at the M3 close, so SDD §4.4's `to` domain is untouched. Unlike the ledger it has **no writer check**: any agent may write there, because the endpoint condenses `message.from`'s memory and nobody else's — an agent writing to it can only ever act on itself.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) `Library.condense()` is the one method allowed to rewrite `memory.md` · everything else appends. It is allowed only because nothing is lost: the archive is written FIRST (a crash between the two writes duplicates a section, which is recoverable; the other order loses one), and `nothingDestroyed` verifies every old section survives byte-for-byte in the new memory or the archive before the rewrite is committed. A failed check writes nothing and throws.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) `nothingDestroyed` does **not** count the condensed core as containing what it summarizes · a summary is not the thing it summarizes, and treating it as one is exactly how "nothing is destroyed" quietly stops being true.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) Reflection needs **no new `log.jsonl` kind** · SDD §4.3's kind list is closed and adding one would be a §8 deviation. It does not need to: the request, the agent's proposal and the endpoint's answer are all *messages*, so `Hermes.deliverFromHarness` (new, and now also used by the ledger endpoint's replies) logs the same `delivery` entries the router logs. The trail — who was asked, what they answered, how many sections went to which archive file — is reconstructible from `log.jsonl` alone (NFR-13). The accept subject carries the count and the file name precisely so the log entry does.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) Threshold 24 000 chars, keep the newest 5 sections · three times the injection budget: reflection should fire because memory has genuinely outgrown a session's context, not the moment the budget first elides anything. A long memory in few sections declines *visibly* — one enormous entry cannot be condensed by moving sections, and archiving the whole file would leave nothing behind.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) An unanswered request is re-asked after 6 h, and an agent that leaves is reported · deferred, visibly, never dropped. Asking twice is harmless: the endpoint applies whichever answer arrives against the plan as it stands then, and refuses one for a memory no longer due.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.4) The scheduler stamps a trigger's clock **before** the run · a job that takes longer than its interval would otherwise be instantly due again on the tick it finished. With re-entrancy already blocked, this is what keeps a slow job on its interval instead of back-to-back.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.5) Four IPC channels join the surface, with their SDD §5 line in the same commit · `agora:memory` is SDD §5's documented `memory(id)`; `agora:recall`, `agora:knowledge` and `agora:register-knowledge` widen it. The M3 close-out set the rule for exactly this (`watch:dismiss` "had widened the documented IPC surface without the doc line + log entry… by the repo's own M3.1 rule that pair is mandatory"), so both are here. Surfaced in the session report so the Architect can object.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.5) A shelf document's name is checked **twice** — in the IPC schema and again in `library.knowledgePath` · the renderer is untrusted (invariant §2), and a traversal that only the schema refused would be one refactor away from being possible again. The Library's check is the one that guards the write.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.5) The Library writes the shelf file; `index.ts` commits it · ADR-0004 gives the Agora exactly one committer, and `library.ts` runs no git. The commit is queued (`commitSoon`), never awaited — the panel must not wait on git.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.5) The rung chip is coloured by rung · `mempalace` in status-success, `fts` in status-working, `grep` in status-looping. A chip that looked the same on every rung would be a degradation nobody notices, which is the failure invariant §7 names.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.5, environment) The Memory panel's **screenshot is owed to a local session** · `electron-rebuild` cannot complete here: this environment's proxy answers 403 to `www.electronjs.org`, so node-gyp cannot fetch Electron headers, `better-sqlite3` stays Node-ABI and `npm run dev` cannot boot. Everything provable without Electron was proven by live run of the shipped IPC deps; the pixels are recorded as owed, never faked — the same discipline the Architect's MemPalace directive prescribes.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.6) The codex adapter was written against a **real `codex-cli 0.150.1`**, installed and run in this session · every flag it uses (`--cd`, the positional `[PROMPT]`), its version line (`codex-cli 0.150.1`) and the existence of `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` were checked by running the binary, not inferred from how another engine works.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.6) Codex ships at **`pty-heuristic`**, writing no settings at all · it has a hook plane, but Codex refuses to run hooks without persisted trust and the only override is `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` — the harness lowering a permission default on the Architect's behalf is exactly what the Watch exists to prevent and a §8 must-ask, not an implementation detail. The hook file's schema also could not be confirmed against the real CLI. Declared = demonstrated, so: no events claimed, nothing written into anyone's repo, and the card says `pty-heuristic`. Raising the grade owes a live demonstration first.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.6) Identity reaches codex as the **first prompt** · SDD §3 names it as one of the three sanctioned mechanisms, and it is the only one available: codex has no `--append-system-prompt`, and its context file (`AGENTS.md`) is not a local/gitignored variant, which ADR-0009 forbids writing.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.6) Codex declares **no transcript reader** · it records sessions under `$CODEX_HOME`, but this environment has no codex credentials, so the on-disk format is unverified. A reader written against a guessed format would fold invented numbers into the append-only cost ledger — the one thing ADR-0011 must never do. Spend for a codex agent reports source `none`, visibly.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.6) The conformance table's two *installer* hygiene cases are now conditional on the adapter declaring settings · an adapter may legitimately install nothing, and the table could not express that tier — it crashed on `settingsRel[0]` being undefined. A new assertion holds the other half: an adapter that declares no settings must actually write none. This is the containment wall growing to fit a grade ADR-0009 already defines, not a core change.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.7) The gemini adapter was written against a **real `gemini` 0.57.0** and its own bundled hook docs · the version line, the positional `[query..]`, `--skip-trust`, `--resume`'s argument domain and the `gemini hooks` subcommand were all read off the running CLI, not inferred.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.7) Gemini ships at **`pty-heuristic`**, for a different documented reason than codex's · its hook plane is the best documented in the roster after Claude Code's, but its project settings file `.gemini/settings.json` is **tracked** (its own docs `git add` it) and it has no project-local variant — ADR-0009 permits writing only local/gitignored variants — and project hooks are untrusted by default with `--skip-trust` as the sole override. Both are §8 must-asks. The wiring is a small change away once the Architect rules; the grade moves only with a live demonstration.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.7) Gemini declares **no `resume`** even though `-r/--resume` is an appendable flag · its argument domain is `"latest"` or an index, never a session id, and `ResumeSupport.resumeArgs` is handed the id the event plane recorded. `--resume latest` would reopen whatever ran most recently in that directory — for two agents sharing a repo, exactly the cross-attribution bug M3 spent a package removing from the cost ledger.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.7) `-p/--prompt` is never used · it runs gemini headless and exits, and an agent whose terminal is not interactive cannot be steered (FR-1.3). A test pins it.

- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) Worktree management lives in `git.ts` · ADR-0004 gives the app exactly one git path and "only main runs git" is only checkable while every invocation is in that file. `AgentManager` gets a four-method seam (`pathFor`/`branchFor`/`create`/`remove`) and cannot become the place a second git path grows; the invariant tripwire still passes unchanged.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) `Worktrees` refuses the Agora itself **and anything under it** · the guard is a resolved-path prefix check, not a string compare, and it is enforced in the class rather than at the call site — a rule the caller could forget is not a rule.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) `--force` never appears · a worktree with uncommitted work is kept, the porcelain lines come back as evidence, and the refusal is *reported* through `onExitError`, not merely logged. An agent that died with unpushed work leaves that work for the Architect; tidiness does not outrank somebody's afternoon.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) An unreadable worktree counts as **not clean** · refusing to remove something we cannot inspect is the safe direction.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) `create` reuses an existing branch instead of failing on it · a respawn must land where the dead process left off, and `branchFor` is stable per agent. The card records `branchCreated` so a reader can tell the two cases apart.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) The cwd is swapped **before** the version probe and before any settings are written · everything downstream reads the card's `cwd`, so grants, the settings install and the transcript directory all follow the worktree with no further plumbing. `LiveAgent` keeps `targetRepo` because removing a worktree is an operation on its repo, and by then the card no longer points at one.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4.8) A worktree that cannot be made is reported and the spawn continues in the target repo · isolation is a nicety; refusing to hire over it would be worse than saying so out loud.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M4 close) Codex stays `pty-heuristic` · the only path to codex hooks is `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`, and the harness lowering a trust default on the Architect's behalf is what the Watch exists to prevent. Hook wiring is owed to a local session where the Architect persists trust interactively and the hook schema can be confirmed against the real CLI.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M4 close) Gemini stays `pty-heuristic` · its hook wiring lives in a TRACKED settings file, and ADR-0009 permits adapters to write only local/gitignored variants — an adapter must never dirty a repo's tracked files. Revisit only if gemini ships an untracked variant.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M4 close) No-resume stands for codex and gemini · ADR-0009's append-only `ResumeSupport` cannot express a subcommand-first resume or an index-based one that would reopen whoever ran last (the cross-attribution class M3 removed). The plan-transform generalization is a recorded candidate for a future ADR-0009 annex — not to be invented mid-package.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M4 close) SDD §4.3 gains the `memory` log kind · added on the Architect's call so the Library's future events have a home; nothing emits it yet, and its first emitter documents its refs.
- 2026-08-27 · (Architect verdict, M4 close) The four Library IPC channels are RATIFIED after channel-by-channel review · `agora:memory`/`agora:recall`/`agora:knowledge` (reads) and `agora:register-knowledge` (name-sanitized, committer-routed write), all zod-validated in main.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4 close-out audit) The reflect-request subject and the library-unavailable reply render from `prompts/library/` · three LLM-facing literals had survived in main (invariant §8); Hermes's tests-only fallback went mechanical to match its own render() standard.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4 close-out audit) The recall smoke's reporting case is falsifiable both ways · `EPH_MEMPALACE` set-but-broken now FAILS the suite (a configured-but-dead rung is the invisible degradation invariant §7 forbids); unset asserts the absence. The old assertion was a tautology that could never fail.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4 close-out audit) A surviving worktree on the agent's own branch is REUSED at respawn · the dirty-unwind keep and the respawn's "already exists" refusal contradicted each other — the card named a worktree the spawn refused to enter. Anything at the path that is not the agent's own checkout still refuses. Two regression tests.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4 close-out audit) `postJson` in `shims/hook-client.mjs` is the ONE low-level socket transport; `eph-recall` delegates to it · the recall shim had grown a duplicate `http.request` implementation against M1.2's single-client rule. Hooks map the shared transport fail-open, recall maps it fail-closed — the contracts differ, the transport does not.
- 2026-08-27 · (M4 close-out audit, recorded not fixed) The codex commit also adjusted the shared conformance harness (+50/−18, disclosed in-commit) to admit a no-settings adapter · TEST-STRATEGY §5's "only its adapter" reading is noted; the change was necessary and reviewed. Also recorded: the declared-vs-demonstrated live check runs only against the fake engine, so codex/gemini honesty reduces to "declares pty-heuristic + provably writes nothing"; and the exit table's `attribution ok (59 commits)` was produced on pre-M4 main — corrected in place, the record-hygiene class the M3 audit named.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1) The agent↔task binding is **derived from `tasks.json`, never remembered** · an in-memory agent→task map would be empty after exactly the event that makes the binding matter most — a restart — and the ledger already records who owes what durably. `boundTaskFor` reads the book of record instead of keeping a second copy that could disagree with it, which is the reasoning invariant §11 applies to spend.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1) The binding ranks `in_progress` over `todo`, then lower priority, then id · status must outrank priority or a gate would attach to work the agent has not started; the id tie-break makes the answer STABLE under file reordering, because an unstable binding would open a gate against one task and settle it against another. Asserted by a table that reorders the same two tasks.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1) The breaker report goes to `log.jsonl`, not into the task · ADR-0011 says the task returns "with the breaker report attached", but `taskSchema` is strict and has no notes field, and widening a normative schema would be a §8 deviation. `stallTask` moves the status and the `task`/`stalled` event carries `because`, `rung`, `signals` and `detail` — the same split `returnTasksOf` already makes for SDD §10's crash note, and what NFR-13 asks for.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1) `BreakerEffects.returnTask` is **required, not optional** · rung 3 always owes the clause, and an optional effect would let a construction site silently drop it — exactly how the clause went unimplemented from M3 to M5. Four construction sites were updated rather than defaulted.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1) `test/scenarios/company.ts` now runs the SHIPPED `LedgerEndpoint` and wires `taskOf`/`returnTask` · the rig already carried the "SHIPPED choke-point wiring, not a copy of it" rule after review found a duplicated `index.ts`; the join gets the same treatment, so the scenarios exercise it rather than describing it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1, environment) `core.autocrlf` is set **false** in this clone · with it true the fast-forward materialized every touched file with CRLF, and `prettier --check` (default `endOfLine: lf`) then failed on 135 files — a red baseline caused entirely by local git config, with CI green on the same commit. The working tree was re-materialized with `git checkout-index -a -f`; zero content changed. A repo-level `.gitattributes` with `* text=auto eol=lf` would prevent the next Windows clone from hitting it — recorded as a candidate, not taken, because it is outside this package.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.1, recorded not fixed) `src/shared/breaker.ts` contains two **literal NUL bytes** — the span key separator is written as a raw `\u0000` character rather than the escape — so git classifies the file as binary: `git diff` shows only `Bin 9634 -> 9944 bytes`, and `grep` skips it without `-a`. A Watch-critical file is therefore unreviewable by diff. Pre-existing (present in `HEAD` before this package) and out of M5.1's scope; the fix is a one-character-class change with identical semantics. Flagged for the Architect.

- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-002) Rung-1 steer rides the **existing decision channel on `post-tool`**, not `additionalContext` · the proposal named the upstream mechanism; the shim already relays `{decision, reason}` verbatim for every event and the engine's documented post-tool semantics for `block` are "prompt the model with the reason" — the same hook-boundary latency with zero shim/adapter changes, where `additionalContext` would have needed engine-specific event names pushed into shim args. Mechanism recorded as a deviation inside the approved scope; the merge-risk the proposal flagged vanishes with it (steer and Stop answer different events).
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-002) One pending note per agent, latest wins, delivered exactly once, cleared on `session-start` · a second trip's sentence must not queue behind a stale one, and a successor session must never be steered by a sentence aimed at its dead predecessor. All four rules unit-tested.
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-002) Channel visibility is a `breaker`/`steer-channel` log event rather than a new agent-card field · the card already shows the hook grade, which *is* the channel selector; the log carries which channel each sentence actually took (NFR-13). The card field can ride a later UI pass if the Architect wants it at a glance.
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-002, environment) `test/main/agent-worktree.test.ts` (4) and `test/scenarios/s-crash.test.ts` (3) fail **at HEAD** in this worktree — spawn-phase timeouts ("timed out waiting for the agent to start", card stuck `installing`) in the AgentManager probe path, verified pre-existing by running both suites with the GYM-002 diff stashed. Not touched by this package; CI (ubuntu) is the gate that counts. Flagged for the Architect: likely this machine's state (OneDrive worktree path), worth a look before the next milestone close.

- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-003) Acks travel as an **endpoint hand-off to `agent.closing`**, a fourth reserved id · the alternative was watching inbox traffic or magic subjects on ordinary mail; the M3 standing rule for harness-owned correspondents already exists and the routing table already knows how to guard an endpoint (`inform`/`done` only — an ACK never asks). `reserved.ts` refuses the id at spawn, so no hire can forge a closing acknowledgment.
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-003) The harness, not Artemis, runs the protocol · upstream mails its orchestrator a shutdown brief and lets it broadcast; here Artemis may be among the workers being closed, and a protocol that depends on one agent's liveness to save every other agent's memory has its dependency backwards. `ClosingTime` mails every live agent directly via `deliverFromHarness` and watches the endpoint.
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-003) An ack is `in_reply_to` the request **or** the exact `CLOSING-TIME-ACK` subject · engines routinely compose fresh messages instead of threading replies; either signal is unforgeable enough (the reply routes through the sender's own outbox, which Hermes already authenticates against the directory owner). A reply that is neither is consumed and logged `closing-unrecognized`, never counted — and an ack with no closing in flight bounces with its reason (FR-3.4).
- 2026-08-28 · (GYM-003) The deadline lives in `ClosingTime`, defaulting 90 s, and the dialog is `showMessageBoxSync` in main · closing time may slow a quit, never hang it; the dialog is UI chrome (invariant §8 governs LLM-facing prose, not window copy). The live-quit evidence run owed by the success metric is booked with the metric check (2026-09-11), not skipped.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) Odeon artifacts are filed as `propose` messages to a reserved **`agent.odeon`** endpoint · SDD §2 gives `odeon/` to the harness alone, so an agent files the only way it can say anything — from its own outbox — and the harness archives. That single rule is what makes the archive trustworthy: a deck cannot be back-dated, edited in place, or written for a task its author was never given. Same shape as `agent.ledger` and `agent.library`, the standing pattern the Architect ratified at the M3 close.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) **The agent supplies deck CONTENT; the harness applies the TEMPLATE** · FR-7.2 requires "an HTML slide deck from the standard template", and the only way "from the standard template" is enforced rather than hoped for is for the harness to be the one that applies it. It also keeps the template in `prompts/odeon/deck.html` where invariant §8 puts every other word, and keeps `odeon/` harness-written. The agent files the six named sections as structured JSON; a missing or empty section is refused, because a deck that quietly omitted "trade-offs" would satisfy the close gate while defeating the reason the gate exists.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) Every section is **HTML-escaped** into the template · a deck is agent-authored text the Architect opens in a webview, so a failing test's output containing `<script>` must render, not execute. The template is the only HTML; everything filled into it is text.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) Any agent may file at the Odeon, but only for **its own assigned task, and only when that task carries the obligation** · the router stays on transport rules (ADR-0003) so the authorship check lives in the endpoint, which reads the ledger. Filing for a task that never asked for a deck is refused, or an agent could manufacture an artifact the close guard then treats as an obligation met.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) The archive is append-only by NAMING, not by permission · `odeon/decks/<taskId>-<ts>.html` gives a revised deck its own file, so the version the Architect already read is never destroyed (invariant §5). `artifacts.deck` names the current one; the older ones stay on the shelf. Nothing in `Odeon` ever opens an existing artifact for writing, and a name collision is refused rather than overwritten.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) `odeon:deck(ref)` is a channel SDD §5's abridged list does not name · the viewer must read one artifact's HTML, and `decks()` returning every deck's body would make the list a copy of the archive. Added with the ref validated in main and resolved only to a well-formed deck name inside the archive directory — a renderer-supplied path never escapes it. Recorded here with SDD §5 updated to name it, the same way `watch:dismiss` was handled at the M3 close.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) An Architect review comment becomes **mail to the orchestrator, never a task** · UC-05 step 4 says comments "become follow-up tasks", but FR-5.2 gives the ledger to Artemis and ADR-0005 keeps the harness out of deciding what work the company does. The comment is delivered to her as a `request` carrying the deck ref; what task it becomes is her judgment, and `prompts/odeon/deck-comment.md` tells her not to invent one to look responsive. A comment with no orchestrator to receive it reports that rather than vanishing.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) The relayed comment is signed **`from: agent.odeon`**, not `from: human` · SDD §4.4 puts `human` in the ADDRESS domain only; `from` must be an agent id, and `messageSchema` enforces it. The Odeon relays and signs as itself — the same reserved-identity answer M3 gave the router's bounce, which had claimed `from: <the original sender>` for a message the sender never wrote. The template names the Architect as the author.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2) The deck viewer renders in a **sandboxed iframe with no `allow-scripts`** · the archive escapes agent content on the way in; refusing to execute it on the way out is the second wall, and the two are independent.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.2, environment) eslint now ignores `.claude/**` · a `.claude/worktrees/` scratch checkout of this same repo (git-ignored via `.git/info/exclude`, created by other tooling mid-session) put a second tsconfig root under the project and made every file fail to parse — 383 errors with nothing wrong in `src/`. Prettier already ignored `.claude/`; this makes eslint agree, so the gate reports on this checkout rather than on transient agent scratch space.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) The memo hold IS a Watch gate, not a second holding mechanism · SDD §7.3 draws the flow as "Watch gate policy match → action held", so a matching action opens an ordinary gate carrying `memoTrigger`, and the memo's verdict settles that gate. One hold mechanism, one queue, one release path — a parallel memo-hold would have needed its own crash recovery, its own UI and its own reconciliation.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) A memo trigger borrows an existing gate KIND rather than inventing a seventh · `GATE_KINDS` is a documented closed set: new-dependency and api-or-schema-change ride `scope-change`, security-posture rides `prod-facing` (it is the kind whose blast radius the Architect reads as "this reaches the deployed system"), spend rides `spend`. The trigger itself is on the gate, so the mapping loses nothing.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) The trigger table matches generously on PATHS and conservatively on TEXT · a false positive costs one memo; a false negative is a dependency that landed with nobody's signature on it. Manifest file names, contract files and security files match by name or path fragment; commands match only on explicit install verbs.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) `security-posture` is checked BEFORE the contract triggers · an action can match twice (a migration that rewrites permissions), and "this also changes auth" is the more consequential framing to put in front of a reviewer.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) **The harness writes the countersignature; the decider never claims it** · `verdictFilingSchema` carries only `memoId`, `verdict` and `notes` — `decidedBy`, `countersigned` and `authority` are filled from `mayDecide`, and a filing that tries to set any of them is refused as an unknown field. An orchestrator that could write its own countersignature could grant itself authority it was never given, which is the widening FR-5.5 exists to prevent. Three table-driven cases pin it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) `mayDecide` is asked AGAIN when the verdict arrives, not trusted from the triage · the verdict comes back as mail, and mail can arrive late, out of order, or from an orchestrator whose authority table changed in between. The countersignature must describe the authority that exists now.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) `memoVerdictSchema` REFUSES a delegated verdict with no countersignature or no named grant · FR-5.5 is enforced by the validator rather than by the caller remembering, so no code path can write an unsigned delegated decision into the archive.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) A memo gets exactly ONE verdict · `recordVerdict` refuses when `verdict.json` exists. An approval that could be quietly replaced after the action it released had already run would make the archive a claim rather than a record (invariant §5).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) `amended` denies the gate, like `rejected` · an amendment is a *different* action from the one that was held, so the agent must come back with the amended plan rather than have the original released under a verdict that was not about it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) A memo's header is read back out of `memo.md`, not kept in a third file · SDD §4.5 gives a memo directory exactly two files, so the facts a verdict needs (gate, trigger, task) round-trip through the markdown this module also writes. `parseMemoHeader` is tested both ways.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.3) `odeon:verdict` carries no `decidedBy` · a verdict arriving through the window bridge IS the Architect and main knows that with certainty. Taking the renderer's word for who decided would let an untrusted surface stamp a countersignature onto the permanent record of a delegated decision — the same reasoning that keeps a channel off `watch:approve` (invariant §2).

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) The briefing runs one way only: **the harness compiles facts, Artemis narrates them** · ADR-0005 keeps judgement out of the harness, so `compileFacts` has no prose and no opinions, and the narration comes back as mail. The direction is enforceable in exactly one place — every sentence must carry a ref that resolves to a fact the compiler issued — which is what turns FR-7.1's "never from free recollection" from an instruction into a mechanism.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) An **unresolvable ref refuses the whole brief**, and nothing is archived · an invented citation is worse than none, because it looks checked. `checkNarrative` names every unsupported sentence at once so one round-trip fixes them all, and the refused brief leaves no artifact behind.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) The spoken-length budget is **word-count math**, not a synthesis round-trip · SRS §6.2 measures ≤ 90 s at VOICE-DESIGN's 150 wpm, and the check has to work before the Herald exists (M6) and without spending a TTS call to discover a brief is too long. A test raises the budget and re-passes the same text, so the check is provably about length and not about the words.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) The headline is chosen **mechanically by consequence**, not by importance · gates before memos before stalled work before completions. "Which is most important" is judgement and belongs to Artemis; "which of these must the Architect act on" is a fact, and the compiler may know it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) A quiet window still produces a headline fact saying so · a brief that vanished because nothing happened is indistinguishable from a brief that failed (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) The outstanding fact set lives **in memory**, deliberately · it is a question in flight, not a record. A restart that loses it costs one skipped standup; persisting it would risk checking a narration against a window that had already moved on. The archived brief on disk is the record, written only once the answer passes.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4, defect found by the live run) A refused brief must NOT settle the outstanding ask · `archiveBrief` settled unconditionally, so a refusal closed the question and the corrected narration was then rejected as answering a brief nobody had asked for — the refusal was terminal and the retry impossible, which defeats the point of returning reasons. The rule moved out of the wiring and into `BriefingJob.narrated(briefId, accepted)` so it is asserted at the module boundary, with two regression tests.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.4) Cumulative spend for a brief is folded from the durable ledger per agent · `totalOfSpend` calls `spendFor(agentId, null).cumulativeTotals`, never a counter this process kept (ADR-0011, invariant §11).

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) The driver owns **who may speak**, and nothing else · Artemis chairs — what to ask, of whom, and when the meeting is done stays hers (ADR-0005). The driver's whole contribution is that two people can never hold the floor at once and that nobody's answer is discarded for arriving early.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) An out-of-turn reply is **held and later released**, in ATTENDEE order · holding is only worth anything if it pays out, so accepting a turn drains every held reply the floor has now reached. Attendee order rather than arrival order, so the transcript reads the way the meeting was convened rather than the way the network delivered it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5, defect found by a test) The floor is handed out after EVERY accepted turn, never only when the holder changed · a drain that releases held replies can wrap the floor back to the same agent, and comparing floor identity there left nobody asked — a full transcript and a stalled meeting. The guard was a premature optimisation that introduced a stall.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) An Architect interjection is recorded immediately and re-asks the holder · the Architect is not a speaker waiting a turn, they are the reason the meeting is happening. Naming an attendee hands them the floor (and releases anything they had said early); naming nobody leaves the floor put, so an aside does not cost the current speaker their turn.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) A non-attendee is REFUSED, not held · holding implies a turn is coming, and somebody outside the meeting has none.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) The minutes print what **never reached the floor** under its own heading · a meeting closed while somebody was still waiting to speak is a fact about that meeting; hiding it would make the minutes a summary instead of a record.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) Action items go to the ORCHESTRATOR, not to `tasks.json` · FR-4.2 gives the ledger one scribe, and a driver that wrote tasks would be the second writer ADR-0004 spent a package removing. The minutes are the harness's to write (SDD §2 gives it `odeon/`); the tasks are hers to propose. Proven live: a closed meeting left 0 tasks and 1 message to the orchestrator.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) The Odeon endpoint now accepts `inform` as well as `propose` · the floor is handed out as a `query`, and ADR-0003's act table makes the reply to a query an `inform` — refusing it would leave the driver unable to hear its own attendees. `propose` still means "file an artifact", and a test pins that an `inform` carrying a deck-shaped body never becomes a deck.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) Attendees gather via the EXISTING station map · SDD §6 already routes the `meeting` tool class to the `odeon` station, so attendance is `pre-tool`/`post-tool` on the avatar director and needs no new avatar state.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.5) One meeting at a time · two live meetings would put the same agent on two floors, and the Odeon room on the floor plan is one room. A second convene is refused rather than replacing the first, which would lose a transcript nobody had archived yet.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) Every org figure is **folded from the book of record on every read** · invariant §11's rule applied a second time. There is no counter anywhere in `shared/org.ts`: tasks done, rework, escalations and tokens are all counts over `log.jsonl` plus the durable cost fold, recomputed per call. A counter is a second record a restart silently zeroes, and a metric nobody can recompute is a metric nobody can argue with.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) An agent that did nothing appears with **zeroes, not omitted** · "did nothing this week" and "is not in the company" are different facts, and a report that could not tell them apart would be the wrong kind of quiet.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) Rates are **null, not zero**, when nothing has completed · dividing by no tasks is undefined, and reporting 0 would say "perfectly efficient" about an agent that has finished nothing. The renderer prints `—`.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) An event is attributed by whichever key names its agent — `agentId`, `assignee`, `by` or `from` · the log's kinds carry the agent differently, and reading only one would quietly under-count exactly the kinds that matter most (gates and memo escalations).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) The org chart is **derived from the roster, never stored** · the roster IS the org model, and a second copy would be free to disagree with who is actually hired. Ordering is orchestrator-first then role then id, so two runs of the same report never differ by shuffling.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) Every retro finding carries refs, exactly like a brief's sentences · a claim about somebody's week that they cannot check is an opinion with a number next to it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) **The org layer computes and archives; it never acts** · no reassignment, no hire, no nudge. UC-12 puts a human between the numbers and any decision, and the archived report says so in its own last section — a report that read like a verdict would invite the company to act on it without one.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) A retro is written once and never revised · a second generate at the same name is refused rather than overwriting (invariant §5), and retros archive beside the other Odeon artifacts because they are the same kind of thing.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.6) Hire templates carry env grant NAMES and a version · `<name>@<version>` is how a registry row refers back to the file it was hired from; a template carrying a secret VALUE would be the leak ADR-0010 exists to prevent, and the strict schema refuses the extra field.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) **Authority-widening is refused as a property of the PROPOSAL, not of any verdict** · `checkWidening` takes no decider, so there is no argument an approver could pass to make a widening proposal acceptable — the Architect included. FR-12.3 does not say "unless the Architect says otherwise". An Architect who genuinely wants to change an invariant edits the document and supersedes the ADR by hand, outside the loop and in the open, where it is reviewable. A door that only opens from the inside is not a door.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) The widening matcher reads the title, the change AND the rollback · a proposal that hides the widening in its rollback is the same proposal. Matching is generous: a false positive costs one rewrite, a false negative is the company quietly editing the rules that govern it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) `GymDecider` has exactly one inhabitant — `'architect'` · R1 is not a runtime check bolted onto a general "decider": the only decider the Gymnasium has a word for is the Architect, so a caller trying to express "Artemis approved it" has nothing to write down. The redundant proposer-is-decider check stays anyway, because the day somebody adds a second decider is the day it stops being redundant and nobody will remember to add it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) `gym:verdict` carries **no decider field at all** · main supplies `architect` in the handler because a call arriving on the window bridge IS the Architect. Giving the renderer a field to name a decider would hand an untrusted surface the one authority ADR-0015 reserves — the same reasoning that keeps a channel off `watch:approve`.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) A proposal is refused **before any human sees it**, with every reason at once · FR-12.2 puts the harness first, so the proposer's only feedback is that list, and a list of one wastes a round trip per missing field.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) An **unmeasurable** metric counts as a regression, not a pass (FR-12.4) · a change whose effect cannot be established is not a change that worked, and the proposal's rollback is what happens next. The log event carries `rollback: true` so the obligation is findable.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) The ledger is append-only in the sense ADR-0015 means · rows advance in place along the documented flow, and `append` throws if a write would ever shrink the row count. Rejected and regressed rows stay, because R2 calls them training data for better proposals — a ledger that hid its failures would be a highlight reel.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) The ledger **seeds from the repo's own `docs/gymnasium/`** at first run (FR-12.6) · the improvement archive is continuous from the build phase into the running system rather than starting empty on the day the product ships. A missing seed degrades visibly and starts empty; it never fails the boot.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) Gym proposals are filed at the SAME `agent.odeon` endpoint as the other artifacts · SDD §7.6 makes a proposal an Odeon memo subtype, and ADR-0015 rejects the separate meta-agent outright: a second governance system is a second place for governance to fail.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.7) `src/shared/gym-view.ts` holds the renderer-facing types · `shared/ipc.ts` and the sandboxed preload must stay free of runtime dependencies (BUILD-PROMPT §10.4), and `gym.ts` imports zod.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5.8) The Odeon endpoint dispatch was extracted to `src/main/odeon-endpoint.ts` · one address takes five filings, and both `index.ts` and the scenario rig now call the SAME factory. This is the M2 close-out lesson applied before it could bite again: the gate choke points had been wired inline in `index.ts` and copied character-for-character into the rig, so a scenario stayed green with the production wiring deleted.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.8) The scenario rig feeds `submitMemoTrigger` off the same `pre-tool` stream `index.ts` uses · without it S-MEMO would have asserted a hold the running app never opens. Found by writing the suite: the first draft passed the hold test and failed everything downstream, because no gate existed.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.8) The rig's org layer reads `hermes.knownAgents()` rather than the registry · a rig hire makes a mailbox rather than a roster row, so reading the registry there reports metrics for nobody.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5.8, defect found by the EXIT DEMO) The ledger endpoint's `task` log row carried no `status`, so `computeMetrics` could never count a completed task from the book of record · every org unit test passed because every one synthesised the row it wanted — the exact seam-blindness class the M3 and M4 audits named, caught this time by running the thing. The endpoint now logs the status the op left the task in, and the regression test asserts the metric against a log the REAL endpoint produced rather than a fixture.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 exit review, gap found and closed) The standup brief had **no gym-slice section** · IMPLEMENTATION M5 names it and FR-12.5 requires the slice reported in briefings; `Gymnasium.slice()` existed but nothing put it in a brief. `compileFacts` now emits a `health` fact refd `gym:slice` naming what the slice has spent and how many proposals are open, wired in `index.ts` and the scenario rig. R3's whole point is that improvement is budgeted rather than ambient, and an ambient slice is one nobody notices growing.

- 2026-08-28 · (fix) The two literal NUL bytes in `src/shared/breaker.ts` are now written as `\u0000` escapes · the span key separator was a RAW NUL in the source, which made git classify a Watch-critical file as binary: `git diff` showed only `Bin … bytes` and `grep` skipped it without `-a`. A file nobody can read a diff of is a file nobody reviews. The separator itself is unchanged — still U+0000, still the right choice because no tool name or fingerprint can contain one — so the rewrite is byte-identical at runtime and the 82 breaker cases pass untouched. Raised at the M5 exit review as recorded-not-fixed; fixed on `fix/breaker-nul-bytes`.
- 2026-08-28 · (docs) The Decks viewer's `sandbox=""` iframe does not appear in `webContents.capturePage()` · a fully-sandboxed frame gets an opaque origin, is composited out-of-process, and Chromium's page capture misses it. The viewer is fine — a throwaway `allow-same-origin` run rendered the deck in frame and confirmed it. The sandbox was deliberately **not** loosened to make a screenshot prettier: `allow-same-origin` without `allow-scripts` is arguably harmless today, but changing a security-relevant attribute for a photograph is the wrong reason to change one. The archived deck is exported to `docs/demo/m5-deck-artifact.html` instead, which a reader can actually open.

- 2026-08-28 · (integration, defect found by the MERGE) The Gymnasium ledger parser read **zero rows out of a five-row archive** · `docs/gymnasium/LEDGER.md` writes each id as a link to its proposal file — a `[GYM-001]` label carrying a `./proposals/…` target — while the parser matched only the bare `GYM-001`. In isolation nothing failed: the M5.7 unit rig seeded from a fixture whose ids happen to be bare. The two halves only met when the branch landed beside the real archive, and the consequence was not a parse error but a silent one — a seeded ledger reads as empty, so the next mint hands out `GYM-001` to a row that is already on the page and R2's "the ledger is total" quietly stops being true. `GymRow` now carries `idCell`, the id is matched in either form, and `renderRow` writes the cell back exactly as the ledger wrote it, because the ledger is a document a human reads and not only a table a machine appends to. This is the seam-blindness class the M3 and M4 audits named, found a third way: not by a demo and not by a suite, but by two correct halves meeting.
- 2026-08-28 · (integration) The eleven M5 branches were merged to `main` **one at a time, each with `--no-ff`**, rather than squashed or fast-forwarded as a block · the parallel research-department line (closing time, the hook-boundary steer, ADRs 0017–0020) had landed on `main` underneath them, and a single collapsed merge would have made the one conflict that mattered — `test/scenarios/company.ts`, where both lines construct Hermes — indistinguishable from the trivial append-only ones in PROGRESS and the log. Each merge was gated before the next began, and the integrated tree was proven on CI (run 33177234907) on a `fix/**` branch BEFORE `main` was moved to it, so the branch that fast-forwarded `main` was a tree Linux had already run green rather than one only Windows had seen.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, fixed) The Gymnasium ledger writer emitted **seven cells under the eight-column header** · `renderRow` skipped the Measured column, so `measure()` recorded an outcome that `rows()` immediately read back as null, and the next `propose()` — which rewrites every row — permanently erased every Measured cell and Outcome on the page. ADR-0015 R2 ("every measured outcome is a permanent ledger row") was mechanically false, one column to the left of where the merge-day fix had just looked. `GymRow` now carries `measured`, `renderRow` emits both cells, `measure()` stamps the check date, and the regression tests round-trip Measured/Outcome through a rewrite — including against the REAL archive's `due 2026-09-11` rows, the case finding 10 said the owed R2 tests never asserted.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, fixed) The memo-verdict endpoint answers now render from `prompts/odeon/verdict-*.md` · two prose literals and a refusal subject had survived on the one Odeon path the M4-era sweep predated (invariant §8); the null-prompts fallback stays a mechanical serialization.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, fixed) The Gymnasium seed copies `proposals/GYM-*.md` beside `LEDGER.md` · SDD §2 always said both; seeding only the table left every row's link broken and `proposalDoc()` null in the running system — half an archive inherited (FR-12.6).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, fixed) `slice()` reports **null, not zero**, when no gym-spend source is wired, and the brief says "not yet attributed" · production wires no `gymSpend`, so the gym-slice fact was narrating a constant 0 as ledger data — the invisible-degradation class invariant §7 forbids. The *attribution* itself (tagging which spend is gym work) is genuinely deferred: it needs the task↔proposal join and belongs with the M6 scheduler work already carrying the metric-check booking. `withinSlice` treats a missing source as 0 spent, so R3's gate is honest-but-idle until then — recorded, visible in the brief.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, fixed) `LOG_KINDS` gains `memory` and the reserved-id spawn-refusal test iterates `RESERVED_AGENT_IDS` · the kind was SDD-ratified at the M4 close but absent from the "verbatim" code list; the test covered two of five reserved ids, leaving GYM-003's "no hire can forge a closing acknowledgment" claim untested.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, recorded) `test/scenarios/company.ts` wires Hermes without the `ledger:` endpoint option · scenarios drive `LedgerEndpoint.submit` directly, so a scenario that MAILED `agent.ledger` would get "endpoint not available" instead of the shipped dispatch. Pre-existing; owed to the next scenario that needs the mail path, not patched speculatively.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5 close-out audit, environment record completed) Three Windows/TZ test-portability defects were failing at HEAD but absent from this record · `test/shared/cost.test.ts` "splits one transcript across the days it spans" is TZ-dependent (local-time `dayKey`, fixture straddles midnight UTC only — passes under `TZ=UTC`); `test/main/engines/claude-transcripts.test.ts` "points at the engine's own project directory" fails on Windows path resolution regardless of TZ; `test/main/library.test.ts` "tells the agent out loud when the budget elided older sections" is load-flake (passes in isolation). All three predate M5 and pass on the Linux CI gate; recorded so the next Windows session reads 10 locals fails, not 7.
