- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) Retirement is a **sibling array, not a flag on an entry** · SDD §4.7 gives the entry shape but no way to express retirement, and FR-13.1 requires `retire`. A `retiredAt` field would have made every consumer responsible for filtering it, and the day one forgets is the day the Stoa studies a source the Architect withdrew. `sources` now holds only studiable sources *by construction* and retired entries move to `retired` verbatim — the same idiom SDD §2 already uses for `inbox/` → `inbox/.done/`. Nothing is deleted, because a brief that cites a source the record no longer contains is a citation nobody can check. SDD §4.7 updated to name the array.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) `pin` is **nullable**, and null means "registered, not studiable" · §4.7 types it as a string because it describes a source that has been studied; the build-phase table carries "(set at first study)" for the rest, and FR-13.2 requires a pinned snapshot. Making the unpinned state representable is what lets `checkStudiable` refuse it — a required-string pin would have forced the seed to invent a commit, which is the one thing the provenance chain cannot survive.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) The `stoa:` IPC group is **exactly SDD §5's five channels** · no `stoa:pin`, even though `Stoa` will need to record the commit a study ran against: SDD §7.7 puts pin-setting inside the study flow, so it lands with M5b.2 that owns it rather than as an Architect button nothing yet calls. Widening a documented IPC signature is a BUILD-PROMPT §8 must-ask, not an implementation detail.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) `register` takes a **draft with no id, no registrar and no timestamp** · main mints all three, exactly as `gym:verdict` supplies its own `architect`. The strict payload schema means a renderer trying to smuggle `registeredBy: 'artemis'` is rejected at the boundary rather than quietly ignored — R1's authority is main's to assert, never the renderer's to claim.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) A damaged watchlist is read as empty and **never overwritten** · the file may be the only copy of what the Architect registered, so a boot that "repaired" it by truncating would destroy the record it could not read. The degradation is reported out loud instead (invariant §7).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.1) The seed copies the table **and** `briefs/` together · the Gymnasium shipped a ledger without the proposals its rows linked to and every link broke (M5 close-out audit, finding 3). A seeded source whose brief did not cross over is the same half-archive one subsystem over, so the test asserts both halves.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) An **unverified license permits study and refuses intake**; UC-14 alternate 2a's "license unverifiable" is a different state · the documents appear to disagree — UC-14 alt 2a says an unverifiable license refuses the study, while FR-13.5, SDD §4.7's schema comment and TEST-STRATEGY's S-STOA clause all say `unverified` allows study and refuses pattern intake. Read carefully they do not: FR-13.5 is the normative requirement and conditions only *intake* on a verified license, FR-13.2 (which governs studies) says nothing about licenses at all, and "unverifiable" — the Architect could not establish the terms — is a stronger state than "not yet checked", one the watchlist has no separate value for and which the Architect expresses by not registering the source (or retiring it). So `checkStudiable` reads only the pin and `checkIntake` only the license, and the researcher's own briefing says which it got.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) A research brief files as `kind: "research-brief"`, not `brief` · the standup narration took `brief` in M5.4, years of code before the Stoa existed. Overloading it would have made two unrelated artifacts answer for each other at one address, and the failure mode — a narration validated as research, or the reverse — is silent. The endpoint now dispatches six filings; the extra word is cheap.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) Research briefs file at the SAME `agent.odeon` endpoint as everything else · the M5.7 reasoning for gym proposals, applied again: ADR-0015 rejects a second governance machine, and a second filing address is a second place for filing to fail.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) `Stoa.plan()` returns a **plan, not a spawn** · the caller executes it. That is what makes NFR-17's posture assertable without running anything: S-STOA reads `envGrants` and `readOnly` off a value instead of trusting a comment above a spawn call. `envGrants` is typed `readonly []` and no parameter can fill it — a researcher reads arbitrary third-party text, and handing it a credential is the one mistake that turns a bad README into a breach.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) The checkout path is re-checked against the Agora and `worktrees/` **inside the driver**, even though the driver supplies the root it was built from · ADR-0004 gives the Agora exactly one working copy and `worktrees/` holds the company's OWN targets; a defence that depends on nobody ever passing the wrong root is not a defence.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) The injection rule lives in `prompts/stoa/study.md`, and a plan with no prompt store carries `instructions: null` · invariant §8, and NFR-17 is the instruction most likely to need rewording as real sources teach us how an attack is phrased — a rule the Architect cannot edit without a rebuild ages badly. Null rather than a silent default, because an un-briefed researcher is exactly the degradation invariant §7 forbids being invisible.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) The brief schema has a place to REPORT a directive — `directive: true` on a finding · without a field for it, the only way a researcher could mention "the README told me to do X" would be to do X. Reporting is the correct outcome (UC-14 alt 3a), the archive marks it loudly, and the `brief-archived` log event carries `directivesReported` so an incident audit can find it without opening the brief.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) Commit comparison is **prefix matching in the longer direction** · a human types a short sha onto the watchlist and a checkout reports the full one. Requiring equality would reject correct briefs over a formatting difference, which teaches researchers to distrust the validator rather than the claim.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.2) A brief archive file is **written once and never revised** · a second filing under an existing name is refused rather than merged (FR-13.4, invariant §5): the proposals citing a brief must keep resolving to the words their author actually read. A corrected study is a new brief, not an edit.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3, latent defect found while building on it) `Gymnasium.append()` **deleted everything below its table** · it rebuilt the file as `preamble + table` and dropped the rest, so any section written under the proposals table — a human's note, or FR-14.5's mode-change record — would vanish at the next `propose()`. Nothing had ever written below the table before, so nothing had noticed. This is the M5 close-out's finding-1 class one row further down: a rewrite that silently loses a part of the document it rewrites. `append()` now preserves the postamble, and three regressions assert a mode section survives a later proposal, that a second change appends under the same heading, and that the mode table's pipe rows are never mistaken for proposal rows.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) A mode change is a **section under the ledger table, not a row in it** · UC-15's postcondition puts every mode change "in the ledger and log", but the proposals table has eight columns meant for a proposal, and forcing a mode change into them would corrupt what both the parser and the reader rely on. The Gymnasium writes the section because the Gymnasium owns that file — one writer, no second opinion about what the ledger says.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) `everEnabledImproving` is **persisted, not inferred** · the proof gate is a first-enable check (FR-14.3). Inferring "has it ever been on?" from the ledger would make a rung-3 auto-revert (FR-14.5) look like the gate had never been met and silently re-impose it — turning a safety stop into a demotion nobody asked for. The Architect can restore `improving` after a breaker revert without re-proving anything.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) A **regressed** row counts toward §6.9's "full loop" · the loop is proposed → verdict → landed → measured, and a change that was measured and missed completed that loop. ADR-0015 R2 keeps regressed rows precisely because they are evidence the company can measure its own failures; a gate that only counted successes would reward never trying anything risky.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) A gating violation refuses the gate **permanently**, and says so · every other clause is satisfiable by waiting. "A proposal reached `landed` with no Architect approval on the log" is not immaturity, it is a broken loop, and no amount of further evidence should open the gate on top of it. The refusal names the row so it can be argued with.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) The mode reaches the scheduler as a `Trigger.enabled()` predicate, checked **before the interval and without stamping the clock** · autonomy is switched off at the one place that starts autonomous work, rather than by each cadence policing itself — a job that polices itself is a job that can forget to. Not stamping the clock means a cadence forbidden for a week fires when it is allowed again instead of sitting out one more interval for having been asked while off.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) A rung-3 revert is attributed to `breaker`, never to the Architect · the log and the ledger both say who did it, so nobody reading the record later mistakes a safety stop for the Architect changing their mind. Attribution of "gym/stoa work" is by ROLE from the roster: a rung-3 stop on ordinary mission work must not switch off self-improvement for reasons that had nothing to do with it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.3) The status strip shows **nothing** rather than `directed` when the mode cannot be read · FR-14.1 requires the mode visible at all times, and a mode nobody could read is not a mode that is off. Claiming the safe state on a failed read is a degradation failing as good news — the one direction invariant §7 does not allow.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) A proposal citing `RB-NNN` is **refused unless that brief is archived** (FR-13.4) · a citation to a brief nobody filed is the uncited-finding problem wearing a different hat: it looks like provenance and resolves to nothing. The link between a brief and the proposals it seeded is therefore the citation itself — no second index to fall out of date, because the evidence ref was already required to exist. The `proposed` log event carries the cited ids so the proof gate can count Stoa-seeded proposals from the log alone.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) Brief ids are matched **inside** an evidence ref, not as the whole of it · `"RB-014 finding 2"` counts. An author naming which finding they built on is being more precise, and a matcher that demanded a bare id would punish them for it.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) The standup's Stoa line is **omitted, not zeroed**, when there is no research department · "the Stoa did nothing this week" and "there is no Stoa" are different facts, and the M5.6 org-metrics decision already settled which way that goes. The mode line rides the same section (FR-14.1), so "did we do this because we were asked?" is answerable from the brief alone.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) S-MODE **archives a real brief** and cites it, rather than citing a fixture id · the §6.9 clause is "seeded by a Stoa brief", and once FR-13.4 refuses unarchived citations, a scenario that cited a literal id would have been testing nothing. Seeding the shared fixture with a brief was rejected: it perturbed S-STOA's archive counts, and a fixture that makes one suite easier by changing another's world is how fixtures start lying.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) E-STOA is a **scorer plus a discrimination test**, not an assertion about a researcher · evals are quality, not correctness (TEST-STRATEGY §6) and non-gating. `test/evals/e-stoa.ts` scores one brief against the fixture's planted patterns, noise and injection; `e-stoa.test.ts` asserts the RUBRIC discriminates — a good brief passes and each named failure mode actually fails it. A rubric nobody has tested against a deliberately bad answer will quietly pass everything on the day it matters.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) The eval's deterministic half runs in CI; the **LLM-judged half is recorded as owed** · citation presence, injection reporting and noise-only findings are computable. "Is this applicability mapping honest?" and "does the prose actually match the file it cites?" need a rubric-scoring model over a real engine, which this machine's sessions have not run. Recorded in PROGRESS with the other owed real-engine evidence rather than faked — the project's standing rule.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.4) Reporting the planted injection **does not cost precision** · the directive finding is a real finding. A rubric that penalised it would teach researchers to stay quiet about injections, which is the opposite of NFR-17's intent. Silently ignoring one fails the run instead — not as a security failure but as an evidence failure: it throws away the most useful thing the study found.

- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) The `*.tiles.json` maps **are committed**; the sheets are not · `.gitignore` now excepts them. A layout is our own work — a table of integers describing where things sit on somebody else's sheet — and it contains no asset data. Committing it is what makes ATTRIBUTION's restore path reproducible instead of a story: a fresh clone copies two PNGs into the drop and the floor paints. The sheets themselves stay out, because the LimeZu licence forbids redistributing the asset.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) Frame indices were chosen by **measuring the sheet, not by eye** · a first pass picked border tiles that looked plain at 3× zoom and would have tiled with visible seams. The second pass scored every candidate tile by per-channel colour variance and required full opacity, which found the flat fills objectively; each chosen index was then rendered back FROM its index (`x = i % columns`, `y = i / columns`) and tiled 3×3 to prove the arithmetic and the seam. `src/shared/tileset.ts` says hard-coding indices for a pack you cannot see would be fiction — measuring is how you avoid writing fiction that compiles.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) `floor-a`/`floor-b` are **near-neighbours in hue**, not just in luminance · the first mapping paired cream (240,240,200) with mint (189,241,229): almost identical luminance, opposite hue, and the floor read as a chessboard rather than one surface. Re-mapped to cream + marble-rose (244,217,212), with the indigo block reserved for `temple` where a distinct, cooler tone is the point. UI-DESIGN §1's ≤8-colours bar is about what the screen READS as, and two hues alternating per tile is two hues however close their luminance.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) A pack declares its **own required credit**, as `credit` in its tile map · the LimeZu licence requires attribution to limezu.itch.io, and the floor's status strip now prints it beside the pack name. It ships with the pack for the same reason the layout does: it is a term of *that* licence, and a credit hard-coded in the app would be wrong the moment somebody swapped the pack. Optional, because a CC0 pack owes none.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) Only the two **Room Builder** sheets were kept; the furniture sheets were extracted and then deleted · nothing maps a frame to them, and `import.meta.glob` would have bundled 2 MB of unused PNG into the shipped renderer. `seat` and `station` therefore stay procedural over a licensed floor — the "any subset" behaviour the tile map was designed for, visible in `docs/demo/m5b-floor-limezu.png`. Compositing a custom atlas from both sheets is permitted by the licence ("edit and use") but is a bigger decision than this package, and is left to the Architect.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) The Kenney CC0 packs were **retired for quality, not licence** · their terms would still permit use. §7's bar is the LimeZu line, and keeping two half-mapped packs in one drop invites exactly the bug `resolveTileset` guards against — painting one pack's frames out of the other's sheet.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) The character generator shipped in the purchase is **never run**, and its character sheets are never used · ATTRIBUTION rule 3 exists so no likeness and no other IP's character can appear on the floor, and a purchased character pack is precisely the temptation it was written against. Citizens stay procedural.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.5) `test/renderer/tileset.test.ts` gained a block that validates **the actual installed drop** when there is one · the sheets are gitignored, so CI runs it with nothing to check; on a machine where the packs are restored it is the only thing that catches a map naming a missing sheet, an index past the end of the sheet, or a wrong `columns`. PNG dimensions are read straight from the IHDR chunk rather than adding an image decoder for one arithmetic check (invariant §10).

- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.6, DEFECT FOUND BY THE EXIT DEMO) The proof gate read **every seeded ledger row as a gating violation**, which made the gate unopenable on any real company · `checkProofGate` treated "reached `landed` with no `approved` event on the log" as the absorbing failure. But a ledger seeded from the build-phase archive (FR-12.6) inherits rows the Architect genuinely DID decide — the archive records the Decided date — while the fresh log has no `gym` events for them at all. So GYM-001…005 each produced a violation, and because a violation can never be waited out, `improving` could never be enabled by any company that inherited an archive. Which is every company. Every unit test passed because each one synthesised the log it wanted; the seam only showed when the running app read the REAL seeded ledger beside a REAL fresh log — the same seam-blindness class the M3, M4 and M5 audits each found a different way. A verdict now counts from EITHER input, which is what FR-14.3 permits ("the Gymnasium ledger and event log"), and the violation is reserved for a row with no verdict in either place. Regressions cover the seeded shape and the genuinely-undecided one.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.6) The reading desk offers a **pin field** · M5b.1 deferred pin-setting to M5b.2's study flow and M5b.2 built `plan()`, which only READS the pin. Neither package owned setting it, so every source registered from the desk was permanently unstudiable — a hole the exit demo walked straight into. The register draft already carried `pin: string | null`; the panel was hard-coding `null`. Empty still means unpinned, which is a legal and visibly-refused state (FR-13.2).
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.6) The exit demo drives the **real desk and the real router** · the URL is registered by filling the actual inputs and clicking the actual button (real IPC, no harness write), and the brief and proposal arrive as files in a real agent outbox so Hermes, the Odeon endpoint, the Stoa archive and the Gymnasium all run their shipped paths. Only the researcher's ENGINE is stood in for. The router earned its keep on the way: it refused the first three attempts for a malformed id, a missing `needs_human`, and `requires_reply: false` on a `propose` (ADR-0003's obligation table) — each parked in `outbox/.rejected/` with the reason, exactly as designed.
- 2026-08-28 · (M5b.6, DEFECT FOUND BY CI) The drop-validation block guarded **every** check on "a map exists", which broke the moment the maps became tracked · committing the `*.tiles.json` maps (M5b.5, deliberately) meant CI suddenly had maps and — correctly — no sheets, so "names a sheet that is installed" and "frames all land inside the sheet" failed on ubuntu while passing on the machine that has the drop. Two populations, checkable in two places: the MAPS are our own work and CI should validate their shape; the SHEETS are the licensed asset and anything that opens one runs only where the drop is restored. Verified both ways before pushing — 33 cases with the sheets moved aside, 35 with them present.

- 2026-08-29 · (M5b close-out audit, evidence integrity) The exit demo's research cycle was **source-fake beyond its disclosed stand-in** · pin `9f3c1de` exists in neither `sst/opencode-sdk-js` nor `sst/opencode`, the cited path is another repo's layout, and the GYM-006↔RB-002 pairing survived in no artifact — the harness's provenance check compares brief-commit to watchlist-pin and cannot see a remote, so a typed pin that never existed passed every internal gate. The exit-review row is amended in place, and the cycle re-run at close against munder-difflin @ `b91a49f` (verified via `gh api` AND a local clone inside the run) through the shipped path — `docs/demo/m5b-cycle-real-source.txt`. Standing lesson for the demo discipline: an exit demo's external references get the same verification its internal ones do.
- 2026-08-29 · (M5b close-out audit, fixed) `Stoa.brief(id)` seeds before reading, as `briefs()` always did · the first-call order decided whether a seeded brief existed; a fresh home refused a proposal citing `RB-001`. First-call regression test added; the half-seeded-seam class is now three-for-three across audits (gym proposals, gym parser, this) — seed-on-EVERY-read-path is the rule.
- 2026-08-29 · (M5b close-out audit, fixed) The Stoa cadence body is the shipped `stoa-cadence.ts` · S-MODE and the scheduler suite each rebuilt the trigger inline, so the production body was exercised by nothing — the exact copy-of-the-wiring class the rig's own comment records. `index.ts` and both suites now run one tick, which returns what it logged so the tests assert the record, not a copy.
- 2026-08-29 · (M5b close-out audit, fixed) `isImprovementRole` is exact and shared, not a substring in `index.ts` · `includes('improv')` would have reverted the company mode over a rung-3 stop on a mission hire named "process-improver-docs". Roles come from ADR-0019's vocabulary (`researcher`, `improver`), extended visibly in `shared/mode.ts`, table-tested with the audit's counter-example.
- 2026-08-29 · (M5b close-out audit, recorded not fixed) Deferred with reasons · FR-14.1's mode tag rides `cadence-fired` alone until M7 creates more autonomous record types; the §6.9 `stoaSeeded` count reads `gym`/`proposed` log events only, so a company inheriting a measured build-phase archive under-counts — the STRICT direction (the gate waits, never opens early), fix rides the M7 gate work; Stoa spend attribution joins the gym-spend deferral (M6.7); no path advances a watchlist pin (`stoa:pin` channel or study-recorded pin — the owed list carries both); `prompts/stoa/rank.md` and `study-refused.md` await the Artemis-ranking leg; `plan.question`'s connective sentence is invariant-§8-adjacent (serialization otherwise); the reading-desk pin fix carries no renderer regression until M6.1 establishes a DOM harness.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) The renderer test harness is **`react-dom/server` static markup**, not a DOM library · the M5b close-out owed the reading desk a renderer regression "once M6.1 establishes a DOM harness", and jsdom/happy-dom would be a new dependency — a BUILD-PROMPT §8 must-ask. Static markup renders the SHIPPED component bodies (never a copy of them, the M5b rig lesson) with nothing added to the tree: `esbuild: { jsx: 'automatic' }` in the vitest config plus a fourth tsconfig project, `tsconfig.web-test.json`, because the node project has no `jsx` setting and no DOM lib and widening it would loosen the whole main/shims/test surface for three files. What it does NOT cover — effects, clicks, state transitions — is tested at the module boundary instead (BUILD-PROMPT §6): the pin mapping moved out of the component into the exported pure `registerDraft`. A jsdom upgrade for interaction coverage is carried as a must-ask in the session report, not decided here.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) `SourceRow` and `BriefCard` are exported presentational components · the reading desk's data-dependent branches (`pin: —` for an unpinned source, the struck-through retired row, the two degradation notes) only exist when rows are present, and effects do not run under static markup. Extracting them makes the shipped row body reachable without a test-only prop on the panel. Behaviour unchanged.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) Role → silhouette is an **exact-match table with a deterministic hash fallback** · §5.1's six roles are named by category ("Scribe / docs", "Builder / code"), so the table keys are those words plus the two role strings the company already uses (`orchestrator`, `engineer`). Exact equality, never a substring test: a hire named "process-improver-docs" matching on "docs" is the M5b audit's `includes('improv')` defect one domain over. Unknown roles hash to one of the five assignable silhouettes — stable and total, rather than a guess dressed as a match. `orchestrator` is unreachable by hash: it is Artemis's alone.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) The `working` overlay is declared as kind `token` with **no pixels until M6.3** · §5.2's working row IS §5.3's carrying token, and §5.3 is M6.3's package. Drawing a placeholder mark would be a shape projecting no fact — the "invented motion" §5.4 forbids one layer up. The phase still reads: `shared/badges.ts` names it in shape and in the floor census. The overlay test asserts the empty-until-§5.3 seam explicitly so it cannot be forgotten.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1, DEFECT FOUND BY THE LIVE RENDER) Citizens gained a **1 px ink silhouette backing** · the first render of the §5.1 sprite passed all twenty unit tests — 8 drawn directions, frame counts, bob at frame boundaries, head-room clear, ≤5 colours, prop sizes — and looked like a stack of boxes that dissolved into the floor, because `skin` is `sand` (`#c9a05c`) and that is exactly `worldTerraceA`. Asserting the spec is not the same as meeting the §7 quality bar. Fixed generally rather than by re-picking one colour: every body part emits an ink backing one pixel larger on each side, all backings drawn before all fills so the outline is continuous. This is why the head starts at row 10 — `10 − 1 (bob) − 1 (backing) = 8`, the first row §5.1 allows a body pixel. Anatomy redrawn at the same time: hairline, neck, tunic hem, sleeve-plus-hand arms.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) `hairSide` gives each view an **asymmetric skull mass** · with symmetric sideburns the `e`/`w` profiles read as slightly narrow front views. The hair mass sits on the side turned away from the viewer, which is the strongest directional cue available at 32 px — and a second reason a runtime flip would be wrong, since a flip would carry the back of the head round to the front.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.1) `docs/demo/m6-1-citizens-v2.svg` is generated **from the shipped modules** · the contact sheet (8 directions × 4 frames × 6 silhouettes, plus every §5.2 overlay frame) is rendered by importing `citizenSprite` and `overlayPixels` themselves, so the evidence cannot drift from the code. SVG rather than a screenshot because it is text, diffable, and needs no image encoder (invariant §10). The generator is a scratch file, not committed — the artifact is.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2) The §5.4 size catalog lives in `shared/floor.ts`, the state model in `shared/stations.ts` · geometry is what the plan needs and the state model does not, so the dependency runs one way and there is no cycle between them. `floorPlan()` now claims each station's whole FOOTPRINT (`stationFootprint`) instead of one tile — before M6.2 the Odeon was 96×64 in the document and 32×32 on the floor, which made §5.4's size column decorative. The Odeon's anchor moved from col 17 to col 16 so its third column is not the east wall.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2) `stationView()` returns a `because` string, and it is not decoration · §5.4 ends "every state maps to an event-plane fact; no station animates on a timer alone". Making the reason part of the return type turns that from a rule someone must remember into one the compiler asks about: a station somebody wants to animate for looks has nothing to put in the field. It doubles as the §8 information-parity text — `stationCensus()` is the same facts in words, so nothing on the floor is visible only as motion (NFR-15).
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2) `AvatarUpdate` gains `pendingMail`; `AvatarSnapshot` does NOT · the desk tray flag IS `pendingMailCount` (§5.4), and it must arrive with the same agent's snapshot at the same moment or the floor can raise a flag on a citizen who has just consumed the mail. It rides the addressed wrapper rather than the SDD §6 state machine, because mail is not one of that machine's states. One source in main (`pendingMailFor`) feeds the autonomy loop, the push AND the `avatars:list` handler — the M5b lesson is that a fact on the listing path and not the others is a seam no unit test sees, so a regression asserts the listing path carries it.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2) Compositions and furnishings ride the pack's `*.tiles.json`, never code · §5.4 stations are multi-tile structures and §5.7 furnishings are place identity; which frames compose either is a property of the PACK, so both are optional map fields validated like the rest. `validateCompositions` enforces `frames.length === cols × rows` AND that the footprint matches the §5.4 catalog — a silently mis-sized composition is a station painting over its neighbour. A broken entry degrades that station to the single frame and then to the procedural painter (invariant §7); it never throws. Both checks are map-only, so CI runs them with no sheets present (the M5b drop-guard rule).
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2, DEFECT FOUND BY THE LIVE FLOOR) `PlanCell` gained `part` · with footprints landed, the painter still painted per tile, so a 64×32 desk came out as two 32px desks and a row of them read as one long slab — and a 2×2 station showed four gold markers. The plan now tells each cell where it sits inside its structure and how big that structure is, so the inset is applied only on outer edges and the marker once per structure, on its anchor. Second time this milestone the running floor said something a green suite could not: M6.1's citizens dissolved into the terrace, and these were wallpaper.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.2) The floor polls `odeon.meeting()` at 5 s · the Watch brazier reads `watch.approvals()` and the existing `gate:open` nudge, but SDD §5's event list has no push channel for a live meeting, so the Odeon fill is polled at the cadence the panels already use. This is the same gap the `odeon:queue` status-strip badge carried item names, and it closes on M6.7's scheduler work.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.3) Every envelope is built from a LOG ENTRY, never from a renderer event · `envelopeFor(entry)` takes its identity from `msgId`, its colour from `act` and its start from the entry's own `ts`, so replaying `log.jsonl` flies the same envelopes at the same moments. That is NFR-13's spirit in the vfx layer: a renderer that minted ids or read its own clock would hold state the record cannot account for, which is the second source of truth ADR-0014 forbids the floor. The floor follows `log:append` and re-reads the tail, seeding its cursor from the newest entry so a window opened mid-run does not replay the day's mail as a storm.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.3) §5.3's **search** row has no tool class, and §6's **meeting** class has no token · SDD §6's station map is the normative list of classes (file/shell/web/mcp/ledger/meeting) and has no `search`; UI-DESIGN §5.3 prints a `search` row anyway. The row is left unreachable rather than inventing a class — adding one is an SDD change, not a floor change. Conversely `meeting` gets `null`: a citizen comes back from the Odeon carrying nothing, which is honest, where invented art would not be. The table is total over `ToolClass` so the compiler keeps it that way.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.3) The token comes from the STATION a citizen is leaving · §5.3 keys tokens by tool class, but an avatar snapshot carries only its station. `STATION_FOR_TOOL_CLASS` is injective, so `toolClassForStation` recovers the class without adding a field to the SDD §6 snapshot and without the floor ever learning a tool NAME (NFR-12). A regression asserts no Claude-ism (`Read`, `Bash`, `WebFetch`, …) resolves to a token.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.3) Reduced motion is read from `prefers-reduced-motion`, not a new app setting · it is the signal the platform already gives, it needs no IPC or config schema, and it is live (a reader may change it mid-session). §8's parity is asserted as EQUALITY between the moving form's information and the still form's — `reduceEnvelope(f).info` must equal `envelopeInfo(f)` — rather than as the presence of a label, because "there is a label" is exactly the check that passes while the label says nothing useful.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.3) `test/main/vfx-seam.test.ts` delivers REAL mail through the REAL router and asks the model what flies · the unit suite writes its own log entries, which is the two-correct-halves blindness every audit here has found. Demonstrated rather than asserted: renaming Hermes's `msgId` field leaves `test/shared/vfx.test.ts` 24/24 green and fails three seam cases. Bounce, hop-cap divert and delivery are each driven through the shipped path.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.4) `seam.ts` is ADR-0007's three interfaces and nothing more · `SpeechToText`, `TextToSpeech`, optional `DuplexVoice` — transcribed, not extended. The M1.1 lesson: an adapter interface that grows a convenience method becomes a contract every future adapter owes, and the ADR is where that decision belongs. There is deliberately no `shouldFailover()`, `retry()` or provider preference anywhere on the seam: an adapter's whole obligation on failure is to classify the fault and hand it up, and the conformance suite asserts no such method exists.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.4) Three fault classes, because the policy treats exactly three differently · `auth` (retrying cannot help — the provider is done for the session), `transient` (worth another provider and a later manual failback), `latency-breach` (answered, but too slowly to be a voice). TEST-STRATEGY §5 asks for "auth vs transient vs latency-breach → correct failover state machine transitions", so the taxonomy is the seam's, and each adapter maps its provider's own shapes onto it — recorded in the fixtures, since ElevenLabs errors are HTTP-shaped and Realtime's arrive as session events.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.4) The failover machine is ONE-WAY; failback is manual · ADR-0007 says so in two words, and the reason is operational: a provider that failed auth will fail it again, and one that breached latency once will do it under the same load, so a timer-based recovery would flap the session. A fault burns its provider for the session; `cooldown` (no provider, voice off, everything else in text per FR-8.6) is the honest end state. A stale fault from a provider already left is ignored — acting on it would burn the provider just switched TO.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.4) The repeat-back TOKEN is derived from the gate; the SENTENCE is config · FR-8.4 needs the Architect to say something specific, so `repeatBackToken()` builds `confirm <first three words of the action>` — "confirm" alone would be a bare assent with extra steps. The asking sentence lives in `prompts/herald/phrasebook.md` (invariant §8), and the policy emits a phrase-book KEY, never a sentence; a test greps the policy source for spoken prose. Bare assents are refused with `because: 'bare-assent'` rather than a generic mismatch, so the Herald can explain itself instead of pretending it did not hear.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.4) The voice conformance suite runs on RECORDED FIXTURES and ships before the adapters · a suite needing a key would not run in CI, and an adapter that only proves itself when someone is watching proves nothing. `test/fakes/fake-voice.ts` replays `test/fixtures/voice/*.json` — real provider error shapes and measured latencies, no key, no network, no audio, and a test that asserts no secret-shaped string got into a recording. M6.5/M6.6's adapters join the same list and nothing about the suite changes (NFR-12).

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.5, ARCHITECT-APPROVED DEPENDENCY) `@elevenlabs/elevenlabs-js@^2.65` and `openai@^7.8` added · a BUILD-PROMPT §8.3 must-ask, put to the Architect with three options and approved on the spot. The alternative was hand-rolling two streaming wire protocols (ElevenLabs realtime STT, OpenAI Realtime duplex) with no key in this environment to verify either — code that would be asserted only against fixtures I wrote myself, which is precisely the plausible-but-unverifiable class the M5b audit caught. The eslint boundary confining voice SDKs to `src/main/herald/` already existed and anticipated exactly this. `npm audit` reports 2 high-severity findings; both are pre-existing (electron, pinned `^37` by the M0 working constraint, and `extract-zip` beneath it), neither introduced by the SDKs.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.5, MY OWN M6.4 SEAM WAS INCOMPLETE) `SpeakOptions.onAudio` added · ADR-0007 says "streamed audio with cancel", and the M6.4 transcription had the cancel with no way for the audio to leave. The first real adapter found it — which is the argument for writing an adapter against a seam rather than admiring the seam. Completing the ADR's own sentence, not extending it.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.5) `spokenSoFar()` is a MEASUREMENT · the SDK's `streamWithTimestamps` returns character alignment, so the "unspoken from here" mark VOICE-DESIGN §2 requires is the characters the provider actually produced audio for — not an estimate at 150 wpm. When the alignment drifts from the text, `bargeIn` treats a non-prefix as "nothing was heard", which is the safe direction: claiming the Architect heard something they did not is the worse error.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.5) The key arrives through an injected getter, never `process.env` · `check-invariants.cjs` PERMITS `process.env` under `herald/`, so the permission needed a second check: a test strips comments from the adapter and asserts the code does not read the environment. Voice id and both model ids load from `prompts/herald/*.md` and a test asserts neither appears in the adapter source — a voice id in code is the persona in code by another name (FR-8.5, invariant §8).
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.5, TRIPWIRE FALSE POSITIVE) The voice-SDK lint patterns gained `!./**`, `!../**` · eslint's `no-restricted-imports` `group` uses gitignore semantics, so the bare pattern `elevenlabs` matched `../../src/main/herald/elevenlabs` — our OWN adapter, which SDD §8 names `elevenlabs.ts`. Rather than rename the file away from the SDD or exempt the test directories (which would let a test import the real SDK), the patterns now exclude relative specifiers: a relative import can never be an SDK import. Verified BOTH directions — the rule still errors on `import from 'elevenlabs'` outside `herald/`, and no longer fires on the shipped adapter.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.6) `DuplexSession.say(text)` added, for the same reason `onAudio` was · ADR-0007 gives OpenAI Realtime one job — be the TTS fallback — and says "the policy layer maps the common conversation contract onto it". A duplex session that could only carry captured audio could not do that job at all. Both seam additions come from sentences already in the ADR; neither is a convenience the interface grew on its own, and the pattern is worth naming: transcribing an ADR's interface NAMES is not the same as transcribing its contract, and only writing an adapter finds the difference.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.6) The Realtime adapter implements `DuplexVoice` and ONLY that · it does not synthesise a `TextToSpeech` out of the duplex session to look like a complete adapter. ADR-0007 assigns the mapping to the policy layer, and an adapter that faked the other two interfaces would make the policy choose it for capabilities it does not have. `HeraldSession.speakWith` holds the mapping: a `tts` adapter is spoken through, a duplex-only adapter is asked to `say`.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.6) Production supplies the Realtime transport; every test injects it · the SDK's `OpenAIRealtimeWebSocket` needs the runtime's native `WebSocket`, present in Electron's Node and absent under vitest on Node 20.16. With no `connect` the adapter reports `{ok: false, fault: 'transient'}` and refuses to open — an honest, visible degradation the policy can act on (invariant §7) rather than a crash inside the SDK.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.6, DEFECT THE FIRST GREEN SUITE HID) A mid-utterance failure was losing what the provider HAD said · `await handle.done()` rejects, so `spokenSoFar()` was never read and the fallback re-spoke the whole line. Every S-FAILOVER case passed anyway, because "the whole line reached the transcript" is true either way — the suite was asserting the wrong half of "continuous". `speakWith` now credits the partial on the failure path, and the continuity case asserts what the FALLBACK was asked to say (the remainder, not the line). Mutation-checked: dropping the partial credit fails it.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7) The Herald parses the ARCHIVE, not the filing · `narrationOf(markdown)` reads the sentences back out of the artifact `renderBriefMarkdown` wrote, so there is no path by which a sentence absent from the archive could be spoken. Taking a `BriefFiling` instead would have been easier and would have let the spoken brief drift from the written one with nobody noticing until an audit read both. The archive is also the artifact that PASSED `checkNarrative` (SDD §7.2), so speaking anything else would be speaking prose nothing had checked — the E-BRIEF-FAITH failure. The `## Source refs` appendix is excluded: it is the audit trail behind the narration, not part of it.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7) A voice approval needs repeat-back for spend even when the GATE did not ask · FR-8.4 names destructive AND spend; `gate.requiresRepeatBack` is set by gate policy, which is a different question from "may a voice approve this". `voiceApprovalAsk` requires a token when EITHER says so, so a policy that had not marked a spend gate cannot let one through on a bare "yes". A refusal returns the line to say and the reason and leaves the gate open — "I could not confirm you meant it" and "no" are different answers, which is the distinction `GateManager` already draws.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7) The metric-check tick RAISES; it does not measure · SDD §7.6 puts the scheduler in the cycle ("row `landed` ─► scheduler books metric check") and nothing booked one, so a landed change whose check nobody remembered to run quietly counted as a success — the gamed-metric failure ADR-0015 opens by warning about. `gymCadenceTick` logs every due check with its declared metric and raises it; the measured VALUE still arrives through `measure()`, because booking a check and deciding what the number was are different jobs. A check stays due until somebody runs it (the row stays `landed`), which is deliberate: a booking that fired once and forgot would let a missed check disappear, and R2 says the ledger is total.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7) Spend attribution takes a `TokensFor` lookup, not a row list · production passes the `CostLedger`'s own `spendFor(...).cumulativeTotals` (durable, so a restart cannot zero it — invariant §11) and tests pass a table, so both reach the same number by the same route. Two paths to one figure is exactly how a total drifts from the ledger it claims to come from. Attribution is by EXACT role (`isImprovementRole`), so a hire named "process-improver-docs" is not counted; and `slice()` now returns `source` beside the number, because the brief is read ALOUD, where there is no card to hover and a bare total invites trust in a scope the listener cannot see.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7) `CountBadge` moved to its own module · the `odeon:queue` badge and the gate badge obey one rule ("an unknown count must never render as reassurance"), so they share a component; it lives outside `App.tsx` because importing `App` pulls in xterm and Pixi, which need a browser, and chrome carrying a rule worth pinning should be reachable from the M6.1 render harness without booting the shell.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.7, OWED NOT FAKED) The wake word is a SETTING, not a detector · `config.wakeWordEnabled` advertises the mode (FR-8.3) and `activeModes` already handles it, but no local wake-word engine is an approved dependency and shipping one that only pretended to listen would be worse than the gap. Push-to-talk is unaffected — it is always available, so enabling the flag adds a way in and never removes one. Detection is owed to a session that can take the dependency question.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6.8, TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING THE BOOKING ON THE REAL LEDGER) The metric sweep is booked, and nearly was not · `metricChecksDue` matched `^due YYYY-MM-DD$` anchored, and GYM-003's real cell reads `due 2026-09-11 (live-quit evidence owed with the metric check)` — so the ONE row the M6 window singles out was the one row that would never have been raised. Rows a test writes for itself are tidy; a ledger a human maintains is not, which is why the exit review ran the shipped booking against `docs/gymnasium/LEDGER.md` instead of against fixtures. Match relaxed to a `due <date>` prefix with the note kept; regression added quoting the real cell. Second defect, same run: GYM-001's due date (2026-09-25) lived only in its metric prose while its Measured cell was `—`, so it was invisible to the scheduler; the ledger now records it where the booking reads. Re-verified after both: 2026-09-11 raises GYM-002/003/004, 2026-09-25 adds GYM-001.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6.8) Committed floor evidence paints PROCEDURALLY · `docs/demo/m6-floor-v2.svg` renders from the shipped painter, citizen, station-art and overlay modules with no pack installed, because the licensed sheets are not in the repository and evidence that needed them would be evidence CI could not reproduce. The live captures (real Electron app with the LimeZu pack; the shipped FloorCanvas with six citizens) are described in the exit review for what each one is — a real app, and a real renderer with a stubbed bridge — rather than blurred together.

- 2026-08-29 · (M6 CLOSE-OUT AUDIT, ARCHITECT-APPROVED SPEC AMENDMENT) The repeat-back token carries the WHOLE subject, matches EXACTLY, and is single-use · the three-agent close-out audit proved by execution that FR-8.4's safety behaviour did not hold. `repeatBackToken` kept the first three words of the gate's subject, which read as faithful to the clause's own example ("say confirm delete") and was not: `delete branch release/9` and `delete branch release/10` produced one token, and every spend gate collapsed to `confirm raise the daily` — so the AMOUNT, which is the whole subject of a spend approval, was absent from the words approving it. Worse, `checkRepeatBack` used `said.includes(wanted)`, so the spoken refusal "no, do not confirm delete branch release 9" returned `{confirmed: true}`: the refusal quotes the token, and quoting was enough. Put to the Architect as a BUILD-PROMPT §8.3 must-ask with three options; the strongest was chosen. The token now carries the normalized subject entire, the match is exact, and `repeatBackChallenge` issues a nonce with a two-minute lapse so an answer is single-use and a stale asking cannot be answered. FR-8.4 and VOICE-DESIGN §3 were amended with the change rather than after it. The cost is a trailing "please" now failing — deliberately the right side to err on for the only spoken act that cannot be undone. Mutation-checked in both directions.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6 CLOSE-OUT AUDIT) `validateCompositions` is CALLED · its own contract comment said a bad composition "degrades that station to the procedural painter and says so (invariant §7)", and the function had only test callers. The degrading half was real (`compositionFor` returns null); the saying-so half was not, so a pack shipping a wrong-sized station silently lost it. `resolveTileset` now appends the problems to the visible `note`, and the pack still installs — one wrong entry must not cost the whole pack. This is the invariant-§7 failure mode worth naming: a degradation path that works, beside a disclosure path nobody wired.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6 CLOSE-OUT AUDIT) `Gymnasium.slice()` emits `spendSource`, the name the brief READS · the producer returned `source` and `BriefInput.gymSlice` declared `spendSource`; nothing anywhere set it, so the true branch in `brief.ts` was dead and M6.7's "the brief prints both" was false. Object spread bypasses excess-property checking, which is why `typecheck` stayed green through two milestones — and both halves had passing tests, because no test spanned the seam. Renamed at the producer, and the regression now drives the REAL slice into the REAL compiler. The general lesson is the M5b one restated: two correct halves that have never met are not a feature.
- 2026-08-29 · (M6 CLOSE-OUT AUDIT) `.gitattributes` added; three files renormalized to LF · `docs/PROGRESS.md` entered the index with CRLF at `5b9ff87` and stayed that way for the rest of M6, so the milestone's diff read as 6582 changed lines where 248 had changed. Nothing was wrong with the content — I verified the only real deletions were the nine `- [ ]` boxes becoming `- [x]` — but PROGRESS.md is the document a close-out audit reads BY DIFF, and a record that cannot be reviewed by diff is where a substantive edit to an earlier milestone would hide. `core.autocrlf` is per-machine and cannot be relied on; `.gitattributes` travels with the repository.

- 2026-08-30 · (ARCHITECT DECISION) **M6.9 — wiring the Herald into the application — is DEFERRED INDEFINITELY** · the Herald is not an important function for the Architect right now, so the effort belongs elsewhere. Recorded as a deferral rather than a descope, which matters for three reasons. (1) The close-out audit's finding is untouched: 1 406 lines under `src/main/herald/` still have no production caller, and the record still says so. Nothing is being quietly reclassified as fine. (2) Nothing is wasted — the ADR-0007 seam, the policy, both adapters, the session and the narration are built, tested, conforming and audited; they are a library waiting for a caller, and M6.9 remains the fix whenever it is called for. (3) The consequence is stated instead of absorbed: all three of M6's exit criteria in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` are voice-live (SRS §6.2 live, §6.5 live, a full day driven by voice), so with M6.9 deferred they are unreachable by construction and M6 cannot close on its criteria as written. That is now an OPEN ARCHITECT DECISION — amend the criteria, waive them on the record, or hold M6 open — and it blocks the M7 start under BUILD-PROMPT §5. The alternative, silently dropping the criteria so the milestone could be ticked, is exactly the failure the close-out audit was convened to catch.

- 2026-08-30 · (M6.10, ARCHITECT-APPROVED DEPENDENCY) `jsdom@^26` added as a DEV dependency · a BUILD-PROMPT §8.3 must-ask, put to the Architect with three options for closing C1 and answered "both" — extract the pure reducers AND mount the component. The M6.1 harness deliberately avoided a DOM library (`react-dom/server` static markup, "no DOM library added to the tree"), and that was right for asserting a panel's body; it cannot mount a component with effects, so `FloorCanvas` had no coverage of any kind and the close-out audit broke its two §5.4 facts with every suite green. **Pinned to 26, not latest:** jsdom 30 fails on this toolchain's Node 20 — `html-encoding-sniffer` does a CJS `require()` of an ESM `@exodus/bytes`, so vitest cannot even start the worker. jsdom 26 declares `node >=18` and runs clean. Dev-only: `npm audit --omit=dev` reports 0 vulnerabilities, and the 2 high-severity dev findings are the pre-existing electron/extract-zip pair recorded at M6.5 — jsdom added none. Only `test/renderer/floor-canvas.test.tsx` opts in, via `// @vitest-environment jsdom`, so the rest of the suite is untouched and still runs with no DOM.
- 2026-08-30 · (M6.10) `FloorCanvas`'s three refs became one `FloorState` · the component kept `avatarsRef`, `mailRef` and a `factsRef` side by side and folded them inline, which is why the renderer half of §5.4's named facts was untestable — the fold had no name and no module. `floor/facts.ts` now owns it as pure reducers (`noteAvatar`, `noteGates`, `noteMeeting`, `factsOf`), the component holds one ref, and the two properties the audit's mutations broke are pinned by name. The general shape is worth keeping: when a component's logic cannot be tested, the fix is usually to give the logic a home rather than to give the component a harness — and here it turned out to be both.

- 2026-08-30 · (ARCHITECT DECISION) **M6's exit criteria AMENDED, and the milestone closed on the mechanical bar** · all three original criteria were live-voice demonstrations (SRS §6.2 spoken, SRS §6.5 key-pulled, a full day driven by voice), and every one depends on the Herald being reachable from the application. With M6.9 deferred indefinitely they were unreachable *by construction*, so BUILD-PROMPT §5 would have held M6 open forever against a bar nobody intended to meet. The Architect's instruction — close M6 once every test and conformance suite passes — is recorded here as an amendment to the MILESTONE GATE and nothing more. **SRS §6.2 and §6.5 are untouched in the SRS**: they are system-level v1 acceptance criteria, they were not demonstrated, they are not waived, and they now ride with M6.9 wherever it lands. The distinction is the whole point: a milestone may close with work deferred, and may not close by deleting the bar it failed — which is the failure the M6 close-out audit was convened to catch, and it would be a poor joke to commit it in the act of closing that audit out. Verified in isolation on `4d831e4` so no result is a load artefact: 19/20 scenario suites, 3/3 conformance suites, 14/14 M6 unit suites; Ubuntu CI green. The 9 local deterministic failures are the recorded Windows-local baseline and touch nothing in M6 — green on the gate, not green on the laptop.
- 2026-08-30 · (FOUND WHILE CLOSING, recorded not fixed) **`main` is not actually protected** · ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §2 says *"`main` is protected: PRs only, CI green, one review"*, and `gh api repos/.../branches/main/protection` returns `404 Branch not protected`. The rule is real in prose and absent in the mechanism — the same shape as the invariant-§7 gap the close-out audit found in `validateCompositions`, where the degrading half was wired and the saying-so half was not. Nothing has violated it (every merge so far went through a PR), but nothing would stop a direct push either. Enabling protection is a repository-settings action, not a code change, so it is the Architect's to take; ADR-0020 will need it genuinely enforced before M7b lets a machine account near the repo.

- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) `hires/*.json` REUSES the shipped `hireTemplateSchema` rather than inventing a profile-local one · M5.6 shipped `hireTemplateSchema` in `src/shared/org.ts` as "hire templates as versioned files (`profiles/`-adjacent per SDD §4.1 `hire` refs)" — and `grep` found only test callers for it, the same shape as the M6 finding. ADR-0012's bundle listing describes the same file, so writing a second schema would have created two ways to say what a role is and one registry row (`"hire": { "template", "version" }`) that could descend from either. M7.1 gives the M5.6 validator its first production caller instead. ADR-0012's word "skills" maps to the shipped `capabilities`, which is the registry's own field name (SDD §4.1): one word for one concept beats a faithful transcription that would need translating at every activation.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) `hireTemplateSchema` gained an OPTIONAL `budget`, without a `schemaVersion` bump · two normative documents put a budget in the hire template and the shipped schema had none: FR-9.1 lists "budgets" among what a mission profile declares, and ADR-0012 spells the file out as "role templates: engine, prompt, skills, env grants, budget". Leaving it out would have pushed the number into activation code, where it stops being a reviewable line in the file the Architect reads *before* activating — which is the whole reason ADR-0012 chose declarative bundles. Optional for the reason `spawnRequestSchema.budget` is optional: an unbudgeted hire is legal and shows as `unbudgeted`, not as a zero the Watch would read as an instant breach. No bump because the change is additive and no hire-template file existed anywhere on disk — every document written against the previous shape still validates. Recorded rather than assumed, because "additive, so it is free" is exactly the reasoning that ships an unversioned schema change.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) A profile's autonomy carries LEVELS, never gate RULES · SDD §9 is exact — "profile autonomy levels can only *loosen* up to global maxima — stricter wins" — so `profile.json` gets a `default` plus per-`GateKind` overrides and nothing else. It deliberately cannot carry `gateRuleSchema`'s `channels`, `maxSpendTokens` or `requireRepeatBack`: a bundle able to name its own approval CHANNELS could grant itself remote approval of a destructive act, and since profiles are shareable (FR-10.4) that field would eventually arrive from somebody else's file. `byKind` is `.strict()` over the six known kinds so a typo'd `"destructve": "manual"` is refused instead of silently falling through to a laxer default — the silent privilege escalation M7.2's risk line warns about, caught one package early.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) Loading is READ-ONLY — the store does NOT seed the home copy from a built-in · `PromptStore.read()` seeds on first read and that is right for prompts, where the editable copy must be the one actually read. It is wrong here for two reasons: the package owes "loading is pure — no activation side effects", and a silently seeded copy would shadow the built-in *forever*, so the next Ephesus shipping a corrected Skeleton Crew would not be the one running. Home still wins when a copy exists; making that copy is the Architect's explicit act, and `list()` says `source: "home"` when it has happened. Proved by running the real app against a temp `EPH_HOME`: after a boot that listed and inspected both bundles, the profiles tree was byte-for-byte unchanged.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) "Cron-like" is an INTERVAL, not a cron expression · ADR-0012 says "schedules (cron-like)" and the shipped `scheduler.ts` takes `Trigger.everyMs` with idempotent ticks (SDD §1.1). A real cron parser is a new dependency — a BUILD-PROMPT §8.3 must-ask — bought for a precision ("every Tuesday at 03:00") that nothing in the SRS asks for. Floored at one minute, in the schema rather than in the scheduler: the guard belongs where the number is written, not in the loop that would merely absorb it.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) The migration ladder is empty at v1 and the WALK is exercised anyway · ADR-0012 requires "a migration path from day one", and at v1 there is no older document to migrate — so a ladder with one fake entry would be a lie and a ladder with a "// TODO: migrations" comment would be untested machinery. `migrateProfileDocument` takes its ladder and target version as parameters, so the production call passes the empty ladder while a test walks a synthetic two-step one and asserts the steps ran in order. All four refusals are named and tested: no integer version, a version newer than this build understands (refused rather than downgraded, because dropping fields you cannot see is data loss wearing compatibility's clothes), a gap in the ladder (named, so the missing migration is something somebody can go and write), and a non-object. A mechanism whose only test is "the empty case does nothing" is a mechanism nobody has run.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) `harbor.json`'s channels and webhooks are schema'd with NOWHERE to put a credential · only `repos` has a consumer in M7 (M7.3's `gh` ingestion, FR-10.1); channels and webhook endpoints are M7b's bridge. They are schema'd now because ADR-0012 names all three and a field added later costs a migration — but as ids, names and event kinds only. No URL and no token has a field to live in, because a profile is shareable by design and a bundle with somewhere to put a credential is a bundle that will eventually be exported carrying one. A webhook entry names the EVENT it carries, not an address: the endpoint is one the harness exposes (FR-10.2's inbound webhooks), so an outbound URL here would be a profile telling the company where to send its data.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1) `profile.json` has no description, title or icon · ADR-0012's listing is "name, version, target binding, autonomy levels", and the risk line for this package says transcribe it, do not extend it. The activation UI's "what this profile MAY do" (M7.2) is computed from the hires, grants, triggers and autonomy the bundle actually carries; a prose blurb beside those facts would be a second place for the bundle to describe itself, and the one a reader would believe over the mechanism.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.1, FOUND WHILE STAGING — recorded, guard NOT freelanced) Two NUL bytes made two new test files unreviewable by diff · `git diff --cached --stat` reported `test/main/profiles.test.ts | Bin 0 -> 10559 bytes`. The file was valid UTF-8 and its 12 cases passed; it carried a single `\x00` where a space belonged, inside a `.DS_Store` fixture, and git's binary heuristic (a NUL in the first 8000 bytes) then classed the whole file as binary. A second one sat in `test/shared/profile.test.ts`. Both replaced with spaces; a sweep of `src/`, `test/` and `docs/` now finds none. This is the M6 `.gitattributes` finding in a new costume: nothing was wrong with the CONTENT, and the record could not be reviewed by diff — which is precisely where a substantive edit hides from a close-out audit. A NUL-byte check belongs in `scripts/check-invariants.cjs` beside the CRLF pin, but adding a CI gate ad hoc is the process change ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §3 forbids without an approved Gymnasium proposal, so it is RAISED to the Architect rather than written. Noted too: the suite was green in both directions with the NUL present, so no test could ever have caught this — only the diff did.

- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) A target is a REF plus a PATH, and the Architect names the path · no document says how `repo:myapp` resolves to a directory on this machine. Rather than invent a target registry or infer a checkout, activation takes `{ kind, id, path }`: the ref is what the registry records (SDD §4.1) and travels between machines, the path is local and is what the agents get as `cwd`. This is the posture `agents:spawn` already has — the renderer names a directory, main validates it — so it adds no new surface and no new trust. A harness that guessed the path would be guessing which of the Architect's checkouts they meant.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) `GateManager` RESOLVES the profile's autonomy itself; call sites do not pass it · `GateRequest.profileAutonomy` and `effectivePolicy` shipped at M3 and, on inspection, had no production caller anywhere — the composition was correct arithmetic nothing could reach, the M6 finding one subsystem over. The fix was not to add the field at each `submit()` call but to inject a `profileAutonomy(agentId, kind)` lookup into the manager, because a field the caller must remember is a field that gets forgotten — and forgetting it is not fail-safe: an agent whose profile TIGHTENED a class would silently get the looser company default, which is an escalation relative to the plan the Architect approved at activation. When both a caller and a profile have an opinion, the stricter is taken; composition can only ever narrow.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) `preview()` returns the SAME plan `activate()` executes · ADR-0012's safety argument is that an Architect can read what a profile may do before trusting it. If the activation screen computed its preview separately from the code that acts, the two would drift, and the one screen that exists to be believed would be the one thing nothing checks. `activationPlan` is therefore a pure function of (bundle, target, global autonomy) and both paths call it. It also carries `clamped` per class, so a bundle that ASKED for more than the company allows is visible as having asked — a fact about the bundle, not noise.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) Activation is ALL OR NOTHING · a hire that fails to spawn unwinds the agents already up and refuses the instance. A partially activated crew is worse than none: the Architect approved a plan containing an on-call agent and has no reason to think that agent is missing. The refusal names the hire, the engine error, and how many agents were killed on the way out.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) Ids are deterministic and REFUSE rather than truncate · `<profile>@<kind>:<target>` for an instance and `agent.<profile>-<target>-<hire>` for an agent. Deterministic is what makes FR-9.4 work in both directions — two targets give two ids so the same profile activates twice, and one target gives the same id so a duplicate is detectable. Carrying both the profile and the target is what keeps two crews out of one inbox (`AgentManager` keys live agents by id and `agora/agents/<id>/` is a directory). An id over 64 characters returns null and the activation is refused, because truncation is precisely how two agents come to share one.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) Event triggers are RECORDED, not armed — and the instance says so · `webhook`, `ci` and `health` bindings have no publisher yet (the Harbor's `gh` ingestion is M7.3, the webhook endpoint M7.4), and `scheduler.ts` only takes intervals. They are surfaced on the instance as `pendingEvents` so the subscriber that arrives can find them and so the gap is a listed fact rather than a watcher the Architect believes is on duty. The mutation sweep found the one hole here: an event trigger could be reported in `armed` while nothing armed it, because no assertion compared what the instance CLAIMED against what the scheduler was actually given. Closed.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) Hires spawn in sorted file-name order · `parseProfile` sorts the bundle's `hires/*.json` by file name, so activation's spawn order is deterministic rather than whatever `readdir` returned. Not cosmetic: a varying order makes the all-or-nothing unwind test flaky and drifts the order agents appear in the ledger. Recorded because it is a real ordering contract a built-in profile may want to lean on, and nothing in ADR-0012 says which hire comes up first.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.2) Four `profiles:` channels beyond SDD §5's abridged list, documented in the same commit · `preview` and `instances` join `activate`/`deactivate` under the M3.1 rule ("a new channel gets a doc line and a DECISIONS-LOG entry, or it does not ship"); SDD §5's `profiles:` block was rewritten to name all six and to record that `activate` takes a request object carrying the target's path. `preview` is not a convenience — it is the screen ADR-0012's safety story rests on.

- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) The Harbor holds NO token, and there is no field for one · FR-10.1 says the company acts "via the `gh` CLI under the agent's own auth", so `GitHubHarbor` has no token option, reads no environment variable, and its `GhRunner` seam takes args and a timeout only — no env parameter for a credential to arrive through. The absence is the design, asserted by API surface the way the broker's write-only-ness is: a Harbor that ACCEPTED a token would be a Harbor an imported profile (FR-10.4) could hand one to, and ADR-0010 exists so no code path holds a credential it did not need. The suite reads the module's own `GitHubHarborOptions` block and fails on the words `token`, `secret`, `auth` or `env` appearing in it.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) A malformed `gh` row is DROPPED AND COUNTED, never repaired · the package's risk line calls ingestion that invents a task "the E-BRIEF-FAITH failure wearing a Harbor hat", and it is the same defect at two ends of the building: a queue listing an issue nobody opened and a briefing narrating a bug nobody filed. So a row missing a number or a title does not become `#0 ""` — it is dropped, the count travels OUT with the good items, and the count raises a visible degradation. Reporting "12 open issues" when 3 rows were unreadable is a false statement, so the number the UI needs to qualify it cannot be left behind in a log line.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) A CI run in flight has a NULL conclusion, and `isCiFailure` is deliberately narrow · only a FINISHED run concluding `failure` or `timed_out` counts. A run still going is not an incident, and a `cancelled` one is a human's decision rather than a fault — a babysitter (FR-9.2) treating either as a failure would open PRs against work somebody is deliberately stopping. Guessing a conclusion for an empty string was a real temptation here because `gh` returns `""` rather than null for some runs; it is mapped to null.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) A `gh` failure fails the WHOLE repo, and never empties a queue · `RepoQueue.failure` is a field rather than an absence, so a repository whose call errored and one with genuinely nothing open cannot look alike — the second is fine and the first means the company is blind (invariant §7). A failure part-way through also fails the whole repo rather than keeping the earlier kinds: a board showing issues but silently no CI runs looks CLEAN to the babysitter, which is worse than a named outage. And a failed ingestion keeps what the Harbor last knew instead of replacing it with emptiness, because turning a degradation into a false all-clear is the exact shape of the bug.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) The `remote` tag is a PROJECTION of the items, not a call somebody remembers to make · FR-10.3 requires every remote-originated item to be tagged in `log.jsonl`. `remoteLogEntries(items)` is a pure function of what was ingested, so an item that reached the queue without reaching the log would have to be one that function never saw. The mutation sweep found the one hole this left: failing only the FIRST `gh` call leaves nothing in flight, so nothing tested whether a LATER failure would still log the items collected before it — half a repo tagged `remote` that the queue never showed. Closed.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3) Registered repos come from the ACTIVE profiles, not a second list · `harbor.json` (M7.1's schema) declares them and `activations.instances()` (M7.2) says which are live, so the company watches exactly what it was pointed at. A separate Harbor registry would be a second source of truth that could disagree with the bundle the Architect approved. Ingestion runs on a 10-minute scheduler cadence whose `enabled()` predicate is false while no profile watches anything — a cadence firing against an empty list would still shell out to `gh` forever for nothing.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.3, PROVED AGAINST THE REAL API) The parsers were verified against real `gh --json` output, not fixtures · the schemas were written from an assumption about what `gh issue/pr/run list --json` returns, and a fixture would have re-tested the assumption rather than the API. The built app was pointed at `mertefesensoy/Ephesus` with the real CLI: `gh 2.92.0`, 50 items, **0 dropped**, 50 `remote` entries. Zero rows needed repairing, so the shapes are right. The `.loose()` on each row schema is why a future `gh` release adding a field will not read as an outage — every field READ is declared, and the rest is ignored and never carried.

- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4, A DOCUMENT GAP RESOLVED THE MINIMAL WAY — ARCHITECT DECISION OWED) **The severity ladder has TWO rungs, because the documents name one** · a search of the whole `docs/` tree finds exactly one severity in existence: "the Herald can announce a **severity-1** aloud immediately" (SRS UC-09 step 4; SDD §7.5 repeats it). Nothing enumerates a scale, and FR-9.2 nevertheless requires "severity-based escalation" and the M7.4 line requires the table to be TOTAL. The documents do describe exactly two *treatments* — announce now, and the ordinary UC-08 path batched into the next brief — so the ladder transcribes those and adds no rung the documents do not imply. Recorded as a decision the Architect may want to take differently, because changing it changes a SPEC CLAUSE rather than code. It is cheap on purpose: `escalationFor` is one total function over one array, so a four-rung ladder is a four-row table and nothing else in the system moves. Totality is enforced by a mapped type rather than a `default:` branch — adding a rung without deciding its escalation fails to compile, where a default case would have accepted one and silently given it the MILDEST treatment, which is the wrong direction for a safety ladder to fail in.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4) **The harness never assigns a severity, and never writes `tasks.json`** · two refusals that shape the whole incident path. UC-09 step 2 gives triage to the on-call agent, so `src/shared/incident.ts` contains no classifier, no keyword list and no heuristic that could become one — the escalation table is driven by the severity an agent REPORTED, and an unreadable report is refused rather than defaulted (defaulting would turn "we could not read how bad this is" into "this is not very bad"). And `LedgerEndpoint.submit` takes a `Message` whose writer check lives in Hermes's router: only the orchestrator's `propose` is ever applied (FR-5.2, FR-4.2). So SDD §7.5's "on-call agent task (auto)" means without the ARCHITECT, not without ARTEMIS — a CI failure is mailed to her from `agent.harbor` carrying the ingested facts and nothing added, and she proposes the task. S-PROFILE asserts the negative directly: after `raise`, the ledger is unchanged.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4, THE DOGFOOD CLAIM SURVIVED INTACT) **The Skeleton Crew needed no field M7.1's frozen schema lacks** · ADR-0012 stakes its central claim on the built-ins exercising no private API, and the package's risk line says a built-in that reaches past the schema invalidates it. Every FR-9.2 component turned out expressible as written: the health watcher and dependency updater are hires with `schedule` triggers, the CI babysitter is a hire with an `event: "ci"` trigger, and severity-based escalation is prose in `incident.md` (judgment) beside the harness's table (mechanism). The temptation was a `severity` field in the bundle; it was unnecessary, because severity is assigned at TRIAGE time, not declared at activation time — and taking it would have meant amending a public schema three packages after freezing it. `test/main/skeleton-crew.test.ts` now checks the claim rather than restating it: it runs against the REAL shipped bundle through the REAL loader, and one case pins the directory listing so a private sidecar cannot be added quietly.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4, AN INERT SUBSYSTEM FOUND AND WIRED) **`onTriggerFired` appended a log line and stopped** · M7.2 armed profile schedule triggers correctly and the production handler did nothing with them, so the health watcher and the dependency updater were spawned and then never asked for anything — two of FR-9.2's four components inert, behind a suite that was entirely green. Exactly the recorded M6 failure, one milestone later. The fix wakes the agent with mail from a new `agent.profiles` endpoint, naming the runbook and never summarizing it; and the message composition was EXTRACTED to `triggerWakeMessage` in `src/main/profiles.ts` rather than left inline in `index.ts`, because logic that lives only in boot wiring is logic no test can reach. That is the M6.10 shape restated: when a component's logic cannot be tested, give the logic a home.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4, TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY WRITING THE TESTS) **`'reproduce'` contains `'prod'`** · the E-PLAYBOOK scorer matched action names by SUBSTRING against the gated-action list, so an agent following `incident.md` step 3 scored as having taken an un-gated production action. This is the M6 repeat-back defect's exact shape — a substring match standing in for an identity check — found here by a test asserting the scorer discriminates, which is why evals get discrimination tests rather than only happy-path ones. Actions are now a closed vocabulary compared by equality, with free prose riding alongside where nothing matches on it. Second defect, same session: `agent.sk-<target>-<hire>`, an abbreviation used only in test fixtures, matches `check-invariants`'s `sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,}` OpenAI-key pattern. The invariant was right and the naming was wrong — production mints `agent.skeleton-crew-myapp-…`, which is what the tests now use.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4) **A severity-1's announcement is RECORDED AS OWED, never faked** · UC-09 step 4 says the Herald announces a severity-1 immediately, and M6.9 is deferred indefinitely by Architect decision, so `src/main/herald/` gains no production caller here. The obligation is therefore logged (`incident-announce-owed`, `because: "herald-unwired"`) and reported through the ordinary degradation channel (invariant §7), while the gate queue takes the escalation through SDD §9's already-wired `needs_human` choke point. The two are kept distinct on purpose: opening a gate is not a spoken announcement, and letting it stand in for one would be the milestone quietly declaring a deferred capability satisfied. Mutation M9 — replacing the report with a silent `void what` — turns S-PROFILE red, so the disclosure leg is load-bearing rather than decorative.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4) **Two reserved endpoints, not one** · `agent.harbor` carries incidents in and triage reports back; `agent.profiles` carries scheduled trigger wakes. Conflating them would make the book of record say a routine 15-minute health sweep arrived through the port from outside the company. Both follow the M3 standing rule for harness-owned correspondents (`agent.closing` is the model): never spawned, never a mailbox, reply-shaped acts only. The router branch matters more than it looks — Hermes's endpoint dispatch ends in an unconditional `else this.submitToLedger(...)`, so an endpoint route with no matching arm becomes a LEDGER submission and answers a correctly-filed triage report with a ledger refusal, with no crash and no warning. S-PROFILE files its report through a real spawned `fake-engine` and `hermes.sweep()` for exactly this reason; a test calling `onTriage` directly would have skipped both edits that make the reply reachable.
- 2026-08-31 · (M7.4) **The built-in ships watching no repository** · `harbor.json` carries `repos: []`. A built-in shipped with somebody else's remote in it would ingest from a repository the Architect never registered; they add theirs, which is "buildable by an Architect with a text editor" in the one place the phrase has consequences. The cost is that a freshly activated Skeleton Crew's CI babysitter has nothing to watch until the file is edited, which is honest rather than surprising — and is flagged in the session report as a shipping posture worth confirming.

- 2026-08-31 · (M7.5, ARCHITECT DECISION ON A BUILD-PROMPT §8.3 MUST-ASK) **`outbound` is a SEVENTH gate kind, and M5.3's borrow rule is qualified rather than broken** · M5.3 recorded the rule that a new concern borrows an existing `GATE_KINDS` member instead of adding one, and justified it because "the trigger itself is on the gate, so **the mapping loses nothing**". That qualifier is what fails here. FR-9.3 requires the Front Office's reply autonomy to be configurable on its own ladder (draft-only → auto-post), and the nearest existing kind is `prod-facing`; borrowing it would mean an Architect who wants the company answering issues unattended has, in the same setting, granted it autonomous production actions, with no way to express "may reply, may not touch prod".
