ADR-0033's rule is that only a human may authorise what the company is not otherwise allowed to do; **a ceiling does not allow anything, it caps**, so lowering one is squarely a script's to make and raising one is the same shape as widening the autonomy ceiling. `unbudgeted` is treated as the TOP of the order rather than as zero (ADR-0029 ships it), so the first ceiling is always a tightening however large. Equal is allowed and says `unchanged`, because a setup script that cannot read the current value first must still be able to assert a ceiling idempotently. There is deliberately no verb for the autonomy ceiling: raising it is what §6.1's last clause measures.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.1 — TWO THINGS THE HANDLER MUST NEVER DO, both mutation-checked) **It patches the ceiling only, and it will not write while the policy file is unreadable** · `saveGateCeilings` writes BOTH company-wide ceilings, so a handler that rebuilt the object rather than spreading `ceilingsOf(view)` would move the autonomy dial nobody asked it to. And `GatePolicyView.warning` is non-null exactly when the harness is running on `denyAllPolicy`: saving then would persist that fallback's `manual` as though somebody had chosen it, which is a degradation written back as a setting — invariant §7's exact failure, in the file the README calls the company-wide autonomy ceiling.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.1 — A MUTANT THAT SURVIVED, and it was a finding rather than a control) **Nothing guarded the CLI line that makes `EXIT-M8` §2 performable** · The round planted "§2 stops naming the verb" expecting it to survive as a second control, and it did — which means the line could be dropped from the script and every test would stay green. That is M8c.10's own defect one section along: a sentence nobody re-reads deciding the outcome of an hour. Four assertions now pin it — the verb is named, the window path is still there for a runner who has one, and the section says the verb may only lower — and the mutant dies.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.1 — AN ORDERING THE FIX DOES NOT ADDRESS, recorded so it is not read as an oversight) **§2 sets the ceiling AFTER §1 has already hired the crew** · The exit run's Finding 5 shows the first ingest raising eight incidents within two minutes of activation, which is before a runner following the script in order would have set anything. So a reachable verb bounds the hour but not the cold start. That is M8c.2 (raise nothing for a run older than the activation) and M8c.3 (a default ceiling, or a first-run confirmation naming projected spend) — the packages that own the ordering — rather than something this one should have moved.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.2 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **Both cold-start rules, not either: newer than the activation, PLUS the newest failure when it still stands** · The record offered them as alternatives and noted they differ on a repository that is red at the moment you activate. Newer-than-activation alone goes SILENT there — nothing raises until somebody pushes again, which reads as a healthy company watching a broken repo. Superseded-alone still replays every failure of a two-week red patch with no green since. Together they bound the cold start absolutely and still notice a repository that is broken right now, exactly once, and one per red branch rather than one per run. "Overtaken" is deliberately ANY later run rather than only a later success: a newer run means somebody pushed again, and if it failed too then IT is the one that still stands. Nothing is lost to an in-flight successor either — if it fails, the next ingest sees a failure newer than the activation.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.2 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2) **`watchingSince` is the instance's own `activatedAt`, carried on the binding, not a clock read at ingest** · ADR-0027 restores an instance with its `activatedAt` intact, so after a restart the company has been watching since the ORIGINAL activation. A fresh `Date.now()` would recategorise a real downtime's failures as history and drop them, which is the opposite defect to the one being fixed. Optional on the binding, and absent means everything counts as new — the additive direction, so a caller written before M8c.2 keeps the behaviour it had rather than silently gaining a filter.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.2 — THE BRANCH WAS THERE ALL ALONG, under another name) **`branchOf` exists because `parseRuns` files `headBranch` in `labels`** · A consumer looking for the branch searches for `branch` and finds nothing — the "absence in one vocabulary is not absence" mistake this repository made three times on 2026-09-07. The producer's choice is fine; what was missing is a name the consumer can use. Naming it once also makes it mutation-checkable, and M9 (read `labels[1]`) dies.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.2 — TWO GAPS THE ROUND FOUND BY PLANTING MUTANTS EXPECTED TO SURVIVE) **An expected survival is a hypothesis, not a licence** · The first round scored 8 of 8 with two planted "controls" that survived: dropping the same-second `ref` tiebreak, and removing the incident board's explicit skip of the new event. Both survivals were real gaps rather than certificates — nothing tested two runs created in the same second, and nothing tested what the board does with an `incident-superseded` row. Writing the two cases turned both into mutants that die. The board's explicit `continue` was then DELETED rather than kept: the row names an incident the board never saw raised, so the fall-through already drops it, and a line that changes no behaviour is the shape of a check that cannot fail. The property is now a test, which is what makes it survive a future change to the row's shape.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.2 — AN ORDERING THAT IS LOAD-BEARING) **Dedupe runs BEFORE the cold-start rule** · A run raised on one ingest can be overtaken by the next; reporting it superseded then would file a "nothing happened" row against an incident the company already has. Reversing the two is a mutant that dies, and the row carries the incident key it did NOT raise so a reader who grepped for that key finds out why nothing came back.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3 — ARCHITECT DECISION, and it was neither of the two filed options) **The ceiling becomes a QUESTION the grant must answer, rather than a default or a notice** · The filing offered a shipped default ceiling or a first-run confirmation naming projected spend. A default would bound every run and would also let a company stop at a figure nobody chose, quietly, mid-hour — trading one silent behaviour for another, and needing an ADR to supersede ADR-0029. A confirmation that names a number and starts anyway changes nothing mechanically: the step that is skipped stays skippable, which is the whole finding. So `unbudgeted` stays the shipped default and ADR-0029 is untouched; what it stops being is something a company can START on by omission. `grant()` refuses an unanswered ceiling and names both ways out by exact command, and the Architect may still run unbudgeted — it just has to be an answer.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3 — WHY THE TERMS VERSION MOVED, and it is the mechanism working) **v1's disclosure asked nothing about spend, so a v1 grant is not an answer to v2's question** · ADR-0032 gave consent a `terms` version precisely so a changed question is re-asked rather than silently inherited. Without the bump the one machine that has already consented is the one machine this package does not reach. A company already RUNNING is never re-interrogated — the check is skipped once `mayStartWork` is true, so `grant()` keeps the idempotence a second window depends on.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3 — A MUTATION ROUND THAT KILLED 7 OF 10, and all three survivors were real) **Seven of ten is not a score to report; it is a list of things nobody had tested** · The terms bump was asserted nowhere, so a v1 grant could have carried straight through. The CLI's `--unbudgeted` flag was parsed and could have been discarded by the handler with no test noticing. And the flag's coercion read as a PRESENCE rather than a VALUE in the mutant, which would turn an explicit `--unbudgeted no` into permission to spend without a ceiling. Three tests later all three die. The round is worth more when it fails.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2) **`--unbudgeted` takes a VALUE rather than being a bare flag** · `ephctl` refuses a flag with no value, and that refusal is worth more than the keystroke: `--profile --target repo:x` is a missing value, not two bare flags, and a client that guessed would answer it with a puzzle. Typing `true` is also an affirmative act, which is the point of this particular flag. The schema accepts `true`/`'true'` and nothing else — `'yes'`, `'1'`, `'TRUE'` and `''` are all refused, so a typo cannot become a permission.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.4 — A FIFTH VERDICT, rather than folding the state into one that exists) **`STARTED, UNFINISHED` — because an entered pipeline is neither working nor unasked** · Three options were weighed and two are lies. `working` is the defect itself. `not-exercised` says "nothing has happened either way" about an area that visibly started, which is a smaller lie in the same direction. `broken` would be worse: a pipeline between raising and triaging is not a fault, and a report that cried broken on every one would train a reader to skip the column — the failure `waitingWhen` already exists to prevent for `unbudgeted`. `Verdict` has no consumers outside `diagnosis.ts`, so the cost is one `MARK` row and one summary block. The row is reported BEFORE the unexercised count because it is the sharper news: in flight or stuck, rather than merely unasked.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.4 — TWO PROBES THE FINDING DID NOT NAME, found by reading the table against the log's own vocabulary) **`watching a repository` was proven by a script, and `spend` could never be proven at all** · Since M8.14 `kind: "remote"` carries the Harbor's ingest AND one row per `ephctl` act, so the bare kind matched a `consent:grant` and the Harbor read WORKING because a script had run. The exit run's own runner walked into the same trap and wrote down the fix — "the correct probe is `"inbound":"ci-run"`" — while the report was making the same mistake one layer away. `provenWhere` exists for that: a predicate on the matched row, for where a kind alone is ambiguous. And `spend` proved on `cost`, which is absent from `LOG_KINDS` and always was, so that probe could not match anything ever written. **A check that cannot pass is the same defect as a check that cannot fail, wearing the other face.**
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.4 — WHAT THE REPORT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO) **It does not count rows or attribute refusals, even though the finding's ideal sentence does** · Finding 7's expected line was `incidents | BROKEN | 8 raised, 0 triaged, 8 task-opens refused by the ledger (seq 90–105)`. Counting and attributing is the incident BOARD's job — `incident-view.ts` folds exactly that — and doing it here would make two readings of one record that can disagree, which is what produced the consent row's own contradiction at M8.13. `DIAGNOSIS.md` stays a reading of the degradation channel and the log, adding no state of its own.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.4 — THE THIRD ROUND IN A ROW WHOSE SURVIVOR WAS A MISSING TEST) **The orchestrator's corrected row was untested, so the correction could have been reverted silently** · M8c.2's tiebreak, M8c.3's terms bump and the CLI flag, and now this. In none of the four was the survivor an equivalent mutant. The pattern is worth naming: when a package corrects a TABLE, the rows it did not have to argue about are the ones nobody writes a case for.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.5 — ONE CONDITION PER FILE, rather than one message naming both) **The dedupe key must be injective over the subjects it reports** · The acceptance allowed either. Per-file keeps each condition's own `count` and `since`, matches the six per-subject causes the codebase already has (`settings/restore:<path>`, `restart/orphan-block:<taskId>`, `secrets/missing-grant:<agentId>`, `budgets/state:<agentId>`, `agents/tool-grants:<agentId>`, `restart/draftless-gate:<gateId>`), and degrades correctly if a third file is ever seeded — a concatenated message grows without bound and eventually truncates, which is the same defect one size larger. **`home/seeded-config` was the only loop in `index.ts` breaking a rule the rest of the file already keeps**, established by reading every `reportDegradation` call rather than by fixing only the one the finding named.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.5 — WHY NO GENERAL MECHANISM) **A ring that warned on "same cause, different detail" would cry wolf** · It would catch this class automatically and fire on every legitimate condition whose detail carries a changing count or error string. The rule is a convention with six examples and now a seventh; a mechanism nobody could trust would be worse than the convention. What IS mechanical is the test: the causes are asserted as a SET, so two files collapsing to one key fails.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.5 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2) **The `home/` prefix is preserved, and it is load-bearing** · `PROBES` in `diagnosis.ts` matches an area's `sources` against the part of the cause before the slash. A key that stopped starting with `home/` would move the condition off the row that reports it — a fix that hid the thing it was fixing. Asserted directly, and the mutant that drops the prefix dies.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.6 — TWO LINES, both always printed, rather than one merged list) **`armed` could not carry the answer the reader was told to look for** · `armed` means "has a clock running", so `armed ∩ events = ∅` by construction — an event trigger is not merely unlisted, it is UNREPRESENTABLE on that line whatever its value. That is a different class from a badly-worded message, and it is why the fix is a partition rather than a rewording. A single merged `triggers` line would have fixed the invisibility and lost the distinction that matters operationally: a schedule fires on a clock this process owns, an event trigger fires when the world does something, and a runner debugging silence needs to know which they are looking at. The empty case is the point — the ABSENCE of a line is what two runners read as the absence of a trigger, so neither line is ever omitted.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.6 — THE PLAN IS THE SOURCE, `armed` is the check) **A declared schedule the scheduler is not holding now says NOT ARMED** · Schedules are read off `plan.triggers` because that is where both kinds live; `armed` is consulted only to mark one the clock does not hold. That state is the REAL version of what §5.1 worries about and was previously indistinguishable from a trigger that was never declared — and it is exactly the state a restored-but-not-reactivated instance is in (ADR-0027). The scheduler's ids are instance-qualified and the plan's are bare; the two vocabularies meet inside the renderer rather than in a caller that would have to know both, which is the shape of the `when === 'ci'` defect that cost the incident path its whole production life.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.6 — A DOC GUARD THAT MATCHED SOMETHING INCIDENTAL, found by its own mutant) **`toContain('20.19')` passed on a section whose toolchain line had reverted** · The mutant put back "Node 20 (`.nvmrc`)" and the test stayed green, because the FOLLOWING sentence quotes the lockfile's range `^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0`. **A documentation guard that matches a substring anywhere in a section is a guard against nothing in particular.** It now matches the toolchain step itself. Worth recording because M8c.10 and M8c.1 added guards of the same shape, and this is the failure mode they share.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.6 — A FIXTURE THAT DID NOT LOOK LIKE PRODUCTION) **`triggerLines` threw on the control-surface fixtures, and the fixtures were the defect** · `activationPlanSchema` requires `triggers`, so a real plan always carries it; four test fixtures did not. The choice was to make the function total over a shape production cannot produce, or to make the fixtures real. The fixtures were made real — a `?? []` there would have been an unreachable branch existing only to protect a test from itself, which is the "branch that exists to be uncovered" this codebase already refuses elsewhere.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.7 — THE ROOT CAUSE WAS NOT IN THE SHIM, and the shim's own timeout is why that is provable) **`EPH_RECALL` began with Electron's binary, and Electron handed a `.mjs` looks for an app rather than running a script** · `eph-recall.mjs` has had a ten-second timeout and named refusals since it was written, and the health-watcher saw ZERO bytes over 90 seconds — so the shim never ran. `index.ts` composes the command from `process.execPath`, which in this app is Electron. It starts, finds no entry point, and sits; exit 143 is SIGTERM, the agent giving up. `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` is Electron's documented answer and appeared nowhere in the tree. It is set on the AGENT's environment rather than prefixed onto the command, because these are command STRINGS an agent pastes into its own shell, and its only effect is on an Electron binary — which nothing else an agent runs is.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.7 — A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME LINE, found by writing the probe) **The command was composed unquoted, so a harness under `C:\Program Files` hands agents three words** · The first REAL spawn in this package's own tests failed instantly for that reason. `shellCommand` quotes both halves. It deliberately does not escape a path containing a double quote: that is not legal on Windows and pathological elsewhere, so handling it would be a branch no test could honestly produce.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.7 — THE DISCRIMINATOR IS OUTPUT, NOT THE EXIT CODE, and getting it backwards is how this stayed invisible) **A named refusal is an ANSWER; a killed process's exit code says nothing** · `eph-recall` exits 1 with a named cause when the harness is down, and that is the shim working — the agent learns something. The 2026-09-09 process was killed, so its code says nothing at all. A check keyed on the exit code would have passed the hang (no code) and failed the working refusal (code 1), both backwards. That pair is the round's control case.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.7 — TWO SURVIVORS OF DIFFERENT KINDS, and telling them apart matters) **A mutant that cannot change what a test reads is not a mutant** · M8 (`child.kill()` removed) was a REAL gap: the probe still reported the timeout correctly and left the sixty-second process running, so the condition was right and the machine was dirtier every boot. M6 replaced the tail of a two-line string concatenation while the asserted phrase lived on the line before — it could not change what any test reads, and scoring it as a survivor would have sent me hunting a missing test that was already there. Re-aimed at the line carrying the phrase, it dies. **A round's survivors must be triaged, not just counted.**
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.7 — THE SECOND TIME A COVERAGE FLOOR CAUGHT A DESIGN DECISION IN THIS MILESTONE) **`reportRecallProbe` exists so `index.ts` holds one call and no closures** · The `boot` row failed on the `.then(…, …)` that was there first — two functions no test can enter. M8c.8's `unattendedFor` seam was the first instance, and in both cases the right reading was not "lower the floor" but "why is that logic in boot at all". GYM-006's row is doing exactly the job it was created for.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.1b — ARCHITECT DECISION, an eleventh change taken deliberately before the rehearsal) **`EXIT-M8` §2's "a few hundred thousand tokens is generous" is contradicted by both measured runs, by a factor of twenty** · No shipped Skeleton Crew hire declares a `budget`, so the company-wide `maxDailyTokens` becomes EACH hire's own daily budget rather than a company total. The 2026-09-09 agents spent 4.9M, 9.4M, 10.2M, 16.0M and 0 tokens. A ceiling of 300,000 — the figure §2's own example used — stops every one of them within minutes, so clauses 1b and 2 would fail for a reason that has nothing to do with the company, and a rehearsal run against that script would measure the ceiling rather than the crew. §2 now states the per-hire semantics, quotes the five figures, and names **5,000,000 per hire**: it bounds the 16M outlier that run actually produced and caps five hires at 25M against the 40.45M the unbounded hour cost. Same class as M8c.1 and M8c.6 — guidance that is true in the producer's vocabulary and false in the reader's — and found by pricing the rehearsal rather than by reading the file.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — FOUND BY RUNNING THE REHEARSAL, five minutes in, before a single agent was hired) **The refusal read twice, because both the source and the sink prefixed it** · `ControlServer` has always prefixed `consent was NOT granted:` at `control.ts:535`, and M8c.3's `grant()` added the same prefix to the reason it returns, so the rehearsal printed it doubled. Fixed at the SOURCE rather than by having the sink strip a prefix it finds — de-duplicating at the sink makes the string a protocol between two modules and would have hidden a third prefix rather than prevented it. The banner renders the reason behind a warning glyph and needs no prefix at all, which is the evidence that the headline belongs to the CLI and not to the answer. **The package that fixed "a refusal must teach the rule" shipped a refusal that stutters.**
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — THE SAME DEFECT M8c.1b HAD JUST FIXED, one document along) **The suggested ceiling lived in five places and one of them moved** · M8c.1b corrected `EXIT-M8` §2 to 5,000,000 with the measurements behind it. The four surfaces a reader actually MEETS — the consent screen, the refusal, `budget:set`'s usage line and the README — kept 300,000, so **the run script and the product disagreed about the one number the run turns on**. `maxDailyTokensSchema`'s own comment already recorded this lesson for the BOUND (*"written twice they agreed by coincidence"*); the SUGGESTION had never been given the same treatment. `SUGGESTED_DAILY_TOKENS` now sits beside it and the three code surfaces interpolate it; the README is prose and is pinned by a test instead.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — WHAT THE MUTATION ROUND COULD NOT SEE) **7 of 7 killed on the first round, and the adversarial pass still found a fifth surface** · Every mutant in the round was a string identity, aimed at tests written against those exact strings minutes earlier, so a perfect first round proves the guard is wired and nothing about a defect nobody thought of. The README's `--daily 300000` was invisible to it because no unit test reads prose. **A clean round is a reason to do the third pass, not a reason to skip it** — which is the standing verification bar working exactly as stated.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — A NON-CHANGE, recorded so it is not re-litigated) **`SettingsPanel`'s ceiling field names no figure, so it cannot disagree with one** · It starts empty with `placeholder="tokens"`. Adding a suggested placeholder was considered and refused: a placeholder that looks like a value is its own defect, and the window user is told the number on the consent screen, which is the screen they meet first. The rule the fix enforces is *every surface that NAMES a figure names the same one*, not *every surface names one*.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — AN ENVIRONMENTAL FAILURE REPORTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND) **`npm run test:coverage` exits 1 on this machine from a teardown race, not a test failure** · The v8 provider's `rmdir 'coverage\.tmp'` loses to OneDrive's file handle. The same suite under `npm test` reports the same 236 files and 4607 tests and exits 0, and `check-coverage.cjs` reads a complete report. Recorded in the implementation doc with both exit codes, because lowering a gate to make a run look clean is the thing this milestone exists to not do.
- 2026-09-10 · (M8c.3b — THE REHEARSAL RESTARTS RATHER THAN CONTINUING) **A run that measures two trees measures neither** · The rehearsal had reached only §1 and the first act of §2 — nothing granted, no company started, no agent hired, $0 spent — so restarting it after this lands costs nothing and buys a record that names one commit. The two findings above stay in the rehearsal record as *found in the first five minutes and fixed before the hour*, which is what a rehearsal is for.
- 2026-10-01 · (#10 — A GUARD TESTED FROM OUTSIDE A PROCESS) **`eph-usage`'s import guard is tested through an importer file, not `node -e`** · That importing the shim runs nothing is a property of a whole process, so one test in the otherwise in-process file spawns one, the way `eph-hook`'s and `eph-recall`'s tests already mix both. Under `-e`, `process.argv[1]` is never a module path — it is undefined, or the first script argument — so the comparison the guard really makes, a module path that is not the shim's, never happens; an importer file reproduces the argv a real importer has. The test asserts the guard's whole claim: exit 0, nothing on stdout or stderr, no reader attached to stdin (`process.stdin.readableFlowing` still null after the import), none of stdin taken (the importer then reads the whole document itself) and no report written. The child does not inherit `NODE_OPTIONS`, whose loaders and flags make Node write its own warnings to stderr. The guard matches a filename *suffix*, so a file named `x-eph-usage.mjs` would run `main()` on import; the other three shims match the same way. Tightening all four to a basename match, and testing the import side of `eph-hook`, `eph-recall` and `eph-gh-token`, is one follow-up, not this change.
- 2026-10-01 · (#10, FOUND BY CI — RECORDED, NOT FIXED, OUT OF SCOPE) **`test/main/pacing-wakes.test.ts` can fail in teardown with `ENOTEMPTY` on a temp Agora's `.git`** · CI run `36924116592` attempt 1 (a push run on `fix/eph-usage-coverage-10` in this repository, commit `adaa6e8`, ubuntu-24.04, node v20.20.2) failed `the pace gates the stop-hook wake path (decideOnStop) › lets the turn end instead of buying another one while slow` with `ENOTEMPTY: directory not empty, rmdir '/tmp/eph-pace-hFrNSG/agora/.git'`; it passed on the rerun, on the five other CI runs of the same production tree on that branch through `cecc69a` (`36923589834`, `36924807939`, `36925713796`, `36930369898`, `36932793164`) and in all five local win32 runs of that tree. The teardown (`pacing-wakes.test.ts:35-41`) awaits `agora.drained().catch(() => {})` and then gives `fs.rmSync` Node's own ten-retry budget, about 2.75 s, so a git process outlived the drain — and the swallowed rejection would also hide a drain that failed. A different flake from the two `WakeClock` entries above. The change that saw it touched neither that file, `src/` nor `test/tmpdir.ts`; finding the writer is its own follow-up.
- 2026-10-02 · (#10 / PR #60 — A CONTRIBUTOR'S PR FINISHED ON ITS OWN BRANCH) **The Architect's fixes land on #60 itself, and #60 is squash-merged** · The fix for #10 is the contributor's, so it merges as their PR. Review found #60's head right but two of its seven commits not typechecking — a literal `\n` put `import fs` inside a comment (`TS2304` ×8 in CI on `60947ed`) — and `main` merges with merge commits, so both would land red (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §1). The fork branch is not rewritten: the Architect's four commits go on top of its head `b437907` by maintainer edit, each typechecking, and #60 is squash-merged so neither red tree reaches `main`. A squash puts one commit on the first-parent chain, authored by the PR's author with the Architect as co-author; `check-attribution.cjs` refuses a co-author only when it names a Claude or Anthropic identity (`scripts/check-attribution.cjs:146`), and on the first-parent chain only a `[bot]` identity, so neither rule applies. Built first and set aside: re-applying the seven commits as five green ones on a maintainer branch, `fix/eph-usage-coverage-10` in this repository — clean history, but it would have superseded the contributor's PR. That branch is kept: the coverage floors' recorded runs were measured there, because upstream CI makes push runs only for upstream branches.
- 2026-10-02 · (pacing-wakes teardown — THE WRITER WAS OUR OWN COMMIT'S HOUSEKEEPING; the follow-up the 2026-10-01 #10 entry above left open) **`ExecGitRunner` runs git's auto-maintenance in the foreground (`-c maintenance.autoDetach=false -c gc.autoDetach=false`), not off and not in tests only** · `git commit` starts `git maintenance run --auto`, which on POSIX daemonizes; git 2.55 repacks there when two loose objects share `objects/17`, so `drained()` resolved with `git repack` still writing `.git/objects/pack` and a teardown met ENOTEMPTY (CI run 36924116592). Turning maintenance OFF would let a long-lived Agora that stores a fresh `log.jsonl` blob per commit grow without bound; a test-only switch would leave production's `drained()` — the quit sequence's `agora-drain` — resolving with git still writing. Foreground is git's documented switch and what Git for Windows already does, because it cannot daemonize. The cost, measured on a production-shaped Agora: an occasional linux/macOS commit includes its housekeeping — 348 ms for git 2.55's routine repack, 2.8 s once when 2.55 first meets a never-packed Agora, 15.5 s for an older git's full `gc --auto` every ~6 700 loose objects (near the runner's 20 s timeout, which the queue's retry absorbs). Delivery never waits; boot (`reconcile()`) and quit (`agora-drain`) can. In the runner, not on the commit call: global options there would break every `GitRunner` fake that reads `args[0]` as the subcommand.
- 2026-10-02 · (pacing-wakes teardown — A SIGNAL THAT IS NOT VERSION-PROOF) **The regression test asserts that the housekeeping's pack exists when `drained()` resolves, not that `objects/maintenance.lock` is gone** · Measured with the old runner right after a commit: git 2.55 held the lock through the detached phase 10/10, git 2.53 held it 0/10 while its repack was still running, so a lock assertion would pass on 2.53 with the defect in place. The pack was missing 10/10 detached and 0/10 in the foreground on both. The same measurement retired the idea of a teardown that waits for the lock to go.
- 2026-10-02 · (temp hygiene — THE GUARD ACCEPTED THE TEARDOWN THAT FAILED) **A test file that reaches real git removes its temp directories with `removeTempDir` and makes no raw recursive removal outside a test; `rmSync` stays acceptable only where no git runs** · The 2026-09-07 guard accepted any file that called `removeTempDir(` or `rmSync(`, so `pacing-wakes.test.ts` kept `rmSync(…, { maxRetries: 10, retryDelay: 50 })` and lost to a detached `git repack` on CI (run `36924116592`; the diagnosis and its evidence are `2026-10-02-pacing-wakes-teardown.md` on `fix/pacing-wakes-teardown`): Node 20's `rmSync` lists a directory once and retries only its `rmdir`. The guard now reads each file's syntax tree, as `reachability.cjs` does. A file reaches git when it imports one of git's doors for its values — `src/main/agora.ts`, `git.ts`, `index.ts` and the two git scripts, a set the guard re-derives from the tree and pins — runs `git` through a resolved `child_process` binding, runs a git script, or imports a `test/` helper that does; that last is how 18 scenario files reach an Agora through `startCompany`, and every such file is judged, temp directory or not. It flagged `pacing-wakes` and `incident-surface-wiring`. `rmSync` stays for files that never touch git on evidence rather than by default: the four that still use it write synchronously, await their reads and run children through `execFileSync`, while a rule wide enough to catch an asynchronous child left in a temp directory would punish `tmpdir.test.ts` and `pin.ts`, which pin one on purpose to measure it. An adversarial pass broke the first version from both sides — git and test bodies recognised by spelling hid `import { Agora as Store }` and `test.afterEach`, and flagged `import type` and an aliased `it` — so the rebuilt guard answers by modules and by where code runs. Detail: `docs/implementations/2026-10-02-temp-hygiene-requires-remover.md`.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — ARCHITECT DECISION) **`fix/shim-import-guards` is stacked on `fix/eph-usage-coverage-10`, not cut from `main`** · The follow-up recorded above covers four guards, but `main` carries three: on `main`, `shims/eph-usage.mjs` ends in an unconditional `main().catch(…)`, and both its guard and its import test arrive with #10's branch, which is unmerged and has no maintainer PR yet. Cut from `main`, the change could tighten only three guards, and introducing `eph-usage`'s a second time would collide with #10's. Asked, the Architect chose to stack the branch on #10's head (`60062b4`), so all four guards are tightened in one change and `eph-usage`'s existing import test gains the basename case. Rejected: cutting from `main` and leaving `eph-usage` owed until #10 merges, and adding a commit to #10's already-reviewed branch. Superseded the same morning by the entry on merging `main` in, below: #10 did merge, as #60 itself.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The three new import tests spawn the importer asynchronously, not with `spawnSync`** · `eph-hook`, `eph-recall` and `eph-gh-token` act on a harness endpoint, and in each test that endpoint — the hook stub, or a real `HookServer` — is served by the vitest worker itself. `spawnSync` blocks the event loop that serves it, so a stray `main()`'s request could be received only after the child had finished, and "no request arrived" would be read before the server could have seen one. An asynchronous spawn keeps the endpoint answering while the importer runs: a stray `main()` really posts and is really answered, and the count of requests received is an observation rather than a race. `eph-usage` keeps `spawnSync`, because it writes a file and talks to nobody. Approved with the plan.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2) **The guard compares the shim's whole file name, not the file's identity** · Each guard is now `process.argv[1] && path.basename(process.argv[1]) === '<name>.mjs'`. The first operand stays because under `node -e` with no arguments, or in a REPL, `argv[1]` is undefined, and `path.basename(undefined)` throws, which would turn every such import into a crash. Rejected: comparing `argv[1]` with `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)`, which would also refuse an unrelated file that happens to share the name. Node absolutises `argv[1]` but loads the main module from its real path, so the two differ whenever the path to the shim crosses a junction or a symlink — measured on node v20.16.0: run through a junction, `argv[1]` names the junction's path and `import.meta.url` the target's — and the shim would not recognise itself. `main()` would then silently never run: a hook that never posts, a recall that prints nothing. The residual is recorded rather than closed: a file that is itself named `<shim>.mjs`, in another directory, still runs `main()` when it imports the shim. No such file exists, and each import test's second case, `x-<shim>.mjs`, marks where the boundary now lies.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — ARCHITECT DECISION, after the mutation round found it) **A read that takes nothing is caught by an import whose stdin never ends** · A bare `process.stdin.read()` at module scope survived every import test, `eph-usage`'s from #10 included. It moves no byte out of `process.stdin`'s reach, so `readableFlowing` stays `null` and the importer still reads all of its stdin. But it starts a read, and a read pending on stdin keeps a process alive until stdin ends: measured outside the repository, an importer whose stdin was written to and never ended exited by itself in 230–340 ms, and with the bare read it never exited and was killed at the timeout (2 of 2 runs; an `on('data')` reader hangs the same way). Asked, the Architect chose to add the check rather than record the survivor: `runImporter` repeats each import with stdin held open (`importWithStdinOpen`, `test/shims/importer.ts`), and `eph-usage`'s test calls the same helper.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — FOUND BY THE BEFORE ROUND) **Two guards looked tested only because `main()` exits inside a vitest worker** · Mutated to `if (true)` or `if (process.argv[1])`, `eph-recall`'s and `eph-gh-token`'s guards passed every one of their tests (12 of 12, 7 of 7), and the vitest run still exited 1: `main()` ran inside the worker that imported the shim, took its own `process.exit(1)` path on the worker's arguments and environment, and vitest refuses an unexpected exit. That is an accident of which branch `main()` takes in a worker, not an assertion about the guard — `eph-hook`, which fails open and never exits, let the same two mutants through a fully green run. And `eph-gh-token`'s guard mutated to `if (false)` survived everything, because no test ran that shim as a program. `test/shims/eph-gh-token.test.ts` now runs it the way `src/main/index.ts:2384` hands it to agents.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — RECORDED FOR GYM-008, NOT A PROCESS CHANGE) **Two conditions voided runs in this change's first mutation round, and a harness that scores by exit code reads both as kills** · (1) The suite's free-memory gate (`test/global-setup.ts`, 1.0 GB) refused to start once other processes on this machine had taken the headroom: those runs executed zero tests and exited 1, and a scratch harness scoring by exit code reported them as kills, the no-op controls included. A run with zero tests is INVALID, never a kill. (2) OneDrive replayed a restored file: `shims/eph-recall.mjs` came back carrying the round's last mutant about two seconds after the harness had restored it and its hash check had passed — the same signature as 2026-09-09, and caught only because the round re-hashed every file at its end. The round was redone in a detached worktree under `%TEMP%`, which OneDrive does not sync, with every file hashed before and after every run. GYM-008's tool, when it is built, should do both.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — ARCHITECT DECISION, after `main` moved under the branch) **`main` is merged into `fix/shim-import-guards` rather than the branch being rebased** · At 09:17 #60 was squash-merged as `cab9e1b`, after this branch had been stacked on `fix/eph-usage-coverage-10` and before its first commit. `cab9e1b`'s code, tests and coverage floors are byte-identical to `60062b4`'s; only `DECISIONS-LOG.md` and #10's implementation doc differ. So all four guards now exist on `main`, and the reason to stack was gone. A trial merge showed four conflicts: this file, #10's implementation doc, `shims/eph-usage.mjs` and `test/shims/eph-usage.test.ts`. Asked, the Architect chose to merge `main` in and resolve them here, rather than rebase onto `cab9e1b` or leave the branch stacked. Resolved as follows. The shim and its test keep this branch's side, because `main`'s versions are this branch's base. #10's implementation doc takes `main`'s, which records how #60 actually merged. This file takes `main`'s entries, with this change's appended. Consequence: the branch's history keeps `fix/eph-usage-coverage-10`'s sixteen commits, whose content `cab9e1b` already carries, and it now merges into `main` without conflict.
- 2026-10-02 · (SHIM IMPORT GUARDS — ARCHITECT DECISION, after review found it) **The guard's first operand is held by an import with no module path** · Review of this change found that no test held `process.argv[1] &&`. Deleted, each guard became `path.basename(process.argv[1]) === …`, which passes every importer-file, program and in-process test, because each of those has an `argv[1]`. Only an import whose `argv[1]` is undefined crashes. Asked, the Architect chose to pin it rather than mark that row of the guard's truth table untested. Each shim's import tests now include `importWithoutModulePath` (`test/shims/importer.ts`), which runs `node --input-type=module -e "await import(<shim>)"` with no arguments and requires exit 0 with nothing on stdout or stderr. It does not contradict the importer-file rule above: the importer files still test the comparison the guard really makes, and this case exists to leave `argv[1]` undefined.
- 2026-10-02 · (GATE FIX — LEDGER ROW GYM-010, FILED AFTER REVIEW) **The single-committer tripwire had never once seen `src/main/git.ts`; three of its rules now read the whole file** · `check-invariants.cjs` tested `GIT_INVOCATION` one line at a time, so the pattern's `\s*` could not span the line break Prettier puts after a long call's parenthesis. `git.ts`'s call has had that shape in every version since `e878641` wrote the rule beside it — 0 per-line matches, 1 whole-file — so its allowlist entry was never exercised, and a Prettier-formatted call planted in `src/main/agora.ts` printed `invariants ok`, exit 0. The truncating-write rule (a long `writeFileSync` loses its path to the next line) and the ledger rule (SQL in a template literal is wrapped by hand, as `db.ts` already does) were blind the same way. All three now match the whole text and report the line each match starts on. The clock, `webContents.send`, environment and secret-shape rules stay per line, because Prettier, asked directly, never breaks the spans they match. **The ledger row is GYM-010.** The first version of this entry argued that a fix which only tightens a gate back to its documented behaviour needs no row, citing 2026-08-27 and 2026-08-29. Review answered that both predate the 2026-09-02 ruling above ("A changed CI gate gets a ledger entry"), which faulted a `check-invariants.cjs` rule for landing without one, and the Architect chose the row over an exemption. The change therefore waits on GYM-010's verdict. The order the Gymnasium asks for (proposal, approval, then code) was not followed here; this records that rather than excusing it. **Proposed separately as GYM-009**, on its own branch: the git allowlist does not fail an entry that matches nothing, as `reachability.cjs`'s does, which is how this one sat dead for five weeks; and the rule cannot see a shell-string command, the binary by path, a renamed or promisified import, or a `.mts`/`.cts` file, none of which the tree contains today. Evidence, the rule-by-rule measurement against Prettier, and a 22-mutant round with a no-op control (22 killed) are in `docs/implementations/2026-10-02-invariants-see-wrapped-calls.md`.

- 2026-10-02 · (TEMP HYGIENE — A HELPER REMOVES WHAT IT MAKES) **`test/scenarios/company.ts` registers `afterAll(cleanupHomes)` itself, rather than the guard requiring every scenario file to call `cleanupHomes()`** · Both were on the table. A rule that obliged each caller would detect a forgotten call after somebody wrote it, and needs name resolution across files; the hook makes the leak impossible to write, and what keeps it so is local to one file — a removal counts only if the file that makes the directory runs it. It cannot race a company still closing: vitest 4.1.11 runs a suite's after-hooks last-registered first (`sequence.hooks: 'stack'`, its default, which `vitest.config.mts` leaves alone), the hook registers when the scenario's import evaluates the module, before the scenario's own teardown, and a file's root `afterAll` runs after every `describe`'s; `test/temp-hygiene.test.ts` carries a tripwire for the order. Measured with `s-livelock.test.ts`'s call deleted: the old helper left three `eph-scenario-*` homes per run with the suite green, the new one none. The same rule refuses a helper that hands its directory to callers, which is what a `makeTempDir()` beside `removeTempDir` would do. See `docs/implementations/2026-10-02-temp-hygiene-blind-spots.md`.
- 2026-10-02 · (TEMP HYGIENE — FOUND BY REFUTATION) **A removal only a kept function performs counts for nothing, and four rigs' homes moved into lists their hooks empty** · `control-server.test.ts`, `s-crash.test.ts`, `ephctl.test.ts` and `eph-recall.test.ts` removed each rig's home only in the rig object's `close()`, which ran only if `rigs.push(rig)` had registered the rig; an adversarial pass deleted the push or the drain in each, and all eight mutations left every home behind with the guard green. Teaching the guard to follow objects through returns and lists to their method calls was weighed and refused — the same machinery again, for values that are not directories — in favour of the shape it already verifies, which is also the robust one: the home is removed whether or not the rig was registered. `s-crash.test.ts` keeps its `close()`-time removal as well: it was left so as not to touch the line beside it while `fix/drained-teardown-no-swallow` (PR #61) was rewriting that line; #61 has since merged, and removing an already-removed directory is a no-op (`force: true`), so it is left for a tidy. The price is recorded: a cleanup closure a test does call is refused, and the fault names the shape that passes. See `docs/implementations/2026-10-02-temp-hygiene-blind-spots.md` §3.7 and §5.
- 2026-10-03 · (TEMP HYGIENE — FOUND BY REVIEW) **A removal moved into a named function counted wherever a call of it was written; the call itself must now run** · The entry above refused a removal inside a kept function, but asked of a NAMED function only whether some call of it sat in code that runs: the call was judged by the nearest named function around it, skipping any object method or closure in between, and a function handed to `push`, `add` or `set` was taken as run. Review of #68, in two passes that found it separately, gave seven shapes that left every directory behind with the guard green — among them the four rigs' defect after a one-line refactor (`rigs.push(rig)` deleted, `close()` calling a named `dropHomes()`) and a named cleanup pushed onto a list nothing runs. Closed rather than recorded with the claims narrowed, which would have left the entry above untrue: a call of a named function counts only when every function around the call runs — the question already asked of a removal — and a collector stores a named function as it stores an anonymous one. Review also found a hand-back that the file both calls and exports inside an object judged only at its calls; a hand-back the file uses other than by calling it is now refused. No file in the tree changed verdict. Measured: a 113-mutant round with five no-op controls (107 killed, 6 survived, all as predicted), and fifteen defects planted into real files — this guard fails all fifteen, the guard before review twelve (review's three shapes, written into `company.ts`, passed it), `main`'s none. See `docs/implementations/2026-10-02-temp-hygiene-blind-spots.md` §3.8 and §4.
- 2026-10-03 · (TEMP HYGIENE — ARCHITECT DECISION, after review found it; LEDGER ROW GYM-011, FILED AFTER REVIEW) **The change gets a Gymnasium row, approved, rather than an exemption** · ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §3 calls an altered CI gate, or a new convention, without a ledger entry a defect. #68 changes what a test inside the required `Typecheck · lint · test` check refuses, and adds four conventions every test author meets: each directory reaches a remover the file runs; a temp-root path outside `mkdtemp` is listed in `UNMADE_TEMP_PATHS` with an exact count and a reason; a helper removes what it makes from its own module-level hook, and one that hands directories back is refused; a removal only a kept function performs counts for nothing. Review found no row and the question unasked. #64's document had argued that no row was owed for this guard, by the precedent of 2026-09-01 and 2026-09-07, but the second postdates the 2026-09-02 ruling above, and #65's ruling the same day went to the row. Asked — file the row and approve it, file it and hold the merge, or rule that a guard inside the suite is not a CI gate under §3 — the Architect chose the row and approved it: GYM-011, decided 2026-10-03, metric 1 met before the merge, metric 2 due 2026-10-16. The order the Gymnasium asks for (proposal, approval, then code) was not followed here; this records that rather than excusing it.
- 2026-10-03 · (REPAIR, the 2026-09-07 METHOD hazard) **A literal NUL hid `tmpdir.test.ts` from grep and from LF normalization; it is now written as the escape it meant** · `test/main/tmpdir.test.ts:192` hands `removeTempDir` the path `'a-path-with-a…-nul'`, and the NUL was in the file as the byte itself, at offset 8958: the only control character in the tree's 909 tracked text files. It arrived with `84a68e9` on 2026-09-01, not with #62, which only moved it (from offset 8848), so it is older than the METHOD entry of 2026-09-07 above and outlived that day's repair. What it did was narrower than "the file is binary", and worse: GNU grep answered "Binary file … matches" with no lines; ripgrep searching a directory skipped the file without a word (96 files listed for `removeTempDir\(`, not this one, which has five); and under `text=auto` git classed it binary, so the LF normalization `.gitattributes` guarantees did not cover it. `git diff`, `git grep` and GitHub's PR view read it as text, because git looks for a NUL only in a blob's first 8000 bytes, and the margin was 959 bytes. Repaired byte-precisely to `\u0000` by a script that builds the six characters from byte values; both versions parse to the same program (1235 nodes, identical decoded values), and a mutant without the NUL fails the case. No check against a recurrence was added: refusing control bytes in tracked text changes what a gate refuses, which needs a ledger row first (ENGINEERING-STANDARDS §3). See `docs/implementations/2026-10-03-tmpdir-test-nul-escape.md`.

- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, A READING OF INVARIANT 9) **A mutation spec carries `schemaVersion` and is validated inside `scripts/mutate.cjs`, not in `src/shared/`** · Invariant 9 puts a schema'd file's validator in `src/shared/`. A spec under `test/mutation/` is read by one development script and by nothing in the application, and `scripts/*.cjs` cannot import `src/shared/*.ts` without a TypeScript runner, which would be a new dependency (2026-09-08, M8.13 D3). `scripts/coverage-floors.json` already has this shape: `schemaVersion` 3, validated by `check-coverage.cjs` itself. Asked, the Architect chose to keep `specProblems()` in the script and to record that choice as the reading of invariant 9 for development-tool files a script alone reads. Rejected: a validator in `src/shared/` run through a TypeScript runner.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **GYM-008's metric is measured on 2026-10-17, two weeks after it landed** · The proposal's metric reads "within two weeks of landing (by 2026-09-24)": the date assumed it would land on its approval day, 2026-09-10, and it landed on 2026-10-03. Asked, the Architect chose the metric's own rule over the date it was written with, so the ledger's Measured cell reads due 2026-10-17 and says why. Rejected: keeping 2026-09-24 and recording the window as having passed with nothing landed.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2; put to the Architect at the task report) **A run is scored from vitest's JSON report, and a report holding no file is a refusal to retry, not a kill** · Measured before the scorer was written, on vitest 4.1.11: a global setup that throws — `test/global-setup.ts`'s free-memory gate does — writes a report holding no file, and one that exits writes no report; both exit 1, as a real failure does. A test file that cannot load (a mutated module that does not parse) is a kill, reported as a file kill so it is triaged: it proves the file depends on the line, not that a test reads it. The table is in `docs/implementations/2026-10-03-gym-008-mutation-harness.md` §3.2.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2; put to the Architect at the task report) **Each mutant is built from the bytes the round started with, and three guards no deterministic test could reach were taken out** · A hash check before each mutant, a read-back after writing it, and a SIGINT handler were written and removed. With the mutant built from cached bytes, a write landing before it is overwritten and one landing after is caught when the run ends, so the first two could never disagree with a check that stays; and Windows cannot deliver a SIGINT a test can send. An interrupted round instead says, before its first mutant, how to restore by hand, and the next round refuses a dirty tree. `--check` also requires a committed tree, so a checked spec is one that would run.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — A MECHANICAL CHOICE, recorded per BUILD-PROMPT §8.2; put to the Architect at the task report) **A run's fault and a failed restore are reported together, and a crash reads as INVALID** · A fault caught during a run no longer hides behind the restore's: both reach the reader, the restore second, because it says the tree is not what was committed. A harness crash exits 2 with its stack, never 1, which means survivors; `main(argv, round)` takes the round as a parameter only so a test can make it crash, since no input to the real round is meant to.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, after an adversarial pass beat the tool) **Every finding of the refutation pass is closed in this package before it lands** · After 60 green tests and two clean rounds on itself, an independent pass against hostile repositories made `scripts/mutate.cjs` at `6acf412` issue verdicts it had not earned, and each was then reproduced against that commit: a baseline PASS with no test executed (`describe.skip`); a worker the mutant killed, scored SURVIVED; an unhandled rejection that leaves vitest exiting 1 read as a green baseline and a ROUND OK; a test path beginning `--` read by vitest as an option; a mutant's leftover file "killing" the next mutant and a write to an unlisted tracked file, both ending ROUND OK with the tree dirty; a hard-linked round file carrying the first mutant out of the repository for good; `assume-unchanged` hiding an edit so the restore destroyed uncommitted work; and an anchor in malformed Unicode matching text nobody wrote. Asked, the Architect chose to close all nine here, with a mutant for each new guard, the gate, the self-round and a second adversarial pass run again. Rejected: closing only the wrong-verdict findings now, and landing as it stood.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A run is scored over the tests that passed at the baseline** · Every listed file must hold at least one test that passed at the baseline, so a skipped, todo or filtered file defends nothing and is refused. A mutant run in which any test that passed at the baseline did not finish — pending, skipped or absent — is INVALID, because a worker that dies leaves its tests pending in a report that still says `success: true`. Tests the baseline skips are printed. Rejected: refusing any skip, todo or `.only` in a listed file (this suite's platform-gated skips would bar those files), and requiring only one passing test per run (a worker dying part-way would still score SURVIVED).
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A report with no failure from a vitest that exited non-zero is INVALID** · vitest's JSON report keeps `success: true` through an unhandled error and through a worker that died, while vitest exits 1. Such a run is INVALID with the log's tail, at the baseline (where it is a red suite) and under a mutant alike; it is never scored from the exit code alone and never a kill, because a dead worker is also this repository's out-of-memory signature. Rejected: a kill when only the mutant causes it, and a reporter of the tool's own that records unhandled errors.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A round guards the whole working tree, and the files that ran must be exactly the files listed** · The whole tree must be clean at the start, untracked files included (ignored ones are not shown and not judged); after the baseline it must still be clean, and after each mutant's run the only change it may show is the mutated file. A run that writes anywhere else — a leftover test file, a tracked file outside the round, a snapshot the baseline creates — is INVALID, naming what it wrote. vitest reads a file argument as a filter, so a run that also picks up a file the spec does not list is INVALID, naming it. Rejected: watching round files only, and reporting extra test files without refusing them.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A round file must be what git holds, where git holds it** · Before anything runs the round refuses a file whose real path lies outside the repository's real path (a junction or symlink on the way), a file with more than one hard link (the write lands in every name and the restore keeps only one), a file whose bytes differ from its index blob, and a file git has been told not to look at (`assume-unchanged` or `skip-worktree`, read from `git ls-files -v`). Rejected: the path and link checks alone, which leave the destroyed-uncommitted-work case open.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A run that hits `--timeout` is INVALID with its own reason and is not retried** · It no longer reads as a suite that refused to start, and the message for a run in which no file ran names the possible causes rather than asserting the free-memory gate. Rejected: scoring a hang as a kill, as conventional mutation tools do (a slow machine would then look like a test catching the mutant), and retrying it at up to three runs of 900 s per mutant.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A path segment may not begin with `-`, and `find` and `replace` must be well-formed Unicode** · vitest read the listed test `--testNamePattern=…` as an option and skipped every test; and an anchor holding a lone surrogate is encoded as U+FFFD, so it matched text its author never wrote. Both are refused by the spec validator.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **Four limits the pass raised stay, and are written down in TEST-STRATEGY §10** · `--check` exits 0 as a ROUND OK does (its CHECKED line is distinct); the control is not machine-checked to be a no-op; a listed test file may itself be mutated; and the nested vitest inherits the author's environment. None of them produces a wrong verdict on its own.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, A CONDITION OF THE EVIDENCE) **The gate and the third round wait for free memory rather than run on a starved machine** · At 14:42 the gate at `ed03256` failed 16 tests in 14 files this change does not touch, every one a 30 s hook or test timeout in a git-heavy suite, with free memory at 0.40 GB while other applications held it; the third round was stopped in its baseline, before any mutant was written, and its worktree was clean. A round run in that state could score a timeout as a kill nobody earned. Asked, the Architect chose to wait: both start once free memory has held at 3 GB or more for a minute. Rejected: running now anyway, and pausing the task.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, after a second refutation pass) **The eleven findings of the second pass are recorded as the round's known limits, not closed in this package** · A second independent pass against `ed03256` found eleven ways in; nine were reproduced in this session before anything was decided (F1–F5, F7–F10): a snapshot written into an ignored directory becomes the oracle that "kills" a mutant; a same-named test, or one file reported twice by vitest `projects`, stands in for the test that passed at the baseline; a test that commits, a submodule marked `ignore = dirty`, and a per-repository fsmonitor hook each carry state across runs where `git status` does not look; a clean filter or `text=auto` with `core.autocrlf=true` hides a working-copy difference that the restore then rewrites (in this repository a working file saved with CRLF is that case); and a vitest `retry` setting turns a kill into a pass. Two were not reproduced and need a repository written to deceive the round: a detached writer or state kept outside the repository (F6), and a vitest config that rewrites the report (F11). Asked to close the cheap ones, record all, or isolate every mutant in its own worktree, the Architect chose to record all eleven as known limits in TEST-STRATEGY §10 and land.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The round's trust boundary is stated in TEST-STRATEGY §10 and in THREAT-MODEL.md** · A round runs the repository's own tests and configuration, so test code or configuration written to deceive it can forge any verdict; a round measures honest tests. Rejected: TEST-STRATEGY alone.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A kill must come from a test that passed at the baseline, or from a listed file that fails to load** · Today any failing test counts, including one a mutant switches on that never passed at the baseline. Rejected: keeping any failing test as a kill.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **No third refutation pass before GYM-008 lands** · Two passes have run; the record of real use through the metric window, 2026-10-17, and the stated trust boundary carry the rest. Rejected: a third pass now.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, a clarification) **The kill rule changes now; the eleven findings are documented; then the gate and a fourth round** · "Record all eleven and land" and "a kill must come from a test that passed at the baseline" pulled against each other, since the second is a code change. Asked, the Architect chose: make only the kill-rule change, run the gate and round 4 on it, record the eleven as known limits, and land. Rejected: landing the code exactly as round 3 left it with the kill rule as a follow-up.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The follow-up for the eleven known limits is a GitHub issue on the repository** · Its text is put to the Architect before it is opened, because an issue is public. Rejected: a Gymnasium proposal filed in this package, and listing them only in the session's report.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The three mechanical choices recorded above for GYM-008 are confirmed** · Put to the Architect at the task report as BUILD-PROMPT §8.2 choices: scoring a run from vitest's JSON report with a no-file report retried as a refusal and an unloadable file named as a file kill; building each mutant from the bytes the round started with, removing the three guards no deterministic test could reach, and requiring a committed tree for `--check`; and reporting a run fault together with a failed restore, with a harness crash read as INVALID. All three accepted as written.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The issue tracking the eleven known limits is opened now, before GYM-008 merges, with the text the Architect approved** · Rejected: waiting until the paths it cites resolve on `main`.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — A CORRECTION to the entry above that records the second pass's findings) **In this repository a working file saved with CRLF is NOT the line-ending case** · That entry says a CRLF working copy here is the `text=auto` case. It was asserted, not run, and a probe refutes it: under this repository's `* text=auto eol=lf`, an LF file committed and then rewritten with CRLF shows as ` M` in `git status --porcelain` with `core.autocrlf` both `true` and `false`, so the round's clean-tree check refuses to start. The finding needs `text=auto` without `eol=lf` under `core.autocrlf=true`, which is how it was reproduced. TEST-STRATEGY §10 item 7 is corrected; this entry stands beside the original, which is not edited.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, after `main` moved under the branch) **`main` (`75ec37c`, #69) is merged into `feature/gym-008-mutation-harness`, not rebased onto** · A trial `git merge-tree` showed one conflict, the DECISIONS-LOG tail, where both sides appended. It is resolved with `main`'s file kept as a byte-identical prefix and this branch's entries appended after it, and the gate is re-run on the merged tree. Rejected: rebasing, which would invalidate every commit hash this branch's documents cite, and pushing unmerged.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The branch is pushed once the merged tree's gate is green; the PR is opened by the engineer after the Architect has seen its text; then the session ends** · Merging the PR stays the Architect's.
- 2026-10-03 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The PR is opened with the title and body the Architect read, after the merged tree's gate and CI went green** · Gate at `3d0ebe6` (win32): exit 0, 240 files, 5006 passed, 8 skipped, floors ok; CI push run 37149172944 at `3d0ebe6` (ubuntu-24.04, node v20.20.2): all three jobs succeeded, 4999 passed, 15 skipped.
