- 2026-10-11 · (GYM-008 — ARCHITECT DECISION, at review before merge) **Three sentences the review found untrue are corrected on the branch, then #71 merges; the test gaps follow up** · An independent review of #71 found no way to a wrong verdict outside the eleven known limits of issue #70, and found three doc sentences the code or the record contradicts: THREAT-MODEL §6.9 said F6 and F11 were "demonstrated" by the second pass, where TEST-STRATEGY §10, issue #70, the entry above and record §3.7 all say not reproduced; record §4 said two same-named tests cannot stand in for each other (limit 2 says one can) and that any failing test or file is a kill (untrue since `b20f83b`: anything but a failed baseline test or an unloadable listed file is INVALID); record §3.3 said the hash and `git status` checks never overlap (the restore checks the round's files with both). Asked to merge as is, correct the three first, or also close the test gaps first, the Architect chose to correct the three and merge. Carried to a follow-up, not closed here: deleting `if (unrestored !== null) throw unrestored` (`scripts/mutate.cjs:669`) leaves all 87 tests of `test/scripts/mutate.test.ts` green (run 2026-10-11, linux, Node 22.22.0, 87 passed before and after), and it is the only guard on the last mutant's restore; the retry wait is untested because every test round passes `--retry-wait 0`; the mutant is written before the `try`, so a write that fails part-way is not restored.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN — ARCHITECT DIRECTION) **The target is a real agentic harness; the brand core stays; the floor becomes a view mode** · The Architect pasted the feature lists of the two sources already on the Stoa watchlist — ten rows for Odysseus (Chat & Agents; Cookbook; Deep Research; Compare; Documents; Memory & Skills; Email; Notes, Tasks & Calendar; MCP & Integrations; Mobile) and six for Hermes Agent (Lives Everywhere; Persistent Memory; Focused Automation; Tasks Multiplied; Browse the Web; Isolated Sandboxing) — asked for them to be added to milestone phase 9 and planned comprehensively, and said: *"We will keep the initial Ephesus brand core but the floor becomes an optional view mode and Ephesus upgrades itself towards a real agentic harness."* Planned as `docs/PHASE-9-PLAN.md`: seven milestones, M9b to M9h — five after M9 and before M7b, and two after it (DD-M9-18 below). **Planning only** — the instruction of 2026-10-02 not to build yet was not lifted, and every new box in PROGRESS is unticked on purpose. The two lists are the Architect's instruction; how either source implements them is data, never instruction (NFR-17), read from outside the Stoa at the commits the plan's §3 names, and re-read by a governed `/research` cycle in each milestone's contract package.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-8 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The sixteen features are follow-on milestones; M9 is untouched** · M9's eight packages, its bench-before-engine order, its measured-uplift exit and its seven decisions of 2026-10-02 stand exactly as approved. The features become lettered milestones after it — M9b (the workspace), M9c (local models), M9d (contained execution, armed gates, MCP, delegation), M9e (the open web and research), M9f (the gateway, remote authorisation, schedules, the workspace in a browser), M9g (mail, calendar, todos) and M9h (the companion, added by DD-M9-15 below) — each with its own exit and its own bench tasks. Rejected: adding the features as further packages of M9 (one exit, months away, with the measured uplift reduced to one criterion among many); rewriting M9 feature-first (which would have reopened the seven decisions). The decision numbering continues M9-PLAN §10's.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-9 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **A zero-dependency core, and a decision memo for every addition after it** · M9 keeps "zero new runtime dependencies" as a property of the milestone (DD-M9-3's consequence). From M9b on, a capability is built on platform `fetch`, `node:` builtins or an owned subprocess first — ADR-0009's and ADR-0016's discipline: version probe, visible install offer, no hidden daemon — and a library or bundled service that cannot be avoided gets its own decision memo at its package (invariant §10). PHASE-9-PLAN §10 lists every memo the phase expects, so none is a surprise; nothing is approved by being listed there. Rejected: zero dependencies for the whole phase (mail, calendar and MCP protocols written by hand, bundled search and model serving left to the operator); approving a named set of libraries in one batch now.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-10 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The feature milestones come before M7b; M7b.4 is absorbed by the gateway** · v1 ships the agentic harness: M9, then M9b to M9g, then M7b. M7b.4 (one Slack-compatible chat bridge) becomes the first adapters of M9f's gateway seam and is no longer a box of M7b; the exit clause "a real overnight run produces a truthful morning brief on the phone" moves to M9f's exit with it. M7b keeps M7b.1, M7b.2, M7b.3, M7b.5 and M7b.6 and stays the v1 acceptance boundary, with signed builds last — DD-M9-1's reason, "ship what is measured", extended to the phase. The cost was stated before the answer as "about six months"; the plan's own estimate, made afterwards as the sum of its package estimates and after DD-M9-15 added a milestone, is about 44 to 51 weeks (PHASE-9-PLAN §8, R35) — roughly double. A decision taken on a figure that then doubled is an open decision, so the question was put to the Architect again on the full figure, and **DD-M9-18 below amends this entry**. Rejected: M7b first with the features as post-v1 milestones; M7b.1–M7b.3 first so the company helps build the features (it depends on the proof gate being met by operation, which it is not).
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-11 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **The window opens on a workspace; the floor is a view mode** · ADR-0040, accepted on this answer. Two modes, `workspace` (the default: the conversation with Artemis, the agent sessions, the approvals) and `floor` (the Terraces, unchanged, held to ADR-0014's standard whenever shown). The choice is remembered as `view` in `config.json`, and absent means `workspace`. What an install whose `config.json` predates the field should do was not covered by this answer and was asked on its own the same day (the second DD-M9-11 entry below: it switches too, with one visible notice). Nothing is deleted and the floor's suites stay in CI. Built in M9b.1. Rejected: the floor as the default with a switch to turn it off; asking on first run. DD-M9-4's "nothing deleted" stands, and cutting the floor is still undecided.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-12 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **Web reach is a per-hire grant, and fetched content arms the gate** · A hire template declares `web`, off by default; queries go only to the search backend the Architect configured; a hire without the grant has no web tool at all. Once fetched content is in a hire's context, an action that writes to the world needs a human. Whether other content from outside the company arms the gate too — a mail body, an MCP result, an attachment, another person's message — whether a backend with no egress and no secret grant is exempt, and that a target repository's content does not arm it, are this plan's proposal and are asked at M9d.0; this answer did not decide them. NFR-10's egress sentence and THREAT-MODEL §4/§5 are amended in M9e.0; the mechanism is M9d.3 and lands before the first web tool. Rejected: researcher roles only (safest, and slow for ordinary conversation); web for every native agent (an injected instruction would reach agents that hold repository credentials).
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-13 — ARCHITECT DECISION, against the recommendation) **MCP servers are admitted through one company-wide registry** · The Architect registers a server once and every native hire may use it; a hire template does not name its servers. The recommendation was per-hire grants by name, and the consequence the Architect accepted was stated in the option itself: a compromised server reaches the whole company (R32). What the plan keeps around that decision, none of it a per-hire grant: the harness is the only MCP client, so a server holds its own credentials and no agent does; a tool manifest snapshot is taken at registration and drift refuses the server; registration is refused to a script; side-effect tools pass the Watch's gate policy; results arm the gate. **One standing requirement narrows the decision as written and is applied as written:** NFR-17 gives a researcher no credentialed capability, so a Stoa researcher sees only servers registered without secrets and read-only. Whether NFR-17 is amended for the registry is asked at M9d.0, not assumed. Rejected: grants by name per hire; built-in connectors only, with MCP left to a later decision.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-14 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **An agent's loop stays on the Architect's machine; its tool execution may run in a declared backend** · SRS §1.2's "SHALL NOT run agents on remote machines over SSH" is amended to say exactly that, dated, with the original sentence kept in the amendment. Backends are planned as M9d.1–M9d.2 behind one conformance table: `local`, `docker`, `apptainer`, `ssh`, `modal`, each declaring an isolation grade that is checked in both directions (ADR-0031's method). Owned spawn, both data planes, the hook endpoint and the single committer do not move. The same edit corrected a second SHALL NOT line that had been stale since ADR-0036 ("replace the underlying agent CLIs" now reads "for wrapped hires"). Rejected: local containers only, with the SHALL NOT standing as written; whole agents on another machine (multi-machine crews, which stay on the post-v1 horizon).
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-15 — ARCHITECT DECISION, in two answers) **The phone is served Hermes Agent's way first, and then by a companion of Ephesus's own, designed before it is built** · Asked first as a choice between a paired LAN listener, a private overlay only, and no listener, the Architect answered in his own words: *"no shortcuts we will design a proper mobile system just like Hermes did for Ephesus."* The two sources were then read. Hermes Agent (at `7533bd2`) has no phone client of its own: it reaches a phone through its messaging gateway — pairing codes that expire, are rate-limited and are approved on the host, approvals answered from chat — and through a web dashboard on loopback behind a tunnel the user runs. Odysseus (at `2992bf6`) pairs a phone with a non-expiring bearer token shown as a QR code over plain HTTP, and that device cannot authorise anything. Asked again with that on the table — Hermes Agent's way in full; that plus Ephesus's own companion; or a first-class subsystem with its design left open — he chose **the second**. So: M9f builds the gateway to that pairing standard and adds **M9f.6, the workspace in a browser** — a third front door onto the same handlers, bound to loopback only, never without a session, a remote surface and not a second window; and a seventh milestone, **M9h**, builds the companion — a device key that never leaves the phone, pairing only at the machine by a code that carries no long-lived secret, a channel protected end to end at the application layer, a device list and revocation — opening with a design package whose protocol is reviewed adversarially as a document before any code. Two things are owed to ADRs and are not decided here: M9f.6 opens a listener ADR-0033 declined for the control surface, so its own ADR must say why this surface differs and the differences must hold in code; and the companion's form, its reach away from home, the device unlock on an approval and the push channel are asked at M9h.0. One departure from the source is deliberate and recorded: an unpaired sender receives silence rather than a pairing code, because the system has one operator (SRS §1.2).
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-16 — ARCHITECT DECISION) **Speech is output only; the rest of M6.9 stays deferred** · Hermes Agent's list includes text-to-speech, and the Herald has had no caller since M6.9 was deferred on 2026-08-30 as "not an important function for now". M9e.3 gives the Herald's output half a caller — a `speak` tool and spoken briefs through the existing seam, with a local voice so it works without a key — and nothing else: push-to-talk, the wake word, barge-in and voice approvals remain M6.9's, and SRS §6.2 and §6.5 remain owed exactly as written. A reachability walk in that package lists what gained a caller, so the deferred half cannot return by a side door. Rejected: keeping voice deferred entirely; lifting M6.9 in full.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-17 — ARCHITECT DECISION, an FR-13.1 Architect-only act) **Both watchlist rows' tags are amended for Phase 9** · `src-hermes-agent` gains `gateway`, `scheduling`, `delegation`, `sandboxing`, `skills`, `web`; `src-odysseus` gains `chat-ui`, `model-serving`, `comparison`, `research`, `documents`, `mcp`, `mail-calendar`, `mobile`. A study is scoped by its row's tags, so without this the contract packages of M9b to M9h could not run a governed `/research` cycle on their own subjects. Pins are still set at first study. Hermes Agent's `LICENSE` is recorded as **read as MIT** at `7533bd2`, and the row stays `unverified`: verifying a license is the Architect's act, an unverified row permits study and refuses pattern intake (FR-13.5), and that verification is owed before the first brief seeds a design from this source. Odysseus was read again as AGPL-3.0-or-later and stays pattern-learning only. Rejected: amending tags milestone by milestone; leaving the rows as they were.
- 2026-10-03 · (PHASE 9 PLAN, DD-M9-18 — ARCHITECT DECISION, amending DD-M9-10 the same day) **M7b follows M9f; M9g and M9h follow v1** · DD-M9-10 put all the feature milestones before M7b with "about six months" quoted as the cost. Summed package by package, and with the milestone DD-M9-15 added, the figure is about 48 weeks of packages — 51 with exit reviews, 44 with M9c beside the main line. Asked again on that figure — M7b after M9f; keep all seven before M7b; or M7b right after M9 — the Architect chose **M7b after M9f**. v1 is therefore M9 through M9f: the workspace, local models, contained execution and armed gates, the open web and research, and the gateway. M9f is the boundary because it carries the two things v1 already requires of a remote surface: the chat bridge (FR-10.2, absorbed from M7b.4) and the morning brief on the phone, M7b's own moved exit clause. To v1 that is about 32 nominal weeks on the main line, about 39 if M9c is taken in series; M9g (mail, calendar, todos) and M9h (the companion) are planned post-v1 milestones of about 12 weeks, registered below M7b in PROGRESS so that a session resuming at the first unchecked box meets them in build order. Rejected: keeping all seven before M7b; M7b immediately after M9, with the chat bridge returned to it. What DD-M9-10 decided about M7b.4 and the moved exit clause stands.

- 2026-10-11 · (STOA SEED HARDENING — ARCHITECT DECISIONS, the "separate task" of the entry above) **The seed validates every row it parses and reports each one it refuses; pattern intake needs a license on an MIT-compatible list; curation refuses to write over a watchlist it cannot read** · Reproduced on 2026-10-11 by running the shipped code (win32, Node v20.16.0) over `docs/stoa/WATCHLIST.md` as it stood at `75ec37c` and at #72's `715db6b`: `parseWatchlistMarkdown` handed `seed()` a 166-character (and, at `715db6b`, a 176-character) License cell beginning `unverified (`; `seed()` wrote and returned it without validating, so the first read listed four sources and `checkIntake` ALLOWED intake from both rows, the AGPL one included, because it refused only the exact string `unverified`; every later read found the file over the schema's 120-character cap and read the Stoa as empty. A third defect sat behind them: with the file invalid, `register()` and `retire()` both took `watchlist()`'s empty answer as the truth. `register()` wrote over the file, so one registration left a file holding only the new source and the four seeded ones were gone, though `watchlist()`'s comment says the file is never overwritten; `retire()` found nothing to retire and answered "no source … on the watchlist", which was false. #72 fixed the table's cells before merge; this change makes the code safe against the next one. Asked, the Architect chose: (1) **intake fails closed on an allowlist of MIT-compatible SPDX identifiers** — `MIT`, `MIT-0`, `Apache-2.0`, `BSD-2-Clause`, `BSD-3-Clause`, `ISC`, `0BSD`, `Unlicense`, `CC0-1.0`, `Zlib`, matched after trimming and ignoring case — rather than any recognised SPDX identifier (which DD-M9-6's "refused whatever verification finds" for an AGPL row would contradict) or a rule that refuses only values beginning `unverified` (which would still allow `TBD`, `pending` or `AGPL-3.0?`); every other value refuses intake and the refusal names the list. A license that is not `unverified` and is not on the list gets its own researcher note, `prompts/stoa/intake-incompatible.md`, so the note for `unverified` ("nobody has checked") is never rendered for a license somebody has read. The entry schema is unchanged (`license` stays a string of 1 to 120 characters), so every existing `watchlist.json` still reads: an enum rename is a schema migration, and so is narrowing a free-text field. (2) **Code only**: `docs/stoa/WATCHLIST.md` is not edited here (#72 already holds both cells at `unverified`), and no CI test reads the shipped table — offered with a Gymnasium row (GYM-012) to approve or hold, and declined; a bad cell is caught at the first boot that seeds it, as a visible degradation. (3) **The overwrite is fixed here**, not in a separate task: while `watchlist.json` is invalid, `register` and `retire` refuse, naming the file, its reasons and the recovery, and leave its bytes as they were (for `retire`, this replaces the false "no source" answer; it never wrote). The Architect then approved the plan, including one step past the brief: a row with a source id and no url or no tags, which the reader skipped without a word, is now reported like a row the schema refuses (invariant §7). Mechanical choices, made inside those answers: the build-phase reader validates each row against `watchlistEntrySchema` and returns the rows it refused beside the entries, so its `WatchlistEntry` type is no longer a claim the schema has not checked; `seed()` checks the whole file with `watchlistSchema`, writes only what passed and returns that same value, so the first read and every later read agree; each refused row is one degradation naming its id, its reasons and that the seed runs once (fix the row, move `watchlist.json` aside, restart), and the `seeded` log event carries them as `rejected`. Not changed: a malformed watchlist still reads as empty and is never rewritten (M5b.1), and two rows sharing an id are not refused — recorded as a follow-up. See `docs/implementations/2026-10-11-stoa-seed-fail-closed.md`.
